## Summary

Horror Entities is the simulation of the one real, hostile thing in the ship: a single segment-rig creature (one type, one instance, one lurk / stalk / reach / strike / retreat cycle) that turns Attention's abstract "the web has noticed you" into a body at a true position, gated by Attention's told-breach permission, that unfolds a limb onto a locked point and snaps, leaves honest marks and Graph footprints wherever it has been, and can be outwalked, hidden from or locked out but never fought or farmed. Read it if you touch any system that deals harm, writes a footprint, dips a lamp, places a sound on a body or draws a mark, because this document owns the tell composition (additive to Attention's 10-30 s window), the walk-out arithmetic that makes death fair, the truth-channel footprints a hallucination cannot hide, and the acceptance criteria that discharge the Horror half of Attention's, the Graph's, the Emitter's, Lighting's and Suit's must-repeats.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer (designed)`, `Life-Support Graph (designed)`, `Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query (designed in this run)`, `Attention System (designed in this run)`, `Suit & Equipment (designed in this run)`, `Ship Body & Interior Physics (designed in this run)`, `Ship Section & Layout Data Model (designed in this run; hull-segment lookup provisional)`, `Save/Load & Versioned Format (designed in this run)`; downstream: `Adaptive Audio & Tells (designed)`, `Perception Filter (designed)`, `Sanity & Hallucination Rules (designed in this run)`

## Overview

Horror Entities is the simulation of the one kind of thing in the ship that is real, hostile and not a machine: a scene-tree-free, integer-only `SIM` block, `horror.state`, advanced by one divisor-5 step hook, `horror.step`, that holds the horror's true position, its behaviour state and the trail it leaves, and that turns Attention's abstract promise ("the web has noticed you") into a body in a room that the player can read, avoid and, if they do not, die to. In one sentence: **it is the web's physical consequence, a single segment-rig creature that lurks in the dark in CALM, stalks toward the light in a TELL, reaches at a point it has locked in a BREACH and then snaps, and that leaves honest marks wherever it has been.**

**The data layer.** The block holds one entity record at MVP (`HORROR_MAX_MVP` 1, type `horror_t01`, a placeholder renamed never aliased): body, section, integer base-px position with sub-pixel residue, behaviour state (`LURK`, `STALK`, `REACH`, `STRIKE`, `RETREAT`, plus `TRANSIT` while it passes through a hull), the locked strike point and the tick its telegraph ends, per-epoch strike, gouge and rupture counts, and a persisted counter for its one random stream, `"horror.tell"`. Beside the record sits a bounded, persisted **mark ring** (`MAX_MARKS`, wet iridescent smear, branching rime, radial hairlines, and the STRUCK burst that records a hit) and the published state of its **light disturbance** (L3) on nearby fixtures. Everything is derived from three reads and nothing else: Attention's committed `attention.view` (phase, locus, the permission predicate), the Emitter's `lux(ref)` for the seats it might prefer, and Layout's room graph for its movement. It writes the world in exactly three ways and no others: Class B footprint deposits through the Graph's `deposit_trace` (M17: CHILL, GOUGE and RUPTURE; never STRESS, which is Attention's alone), Class B `apply_suit_damage` on a body inside a telegraphed strike disc, and its own published state (marks, disturbances, `horror.view`) that Lighting and the presenters read. It is a registered Emitter subject (it can be lit and therefore seen) and it emits nothing, occludes nothing and casts no shadow. Its position is its own, moved by Body's pure `LocomotionStep` against Layout's solids; it is never a Body fact, a `CharacterBody2D` or a physics query. It holds no hit points, no aggro, threat, alert or awareness value of any kind, and no meter: the only thing it remembers is where it has been (marks) and what it has already done this epoch (counts).

**The player-facing effect.** The player does not meet a monster; they meet weather with intent. In CALM they may glimpse something long and low at the edge of their lamp, never nearer than a few tiles, and later find a wet streak or a forked white rime where it passed: the ship is not empty. A sighting in CALM is information and never a danger: the thing cannot hurt you in CALM (HE-11), but it tells you which body it lives in and that its trail and rime are fresh, and a player who reads that changes the route they take, whether the lamp is on and which job they do first. In a TELL, while Attention's creaking and filaments rise everywhere, a hairline appears on a hull face, a fixture nearby dips on its own irregular rhythm, and the thing comes in through the wall toward whoever is carrying light. When the TELL becomes a BREACH it closes at a pace the player can outwalk, plants its body, and unfolds one long jointed limb across the floor until the tip rests, motionless, on the spot where it has decided to strike. The player has one to three seconds to see the limb and step off that spot. If they do, nothing happens to them and the ship pays in scratched hull; if they do not, the suit takes a hit and the floor keeps a hairline burst where it landed (a miss leaves only the smear); the second hit opens a leak and staggers the body to a walk, and three such hits take the body down. The horror then retreats, and the cycle repeats until the breach subsides. Nothing the player carries can kill it, nothing it does can be farmed, and it leaves nothing behind but its trail.

**The contract this GDD owns.** Horror is the only gameplay system whose lethal acts are neither player-originated nor environmental, so it is where the Graph's exported requirement ("every non-player exogenous source must be preceded by that system's own Pillar 2 tell of 10-30 s", LSG:144) meets a body. Attention satisfies the 10-30 s at the breach level (AT-15); Horror **repeats** the permission predicate `exogenous_permitted(section)` at its three check points (REACH begin, STRIKE entry, every M17 RUPTURE or GOUGE call), adds two layers that are *additive and never a substitute* (the visible stalk and the REACH telegraph at a locked point), and publishes the facts a `TellAudit` replay needs. It discharges the Horror half of LSG:1063 and the crew-body scope of AT-15 R15. It is also the second GDD to land on the Emitter's "the beam reveals, never repels" rail (EM:89) and adopts it verbatim.

**What the game loses without it.** Without Horror, a breach on a clean ship is "nearly a non-event" and *commit* dominates *abort* (AT-11): restraint becomes decoration, Pillar 3 is hollow and the "chaos" half of Pillar 2 is empty. The Graph's exogenous footprint kinds have no caller, so a hidden real entity has no trail and Pillar 4's class (a) has no substance. Death-is-a-bitch (the user's decision) would arrive only through plumbing (decompression, cold), never through something that moves. Adaptive Audio would have no body to place a creak on, and Lighting's L3 disturbance would remain a stub. The core hypothesis (the boarding-and-breach loop is tense and absorbing for 30 minutes solo) needs a consequence that is felt at body scale.

**Index wording, reconciled.** The index bullet reads: "Segment-rig horrors with creep/unfold/snap behaviours, real occlusion and shadow; writes hidden-entity footprints (branching rime, radial hairlines, light disturbance) into the Graph footprint channel and submits light-disturbance modifiers to Lighting. MVP: one behaviour." This GDD honours every clause but one, and says so. *Segment-rig, creep / unfold / snap, one behaviour*: HE-1, HE-14, HE-15 (one cycle of one type; creep is LURK/STALK/RETREAT, unfold is REACH, snap is STRIKE). *Footprints into the Graph footprint channel*: the **physical half** (CHILL, hull-only GOUGE, RUPTURE) goes through M17; the **web-grammar half** (smear, branching rime, radial hairlines) is Horror's own persisted mark ring, because Failure & Trail draws only Graph descriptors (FT-31) and the Graph has no mark record (HE-27). *Light-disturbance modifiers to Lighting*: published state that Lighting reads, never a call (KRN:117; HE-35). *"Real occlusion and shadow"* is **read as visual opacity by draw order plus locomotion against Layout solids, and is not a `LightOccluder2D`, a shadow or an emission**: three designed GDDs (EM-20 (c), LO rail 10, FT-31) and the art bible's own recommendation (ART:296) rule the opposite, and a horror shadow would destroy the free stage-2 falsifier "a shadow with no occluder" (HE-2; orchestrator ruling R1). The index bullet, the routed thread, the art bible's "Physical honesty" bullet (ART:542) and PIPE:140 are listed as owed wording refreshes in Cross-References and are not edited here. **Until those rows land the art bible and this GDD contradict each other on the horror shadow, so the refresh rows (ART:542, ART 5.5, PIPE:140, IDX:35) are a BLOCKING precondition of `/asset-spec system:horror-entities` for the rig, exactly as C-HE-21 is for rime and hairlines (AC-HE-123); they are not advisory.** The creative-director affirmed the ruling at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate (R1).

**Status of neighbours at authoring time (2026-10-26/27).** Designed and locked: Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, Player Controller & Input; designed in this run: Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, Attention System, 2D Lighting & Occlusion, Failure & Trail System, Item & Module Database, Suit & Equipment, Interaction, Ship Body & Interior Physics, Ship Section & Layout Data Model, Save/Load & Versioned Format, Derelict Authoring. Modular Ship Assembly and Sanity & Hallucination Rules are also designed in this run (`Status: Designed`); Web Map & Transit exists as a draft (`Status: In Design`) and is treated as in flight; Boarding & Salvage also exists (`Status: Designed` on disk at the 2026-10-27 review). Perception Filter, Adaptive Audio & Tells, HUD & Suit Layer, In-World Instruments, Chart Table and Debug Overlay have no GDD; every assumption about them is marked `(provisional — <dep> undesigned)`. Engine reference: `docs/engine-reference/godot/` has no `scripting.md` or `core.md` and no 2D-skeleton coverage in `animation.md`; GDScript operation cost and per-instance shader parameters are spike parameters, not sourced facts.

## Player Fantasy

**The fantasy in one sentence.** *"The light we need to keep the ship alive is the light that brings it, and we survive by reading the dark it walks through, never by fighting it."* This is not a scary-monster fantasy. It is a **prey-with-knowledge** fantasy that grows out of two locked facts that collide on purpose: every channelled repair verb is lit-only (Interaction `NOT_LIT`, IA-6) and the chest lamp is hands-free (user decision), so to fix the scrubber you must be lit; and the horror's one preference among living seats is the highest `lux_pm`, recomputed from physical light on every run with no stored value (HE-13). The core Horror decision of every BREACH is therefore the game's core fantasy compressed: *keep the ship breathing (light on, channel the repair) or keep the body breathing (lamp off, close a door, step out of the reach, pod-hop).* The Core Fantasy line "the ship is alive because we keep it alive, minute by minute" becomes literal: **the minute you spend keeping it alive is a minute you are visible.**

**Framing (orchestrator ruling: creative-director framing A, with B as tonal layer).** Direct framing, **"Read the Dark"**: *it comes to the light; I read its trail and choose when to be seen.* Every rule in this document can be checked against that sentence, it puts the player's competence in reading rather than in reflexes, and it turns the lit-only repair rule into the central dilemma instead of a friction. Framing **B, "The Web's Hand"** (the horror is the web reaching into the ship through this limb because the crew has earned its attention) is adopted as the fiction behind A only: it justifies why the thing is "of the web" and why it casts no shadow (see the fiction gloss below). Framing **C, "The Hunter"** (something is tracking us) is rejected: a hunter remembers, which requires a stored pursuit or last-seen value, which is a hidden aggro number and fails Pillar 1's own design test. *(Framing A with B as fiction, and the rejection of C, were ratified without change at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate; later edits must not drift from "the minute you spend keeping it alive is a minute you are visible".)*

**The anchor moment: a limb entering the cone before the body.** The player is crouched at a derelict's failing scrubber, channel bar half full, chest lamp lit because the verb demands it. At the edge of the cone, something thin and segmented arrives first: one long jointed limb, iridescent only where the beam touches it, laid along the floor toward them. The body is still in the dark. The player has a choice that is legible, not a reflex test: finish the channel (the bar shows the ticks left) or abandon it and walk out of the reach. If they walk, nothing touches them; the scrubber is still broken. The feeling is *I lived, and the ship paid.*

| Tick (from TELL entry) | Attention (owned there, read-only here) | Horror (this GDD) | What the player can perceive |
|---|---|---|---|
| T = 0 | TELL begins; `tell_epoch` +1; locus recorded (AT-14) | The entity leaves LURK; if the locus body is not its body it leaves through the hull (egress mark) and enters `TRANSIT` | Attention's global tell (beat on every lit region, filaments, dust turning): "it noticed" |
| T >= `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` − `INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` (mark), `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` (body) (240 / 300 [PH]) | TELL continues | Ingress through a hull segment one room hop from the brightest living seat's room, never into a seat's room, never inside `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` (HE-20) | The first local sign: a radial hairline on a hull face, rime branching from it, a fixture nearby dipping on its own rhythm |
| `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` to tell end | The tell length was drawn in [`TELL_DRAW_LO`, `TELL_DRAW_HI`], never below `TELL_MIN_TICKS` | STALK at `V_CREEP_MPX`; never within `LURK_STANDOFF_PX`; no RUPTURE, no GOUGE, no harm (HE-12) | A visible stalk of at least `TELL_MIN_TICKS − INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` = 300 ticks at the placeholders, typically 600 to 1500 |
| BREACH entry | `exogenous_permitted(section)` true in `locus_body` | Approach at `V_HUNT_MPX` toward the highest-`lux_pm` living seat | "Controlled panic, more things moving, not the screen fighting the player" (ART 2.8) |
| REACH start | n/a | The strike point is LOCKED (non-tracking); `UNFOLD_TICKS` drawn in [`UNFOLD_LO`, `UNFOLD_HI`] = [95, 180] ticks [PH] (HE-14, HE-F5) | **The limb enters the cone.** About 1.6 to 3 seconds of unfold, then a held stillness |
| REACH end | The predicate is re-checked | STRIKE resolves only if `exogenous_permitted` still holds; every living seat in the disc takes `STRIKE_BP` (HE-15) | A snap, the touch-down smear and Audio's positional strike cue. Strike one is witnessed by the strike itself, by Suit's integrity reading (the STRAINED rung is inert and carries no witness, by Suit's design) and, if a seat was hit, by the STRUCK hairline burst laid at P that a miss never leaves; the first physical consequence is strike two (leak, seam jet, CRITICAL, stagger, walk-only) |

**Worked moment (single player, derelict B, placeholders).** The player is at 40 % of a scrubber channel with the lamp on; `lux_pm` reads 1000 because the owner stands in their own light (EM-21). A TELL begins at T = 0 with a drawn length of 1200. Ingress is at T = 300, one room away; the hairline and rime are visible through the doorway; the body crosses the door frame near T = 700. BREACH begins at T = 1200 and the horror closes at `V_HUNT_MPX` 1500 (1.5 px per tick). It closes from the standoff (192 px) to `REACH_RANGE_PX` (96 px) in 64 ticks, so its REACH starts on the run grid at T = 1265; `UNFOLD_TICKS` draws 135. The player sees the limb at T = 1265, abandons the channel at T = 1295 (30 ticks of reaction) and walks at `V_WALK` 2: 72 px in 36 ticks, so (with the kernel's one-tick command application, KRN D.4) they stand exactly outside the disc at T = 1332, 68 ticks before the snap at T = 1400. No harm. One wet smear sits where the tip rested; if the tip was within `HULL_CONTACT_PX` of a hull face, radial hairlines and a hull GOUGE mark the wall. The scrubber is still broken.

**Explicitly not a tell length.** The kernel's illustrative `rng_int(600, 1800)` on the stream name `"horror.tell"` (KRN D.10) is an example, not Horror's tell and not Horror's REACH range. The Pillar 2 requirement of 10 to 30 seconds (LSG:144) is met by Attention's window (AT-15); Horror's stalk and REACH are **additive layers and never a substitute**, and nothing in this GDD shortens Attention's tell (HE-9). The stream `"attention.tell"` is Attention's.

**Pillar text served (verbatim) and the design tests as they bite Horror.**

| Pillar | Text (game-concept) | How this GDD serves it |
|---|---|---|
| **P2 Dread Before Chaos** (primary) | "Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived (sound, light, gauge drift, hull stress)." Design test: *breach fires at a hidden threshold vs. breach preceded by a 10-30 second escalating tell -> the tell.* | Every lethal act lands inside Attention's told breach (AT-15) and is further preceded by the visible stalk and by the REACH telegraph at a locked point. Three told layers, never one. |
| **P1 The World Never Lies** (primary) | "Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth." Design test: *a hidden stat vs. a visibly leaking pipe -> the pipe.* | The horror is a SIM entity at a true position that leaves true marks, true Graph footprints and true light disturbance. No aggro or threat number exists, stored or derived. |
| **P3 The Web Watches** (serves) | "Attention is shown in the world (creaking, flicker, filament movement), never as a hidden meter. Restraint and stealth are real strategies." | The horror is Attention's consequence, never a second meter (AT-2). Its CALM, TELL and BREACH behaviour is gated by `attention.view.phase` only; going dark is real counterplay. |
| **P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality** (serves) | "A hidden real entity still leaves its P1/P2 footprint (sound, frost, hull stress, light disturbance) — the hallucination hides the body, never the trail." | Horror owns the web-grammar half of that guarantee: marks, disturbance state and Graph footprints are bit-identical whether or not any viewer's body-hide is active (HE-32). |
| **P5 Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total** (light) | "Losing the home-ship or a crewmate costs ship and body, never knowledge." | Death by horror restores from the newest autosave (SL-7); what the player learned about reach, rime and light survives in their head and, at Vertical Slice, in the Codex through stable `type_id` and `tell_id` enumerations. |

*Design test (this system):* **a horror that reacts to a number nobody can see vs. a horror that reacts to light the player chose to show -> the light.** *Secondary test (Pillar 2 at body scale):* **a strike that lands from the dark vs. a limb that enters the cone before the body at a point that no longer moves -> the limb.** The tests decide four recurring debates: keep a "last target" so the horror follows the player who switched the lamp off (no: turning the lamp off removes the seat from preference on the next run; memory would be a hidden number); let the strike track a moving player for "fairness of difficulty" (no: the point locks at REACH start); let it charge in CALM (no: CALM is non-lethal by construction and it never comes inside `LURK_STANDOFF_PX`); make the beam repel it, or make it shrug the beam off to stay scary (neither: the beam only reveals, EM:89 adopted verbatim).

**How the fantasy deepens under hallucination.** The horror's truth is its trail. Because a real entity always writes marks, Graph footprints and disturbance state and a phantom never does, low sanity turns "is it there?" into "is the floor marked?". The player stops reading the body and starts reading the ship, which is exactly the Pillar 1 and 4 tension the concept calls "the game". Horror's obligations in each class are small and absolute.

| Class | What the player is shown | The channel of truth that remains | Horror's obligation |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) phantom entity (invented) | The Filter draws a horror rig from a phantom pose stream; it may lunge | No told breach precedes it (TellAudit records nothing, AT E-53); no rime, smear or hairline; no disturbance; no Graph footprint; the suit rung does not move | Keep `horror.view` real-only; the rig presenter accepts any pose stream (HE-40) |
| (a) hidden real entity (Vertical Slice) | The Filter undraws the body for one seat | Marks, disturbance, M17 writes and suit damage are bit-identical with or without the hide; a saner crewmate may still see the body | Keep every real entity registered with a true anchor; read no perception state (H5, CR-12) |
| (b) false system state | Gauges and sprites lie | Horror touches nothing in (b); its RUPTURE and GOUGE surface as true Graph descriptors that Failure & Trail draws (FT-31) | None |
| (c) crewmate as horror (Vertical Slice) | The Filter substitutes the horror rig for a crewmate's body, driven by their true velocity | The true beam: "a horror with a player's beam is your friend" (ART:292); a real horror emits nothing, so the beam stays a perfect discriminator | Real horrors never carry or emit light (HE-2) |
| (d) phantom salvage | Not Horror's | n/a | None |

Three falsifiers cost the player something, as Pillar 4 demands (checking costs time, risk or trust): **look at the floor** (time: in the dark you must light it, which draws the real one), **check the phase** (risk: is Attention's tell running?), **ask the crewmate** (trust; co-op). The no-shadow rule adds a fourth, free falsifier at stage 2: *a shadow with no occluder is never the web: it is you.*

**Fiction gloss for the no-shadow rule (creative-director; adopted at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate; to be written into ART:542 as part of the owed refresh, which gates the rig's asset-spec).** *"The web does not stop light; it only takes it."* Web-matter is real: it stands on the deck, scars the hull, leaves rime where it passed and moves dust. But light goes through it as through a filament: the beam shows its surface as a thin iridescent band and continues to whatever is behind it. Nothing that belongs to the web casts a shadow, so a shadow with no occluder is never the web.

**Reference techniques, adopt and reject.**

| Reference | Technique | Verdict | Where it lands |
|---|---|---|---|
| *Alien: Isolation* | One persistent, physically present entity, never despawned | ADOPT | HE-1, HE-20 |
| *Alien: Isolation* | A director's "menace gauge" steering the alien | REJECT: guard rail 1 (no threat value); Attention is the only director (AT-2) | HE-7 |
| *Alien: Isolation* | Grab-instakill on contact | REJECT: three told strikes | HE-15 |
| *Amnesia: The Dark Descent* | "You cannot fight it, only hide" | ADOPT in spirit | HE-38 |
| *Amnesia* | Sanity drains from looking at the monster | REJECT here: Sanity owns exposure; Horror computes none | HE-40 |
| *Amnesia* | The monster vanishes when out of sight | REJECT: a SIM entity at a true position; dormancy freezes, never despawns | HE-21 |
| *Darkwood* | Light attracts; the world carries marks of what passed | ADOPT | HE-13, HE-27 |
| *Darkwood* | Melee combat, crafted weapons | REJECT: anti-pillar; HORROR takes no verb | HE-38 |
| *Barotrauma* | Creatures that hole the hull; crew patch it | ADOPT | HE-16 |
| *Barotrauma* | Turrets, weapons, creature loot | REJECT | HE-38 |
| *Project Zomboid* | Death is final and common ("it happens") | ADOPT (user decision) | HE-15 |
| *Project Zomboid* | A killable horde as content | REJECT: "horrors are avoided and survived, not farmed" | HE-38 |
| *Pandorum* (film) | A hands-free chest lamp in a dark derelict; hunters in the margins of the light | ADOPT (user decision) | HE-13 |
| *Pandorum* | Fighting the hunters hand to hand | REJECT: no attack input (PC-12) | HE-38 |
| *Beyond the Aquila Rift* | The kindness of the lie: the wrongness is in what you are shown | ADOPT, routed to Sanity and the Filter | HE-32 |
| *Annihilation* (film) | The shimmer: iridescence as the signature of something not of our world | ADOPT: a band only under light, never a fill | Visual/Audio Requirements |
| *The Thing* | The body as wrongness, imitation of crew | REJECT for Horror: class (c) is a render substitution owned by the Filter | HE-40 |
| Souls-like games | A long, readable wind-up at a fixed point | ADOPT | HE-14 |
| Action games | Hit-stop, screen shake, damage numbers | REJECT: the camera refuses horror callers (PIPE C1, C9) | HE-38 |

**Tone and vocabulary.** Patient, physical, quiet; closer to weather with intent than to a monster with a personality. It never roars at the camera, never poses, never "spots" anyone; it arrives in increments the player could have seen. Used in this document and in any player-facing text: reach, unfold, snap, strike, lurk, stalk, retreat, ingress, egress, "it noticed" (Attention's mood), "it comes to the light", smear, branching rime, radial hairline, "the trail", "the floor remembers", dip (for a lamp). Never used, outside a quotation or a negation: `threat`, `aggro`, `alert`, `awareness`, `suspicion`, `hate`, `detect` as horror state; `enemy`, `mob`, `boss`, `spawn` (use ingress), `kill`, `damage` (use `strike_bp`); `flicker` of a fixture (use dip); `meter`, `bar`, `gauge`, `indicator` near a horror or proximity; `real occlusion`, `cast shadow`, `occlude the beam`. These double as the stale-token lint (Acceptance Criteria).

**Emotional arc across a breach cycle (mapped to Attention's phases).**

| Phase | Intended emotion | Horror's contribution | Flow note |
|---|---|---|---|
| CALM | Unease, then orientation: "it lives in this body, and its trail is fresh" (information, never danger: CALM is non-lethal by construction, HE-11) | LURK: may be seen at a distance and cannot harm; marks age; disturbance dips near it | Flow of work with a background tax of glances at the floor, paid in choices (route, lamp, which job first), not in fear of a hit |
| TELL | Dread, rising | STALK visible; ingress hairline and rime; disturbance near it | An intentional flow break: the player reprioritises |
| BREACH | Controlled panic | Approach, REACH telegraph, STRIKE at a locked point | Choice under time, never a reflex test (the walk-out bound) |
| SUBSIDING | Relief with residue | RETREAT; no new REACH starts | Recovery |
| After | Reading the aftermath | Marks: where it came from, where it touched the hull | Discovery: "it came in through there" |

**Anti-pillar guard rails (restated as lints in Acceptance Criteria).**
1. **Not a combat game.** No horror HP, no kill state, no loot, no XP, no kill counter, no hit feedback for tool use, no player damage output; HORROR subjects take no verb at MVP (`INVALID_TARGET`, IA-39); the beam only reveals.
2. **No reward path.** Nothing drops, nothing unlocks, no codex entry is earned by approaching a horror at MVP. Surviving costs the ship (footprints), never pays.
3. **Not intentional PvP.** A horror's lethal act on a crew body is inside the told breach and audited by TellAudit (AT-15 R15); friendly harm stays Interaction plus Sanity; Horror never routes harm between players.
4. **Not a freeform builder, no authored story.** Horror places nothing and narrates nothing; Derelict Authoring "places no entity" (DA:101); ingress is derived from Attention's locus and Emitter reads, never a trigger volume ("monster closets: REJECT").
5. **No win state.** The horror cannot be removed from the world; it only retreats.
6. **Death is lethal (user), and is not Horror's.** Horror owns the act and its tells; DOWNED and DEAD are Suit's (SE-24); restore from the newest autosave is Save/Load's (SL-7). No death screen, no beat.

**Validation targets (creative-director, `[PLACEHOLDER]`, measured in MVP playtests of the concept's 30-minute solo session).** (1) *Fair death:* at least 80 % of horror-caused deaths are described unprompted, or on one neutral question, with a cause the player saw ("I saw the arm", "I stayed in the light too long"); below 60 % the telegraph or stalk is failing. (2) *Not an alarm monster:* in at least 70 % of sessions the player sees the horror or its fresh marks during CALM at least once. (3) *The dilemma exists:* at least half of playtesters, asked "what did you do when it came?", describe turning the lamp off, abandoning a repair or closing a door; if the dominant answer is "I ran", target preference by light is not legible. (4) *No hidden-number belief:* zero playtesters describe the horror "remembering" or "locking on" across a lamp-off. (5) *Marks read as web, not ship:* a blind classification of rime against coolant frost is correct at least 90 % at k = 1, `k_auto` and `k_auto + 2`. (6) *Teeth:* in a replay of a breach on a clean ship, the player loses at least one repair's worth of progress or one suit rung in at least 60 % of breaches they choose to commit through lit. (7) *A CALM sighting changes behaviour:* at least 60 % of testers who saw the horror or a fresh mark in CALM report, unprompted or on one neutral question ("did seeing it change what you did next?"), that it changed what they did (the route, the lamp, or which job they took first). If this fails, the first lever is `RETURN_HOME` 0 (OQ-1); HE-11 is not relaxed, because CALM stays non-lethal to protect Pillar 2.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

Rule ids are `HE-n`; formulas `HE-F n` (Formulas section, ids reserved in the index at the end of this section); edge cases `E-HE-n`; acceptance criteria `AC-HE-n`; conflicts `C-HE-n`. Every Horror-owned number is `[PLACEHOLDER]` and a registry candidate unless marked CITED (a locked name quoted verbatim with its registry value) or DERIVED. The Graph's `RUPTURE_HP`, `BREACH_HP`, `FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q`, `FOOTPRINT_MAX_GOUGE_BP`, `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS` and `C_TILE` are Life-Support Graph registry constants (registered 2026-10-27), cited with the registry value and their LSG line and never restated as Horror's own. Rules state observable behaviour and contracts; implementation patterns are marked "-> ADR candidate". "Run" means one execution of the `horror.step` hook (every 5th tick).

#### Group A — Identity, scope and ownership

**HE-1. One type, one instance, one behaviour cycle.** *Rule.* MVP ships exactly one type row, `horror_t01` (a placeholder name, renamed never aliased: floor-attached, three-branch, about 1.5 times crewmate height, one extensible reach limb; the art bible's outer-ring working target, ART:546), at most `HORROR_MAX_MVP` 1 resident record, and one behaviour cycle: **LURK (CALM) / STALK (TELL) → REACH (telegraph) → STRIKE → RETREAT**, plus `TRANSIT` while it passes through a hull. The motion vocabulary of the art matrix maps onto it as *creep* (LURK, STALK, RETREAT), *unfold* (REACH) and *snap* (STRIKE). "One behaviour" is **one cycle of one type, never three monsters.** More types, per-ring rosters, filament-riders, hidden-entity wiring, `repel`, flares and decoys, and co-op replication are Vertical Slice and are named in Open Questions and in the block shape only. *Why.* The concept's MVP item 4 is "Attention with one threshold and one horror behaviour"; AT-11 makes a told breach without teeth a named design failure (cry-wolf; commit dominates abort), and the art bible names the reach as the signature image (ART 5.4). *Cites.* AT:197, AT:373, CON MVP definition, ART:546.

**HE-2. A real horror is a true SIM entity that touches nothing optical.** *Rule.* A horror has a true integer position in its own block, is solid to Layout solids and to nothing else (it passes through crewmates and they through it, BDY-17), is drawn in the single Y-sorted layer so its sprite hides what stands behind it, is hidden by walls and by darkness, and is revealed only by light. It **contributes no occluder, casts no shadow, emits nothing, and never changes the beam, any subject's `lux`, `lit` or any emitter field.** "Real occlusion" in the index row and the routed thread is read as *visual opacity by draw order plus locomotion against Layout solids*, never as a `LightOccluder2D`. Nothing a horror does is a light event except the disturbance state of HE-35, which Lighting reads and executes. *Why.* Three designed GDDs decide it (EM-20 (c) and AC-24; Lighting rail 10 "Horrors cast no shadow and emit nothing" and AC-LO-16; Failure & Trail FT-31) and the art bible's own recommendation agrees (ART:296); a horror shadow would destroy the free stage-2 sanity falsifier "a shadow with no occluder" and with it Pillar 4's "no lie is unfalsifiable". "Displace dust" from the art bible is retained in spirit through the marks of HE-30, not through the Attention Presenter's shared dust process. *(orchestrator ruling: option A, affirmed by the creative-director at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate as R1; the fiction gloss "the web does not stop light; it only takes it" is adopted; the wording refreshes are owed rows, never edits here.)* **Gate.** Until the owed ART:542, ART 5.5, PIPE:140 and IDX:35 refresh rows land, the art bible and this GDD contradict each other; those rows are a BLOCKING precondition of `/asset-spec` for the rig (AC-HE-123), as C-HE-21 is for rime. *Cites.* EM:208, LO:151, FT:199, ART:296, ART:542.

**HE-3. Block and hook identity.** *Rule.* Block `horror.state`: class `SIM`, `persist` true, `persist_scope` WORLD, scope SHARED, `repl_hint` SHARED_SUMMARY, `schema_version` 1, `supported_versions` [1], `restore_after` `[layout.state, graph.state, emitter.state, attention.state]`. Hook `horror.step`: divisor 5, offset 0, `after: [attention.step, emitter.place]` (the must-repeats of Attention (AT:373), the Emitter (EM AC-60) and the kernel (KRN:201), exactly as ruled; C-HE-20 closed). A hook id that is unknown at boot fails boot (CR-3, no alias concept); grep of `horror.step`, `horror.state` and `horror.view` across the neighbours before closing found no collision. The read view is `horror.view` (HE-37). *Why.* `body.integrate` precedes `horror.step` on every Horror tick through `emitter.place`'s own `after` list (EM:104: `after: [body.integrate, graph.propagate.failure]`) and the CR-3 total order, so the same-tick seat read that the exact walk-out arithmetic of HE-F2 needs holds without extending the ruled list; Horror writes nothing Body reads, so no cycle can form. A one-tick-stale seat read would let an escaping seat read up to 2 px closer than it is. *Cites.* KRN:71, KRN:201, EM:104, AT:125-131, BDY-8.

**HE-4. Cadence: one run is one rig pose, and what Horror reads between Attention's runs.** *Rule.* `horror.step` runs on ticks that are multiples of 5 (12 Hz), so one run is exactly one 12 fps rig pose (PIPE row 14). Attention and the Graph run at divisor 6; LCM(5, 6) = 30, so the two hooks coincide only when `T mod 30 = 0`, and CR-3's `after` orders the pair only on those ticks. On every other run Horror reads `attention.view` **as committed by Attention's most recent run**; this is a reading of committed state, not a new kernel rule. Presentation interpolates `pos_prev` to `pos_curr` across the five ticks of a run; the rig pose is stepped, never tweened. Every Horror duration is authored as an integer count of runs and published in ticks as `runs × 5` (`HORROR_RUN_TICKS` 5, DERIVED from the divisor); a duration that is not a multiple of 5 is a data-load lint failure (it would silently round up to the next run, a 1 to 4 tick bias that matters at the `UNFOLD_LO` bound). **The lint covers every SIM-clock duration** (`UNFOLD_LO`, `UNFOLD_HI`, `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS`, `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS`, `RETURN_DELAY_TICKS`, `LURK_DWELL_TICKS` and the rest of the type row); it **exempts the L3 disturbance lengths `L3_DUR_LO` and `L3_DUR_HI`** (18 and 30), which are published to Lighting in ticks and are presentation-timed (LO-19, the gutter grammar), not run-quantised: a disturbance *starts* on a run, its length does not round to one (C-HE-29). *Cites.* PIPE:303, KRN:71, HE-F5.

**HE-5. Data-driven type row.** *Rule.* Every per-type value lives in a data file keyed by `type_id`; behaviour code is generic over the row and contains no behavioural constant. A row carries: `v_creep_mpx`, `v_hunt_mpx`, `r_snap_px`, `reach_range_px`, `hull_contact_px`, `strike_bp`, `strike_cooldown_ticks`, `strike_hold_runs`, `unfold_lo`, `unfold_hi`, `lurk_standoff_px`, `lurk_dwell_ticks`, the L3 constants, the mark and rig asset ids. The data lint refuses any field named `damage`, `accuracy`, `armour`, `dps`, `xp`, `unlock`, `health`, `hp`, `kill`, `loot`, `threat`, `aggro` or `hate` (Horror's own harm field is `strike_bp`, a Suit-scale amount, never `damage`). *Why.* Coding standards (gameplay values are data-driven) and the Item DB's lint AP-2; the field list is also the anti-combat guard (HE-38). *Cites.* IDB:97.

**HE-6. Boundaries.** *Rule.* Horror does not read or write Attention's level, does not call `set_beacon`, does not schedule a breach, does not add a Graph footprint kind, does not read any `PERCEPTION_OUT` block, does not move, push or stagger a player (the stagger is the Controller's response to the Suit rung), places no content, narrates nothing, calls no physics query, owns no sound, mix or cue name, owns no HUD element, and holds no stimulus tracker, accumulator, "awareness", "alert", "threat" or "aggro" value, persisted or derived. *Why.* Guard rail 1: absorbing Attention is the most likely way this GDD drifts; the grep of this document for those words outside a negation returns zero (Acceptance Criteria lint).

#### Group B — Permission, tell composition and the audit

**HE-7. Phase-sourced behaviour, no stored drive.** *Rule.* Every transition between LURK and STALK, and every harm gate, is a function of `attention.view` fields only: `phase`, `tell_epoch`, `tell_outcome`, `locus_section_id`, `exogenous_permitted(section)`, `breach_enter_T`, `phase_enter_T`. The target (HE-13) is recomputed from physical `lux` on every run and is **not persisted**. The only things the record remembers are where it is, what state it is in since when, what it has already done this `tell_epoch` (counts), the locked point of a REACH in flight, and the stream counter. *Why.* AT-2 (one level for the world), Pillar 1 (no hidden stat), guard rail 1. *Cites.* AT:139, AT:214-232.

**HE-8. The permission predicate, repeated at three check points.** *Rule.* Every web-originated harm call (M17 RUPTURE, M17 GOUGE, `apply_suit_damage`) is made only if `exogenous_permitted(section)` holds **at the call tick**, where for a bodily hit `section` is Body's `actor_section(seat)` (crew-body scope, AT-15 R15; not the Graph's `section_of`) and for a hull deposit it is the section of the hull segment. The three check points are: (1) **REACH begin** (HE-14 condition c), (2) **STRIKE entry** (HE-15), (3) **every M17 RUPTURE or GOUGE call** (HE-16, HE-29). **CHILL is a non-harm footprint, permitted in any phase, bounded by HE-F4 and HE-31** (C-HE-28). A REACH may *begin* only in BREACH (not SUBSIDING); an in-flight REACH resolves only if the predicate still holds at its last tick, otherwise it fizzles with no harm, no hairlines, no GOUGE or RUPTURE (the touch-down smear laid at REACH start stays: it is true). Horror issues no STRESS (Attention's alone, AT-11). The registered formula `tell_window_contract` is the audit: for every harm event `e` there is an epoch `k` with `tell_start_k + tell_len_k <= t_e < subside_end_k`, `tell_len_k >= TELL_MIN_TICKS` and `section_e` in `locus_body_k` (the epoch's locus body in force at `t_e`: recorded at TELL entry, replaced only by an AT-14 fallback that TellAudit records as LOCUS_FALLBACK); zero violations over a replay. This GDD **repeats** AT-15; it does not restate a different rule. *Cites.* AT:211, REG `tell_window_contract`, AT:794-810.

**HE-9. Tell composition: additive layers, never a substitute.** *Rule.* The Graph's 10-30 s requirement (LSG:144, LSG:1063) is met by **Attention's window** (AT-15); nothing in Horror shortens it, defers it or replaces it. Horror adds layers on top, and a harm path that skips one of them is a defect:

| Layer | Owner | Duration | What the player can perceive | Present for |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L0 Attention tell | Attention | `TELL_MIN_TICKS` 600 to `TELL_MAX_TICKS` 1800 | beats, filaments, dust, lamp gutter, audio (Attention's) | every harm path |
| L1 Presence and stalk | Horror | from ingress (at least `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` after TELL entry) or from TELL entry if already resident, to the first REACH | the entity in light, marks, disturbance dips, audio facts | bodily and hull paths |
| L2 REACH telegraph | Horror | `UNFOLD_TICKS` per strike, 95 to 180 ticks | the limb entering the cone before the body, tip resting on the locked point | every strike |
| L3 Footprint trail | Horror | continuous, any phase | smear, rime, hairlines | all |
| L4 Hull "scrape" | Horror | at least `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` before a RUPTURE | a GOUGE with radial hairlines on the same segment (HE-16) | RUPTURE only |

The kernel's illustrative `rng_int(600, 1800)` on the stream name `"horror.tell"` (KRN D.10) is **not** Horror's tell length and not Horror's REACH range; the stream `"attention.tell"` is Attention's. Horror draws from `"horror.tell"` only the REACH length (HE-14). *Cites.* LSG:144, AT:211, KRN D.10, reflexion entry on examples read as contracts.

**HE-10. TellAudit and the crew-body scope.** *Rule.* Horror publishes in `horror.view` the facts a `TellAudit` replay needs for every harm event (HE-37: `strike_T`, the seats hit, the hull segments gouged or ruptured, the `tell_epoch` it ran in) and submits no harm that the predicate would reject. A lethal act on a crew body outside a told breach is exactly the ambush Pillar 2 forbids, and Horror's stalk is additive and never a substitute. A restored CALM save never contains a lethal horror adjacent to a seat (CALM is non-lethal by construction, HE-11), so autosave needs no Horror-specific gate (SL-5 unchanged); if HE-11 is ever relaxed, SL-5 must be revisited. *Cites.* AT:211, SL:190.

#### Group C — The behaviour cycle

**HE-11. LURK: the non-lethal CALM presence.** *Rule.* In CALM a resident record is in LURK in its current body. It loiters at `V_CREEP_MPX` between waypoints drawn from Layout `anchor_geometry`: it dwells `LURK_DWELL_TICKS` at a waypoint, then the next waypoint is the lowest-hash candidate (a pure integer hash of `(entity_id, dwell_index, HE_HASH_SEED)`) among waypoints in loaded sections of its body that are at least `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` from every living seat. It **never comes within `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` of a living seat**; a seat that walks toward it makes it step away along the room graph, and if cornered it holds still and does nothing. It deposits no RUPTURE and no GOUGE and applies no suit damage; it may be seen, lays marks (HE-30), makes CHILL deposits (HE-27) and disturbance dips (HE-35). A seat may walk through it (no collision, BDY-17); that costs nothing and is a free moment of inspection. Its transitions out of LURK come **only from `attention.view.phase`**, never from the player's light, noise or distance. *Why.* Attention's "CALM is never known-safe" (AT:92, AT:373) is a claim about the phase (a TELL can begin at any time), not a claim that this presence can hurt, and this rule does not imply one: the CALM presence is provably harmless (no harm call, the standoff, the step-away) and **what a CALM sighting is worth is information**: which body it lives in (`RETURN_HOME`), and that its trail and rime are fresh (stage-0 marks), so the player may change the route, the lamp or the first job. It is not made dangerous to keep the dread up, because a harm in CALM would arrive with no tell and break Pillar 2; the standoff and the phase-only transitions also keep it from becoming a timer or a second meter (AT-2). Validation target (7) measures whether the information is used (AC-HE-122); if it is not, the first lever is `RETURN_HOME` 0 (OQ-1), never relaxing this rule. *Cites.* AT:92, AT:373.

**HE-12. STALK: creep in a TELL, hunt in a BREACH.** *Rule.* On a TELL entry (a `tell_epoch` different from the record's `last_seen_tell_epoch`) a record whose body holds the locus goes LURK → STALK; a record in another body goes LURK → TRANSIT (HE-20). In a TELL, STALK moves toward the target (HE-13) at `V_CREEP_MPX` and stops at `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` from every living seat; it deposits no RUPTURE and no GOUGE and calls no `apply_suit_damage`. In BREACH it moves at `V_HUNT_MPX` and closes to `REACH_RANGE_PX` of the target point. STALK is one state; the speed is a function of `attention.view.phase`. **A TELL abort** (the phase leaves TELL with an aborted `tell_outcome`) returns STALK to LURK with no strike and no mark burst. Entering SUBSIDING sends STALK to RETREAT. *Why.* Pillar 2 (the stalk is the additive earlier layer) and Pillar 3. *Cites.* AT:373, AT-16.

**HE-13. Target from physical light.** *Rule.* Candidates are living seats (not DOWNED, DEAD or IN_POD per the Controller's `loco_state`) whose `actor_section` is in `locus_body` (TELL, BREACH, SUBSIDING) or in the record's own body (CALM). One `lux(ref)` read per candidate per run, zero probes. A candidate is lit if the Emitter's `lit` hysteresis flag is set (`LIT_ON_PM` 150, CITED). The target is the lit candidate with the highest `lux_pm`, ties lowest seat id; if no candidate is lit, the target is the locus section's anchor (the anchor REACH of HE-16). **The preference is recomputed every run and never stored.** The horror moves identically lit or unlit and is never repelled, stunned, slowed or harmed by light. This GDD **adopts the Emitter's text verbatim**, as the second GDD to land: "No beam stuns, burns, repels or damages a horror or a crewmate ... being revealed is the beam's whole effect" (EM:89). Objects are never targets at MVP: a dropped glowstick is a Chemlight pool that counts as exposure to whoever stands in it, but it is not a seat, so it does not pull the horror toward itself; flares, torches and decoys that repel or distract are Vertical Slice (EM:89). *Why.* Pillar 3 made legible ("you lit up, it came") with no meter. With two or more lit seats a stateless argmax can alternate; a hysteresis would need a stored value, which is a Vertical Slice question (Open Questions). *Cites.* EM:89, EM-21.

**HE-14. REACH: the telegraph (unfold).** *Rule.* STALK → REACH begins at a run when **all** of the following hold: (a) `phase` is BREACH; (b) the target is a lit seat, or the anchor under HE-16; (c) `exogenous_permitted(actor_section(target seat))` holds for a seat REACH, and `exogenous_permitted(anchor section)` holds for an anchor REACH; (d) the distance from the horror's foot point to the point is at most `REACH_RANGE_PX`; (e) `T >= cooldown_until_T`. At that run it **locks** the point P (the target seat's foot point; non-tracking) and draws `UNFOLD_TICKS = 5 × rng_int(UNFOLD_LO/5, UNFOLD_HI/5)` from `rng_derive(world_seed, "horror.tell", counter)`; the counter increments in the same block write, is persisted, and a restore never rerolls (a REACH that later fizzles has still consumed its draw). The body is rooted for the whole REACH; the limb extends toward P; `reach_end_T = reach_start_T + UNFOLD_TICKS` is a multiple of 5, so the strike resolves exactly on a run. During REACH the record publishes `state`, `locked_pt` and `reach_end_T` in `horror.view` (the rig and Audio read them). `UNFOLD_LO` and `UNFOLD_HI` must be multiples of 5 (data lint). The picture is the warning: the rig's reach-limb tip rests on P for the last part of the unfold (Visual/Audio Requirements), and the telegraph is **the limb entering the cone before the body**; no icon, ring, arc, disc, radius or ground decal marks the strike point. **The tip rests on P, which is the victim's own foot point, inside the victim's own light (EM-21: the owner stands in their own light), so the tip is lit for the victim wherever the body stands**; a touch-down SMEAR is laid at P at REACH start (a true mark and a ring entry, not a marker); Audio renders a positional REACH cue from `locked_pt`: a non-percussive lead-in started `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` 90 before `reach_end_T` (registry `reach_resolve_start`, `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS <= UNFOLD_LO`) and ending at `reach_end_T` with no impact transient (Audio AA-28 b). There is **no lit-self gate**: the horror moves identically lit or unlit (HE-13). The point is locked at the start and never updated, so moving out of the strike disc (HE-15) always works. If the blind-legibility acceptance criterion fails, the fallback is a larger `UNFOLD_LO` or a smaller `R_SNAP_PX`, never a victim-side marker (a marker is a HUD by another name, Pillar 3). *(orchestrator ruling: checkpoint CP-1; C-HE-9 closed.)* *Why.* ART 5.4 ("a limb entering the cone before the body"), Pillar 2; a tracking strike is learnable never. *Cites.* HE-F2, HE-F5, ART:540.

**HE-15. STRIKE: bodily resolution.** *Rule.* At the first run with `T >= reach_end_T` and the predicate satisfied, the state becomes STRIKE for `STRIKE_HOLD_RUNS` runs (the snap pose; no travel). On the entry run, for **every** living, non-DOWNED, non-DEAD seat whose `actor_section` is the section of P, whose room (Layout `room_at`) equals the room of P, whose foot point is **strictly within** `R_SNAP_PX` of P (`dist < R_SNAP_PX`), and for which `exogenous_permitted(actor_section(seat))` holds at that tick, Horror calls `apply_suit_damage(player_id, STRIKE_BP, source_ref = the horror's ref)` once. `apply_suit_damage` is the 3-argument Suit mutator; the class (EXOGENOUS) travels on `source_ref`, classed EXOGENOUS once at record creation (boot or ingress arrival), persisted with the record, and valid across restore independently of E6 (SE:322, SE-23); an unclassed ref is a programmer error. The room test stops a strike through a wall. A seat standing on a door sill belongs to the sill's room (Layout `room_at`). The strike is target-agnostic *within a body REACH*: any living seat in the disc is hit, whether or not it was the target. `STRIKE_BP` 3200 satisfies HE-F3 (strike one: 10000 → 6800, below `SUIT_HP_OK`, rung STRAINED; strike two → 3600, below `SUIT_LEAK_HP`, CRITICAL, stagger, walk-only; strike three → 400, below `SUIT_HP_FAIL`, FAILED → DOWNED → `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800 → DEAD, Suit's ladder, Save/Load's restore). Each exogenous harm lands through `apply_suit_damage(..., EXOGENOUS)` only after the horror's own 10-30 s tell (HE-9). Horror assumes `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` 1 (an EXOGENOUS hit sets the integrity rung from `integrity_bp` with no limiter hold; Interaction's IA-31 now quotes the default 1, matching the registry: C-HE-5 closed 2026-10-27). Horror never moves, pushes or staggers a seat. A STRIKE publishes `strike_T` and the seats hit (HE-37) as the hit cue, because Suit's STRAINED rung is inert and carries no witness of its own (SE:83, SE:129). **The hit mark.** On the same entry run, if `apply_suit_damage` was actually called at least once, Horror lays exactly one **STRUCK** mark at P (HE-27, HE-30); if it was called for no seat, no STRUCK mark is laid. A touch-down SMEAR is laid at REACH start whether or not the strike lands (HE-30), so without the STRUCK mark the floor would look the same after a hit and after a miss; with it the floor records a hit, and a death has a post-hoc cause the player can read (validation target 1). The mark is a true ring entry written by the same run that struck, never a marker and never read back as a hidden value. *Why.* The user's decision ("death is a bitch. It happens.") bounded to three told strikes. **Strike one is not a visible rung:** Suit's integrity STRAINED (`SUIT_LEAK_HP <= integrity_bp < SUIT_HP_OK`) is inert and has no witness by design (Suit's creative-director review chose none; reversal path SE OQ-19). Strike one is therefore witnessed by the strike itself (snap pose, Audio's positional strike cue from `strike_T`), by Suit's integrity reading, and by the STRUCK mark that stays on the floor after the moment has passed (a miss leaves only the touch-down smear); the first physical consequence is strike two (leak, seam jet, CRITICAL, stagger, walk-only). A hit that left no trace anywhere would be a hidden stat (Pillar 1), which is why the strike itself carries the witness and the floor keeps it: the pipe, not the number. *Cites.* SE:322, SE:221, SE:223, HE-F2, HE-F3.

**HE-16. Hull acts: GOUGE, RUPTURE and the anchor REACH.** *Rule.* (1) *Hull GOUGE with a strike.* If P lies within `HULL_CONTACT_PX` of a hull-segment face, the STRIKE entry run also deposits GOUGE (`deposit_trace(GOUGE, hull_segment_id, magnitude <= FOOTPRINT_MAX_GOUGE_BP)`) on that segment, at most `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` per `tell_epoch`, and lays radial hairlines at the contact point. **GOUGE subjects are hull segments only** (binding on this GDD by the creative-director's CD-GDD-ALIGN ruling, FT-31 and FT:1412): a duct, cable, pipe or door is never a GOUGE subject (lint `GOUGE_NON_HULL`). (2) *RUPTURE.* A RUPTURE (`deposit_trace(RUPTURE, hull_segment_id, ...)`, hull only) is issued only on a STRIKE whose P is within `HULL_CONTACT_PX` of a hull segment that **this epoch already carries a Horror GOUGE**, not earlier than `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` after that GOUGE (layer L4 of HE-9), and at most `RUPTURES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` 1 per epoch. Each act has its own REACH and its own predicate check. (3) *Anchor REACH (no lit seat).* With no lit candidate the target is the locus section's anchor: **the lowest-id anchor (Layout `anchor_geometry`) of the locus section's outermost compartment that lies within `HULL_CONTACT_PX` of a hull-segment face; if there is none, the REACH lays hairlines only.** The horror stalks to `REACH_RANGE_PX` of it and runs a REACH whose resolution lays radial hairlines and, because `ANCHOR_REACH_GOUGES` defaults to 1, **one hull GOUGE** (hull only, under `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX`, never RUPTURE, never `apply_suit_damage`). Its `fatigued` creep is ENDOGENOUS and told by the Graph's own gate (P2 holds), so going dark now costs the ship (AT-11, Pillar 5). *(orchestrator ruling: checkpoint CP-4; C-HE-24 resolved.)* (4) *The Graph's consequence is the Graph's.* A GOUGE sets the segment `fatigued` (LSG M17 row); the creep it starts is an ENDOGENOUS clock that outlives the breach and sits under the Graph's own `W_MIN_WARNING_S` gate. Horror neither alters nor shortens it. (5) *Read owed.* Mapping P to a Graph hull-segment id needs a lookup that no designed GDD yet publishes (`hull_segments_near(section_id, px, r)`, name provisional, owner Layout or the Graph): *(provisional — Layout/Graph read owed, C-HE-11)*. *Why.* R2: the physical half of the footprint grammar, hull-only, bounded per epoch; the "scrape then tear" ladder keeps even a hull RUPTURE preceded by a told GOUGE. *(Open Question: whether RUPTURE belongs in the anchor ladder at all; at the default it does not, so RUPTURE is reachable only when a lit seat stands beside a hull face that already carries this epoch's Horror GOUGE.)* *Cites.* FT:199, FT:1412, LSG:196.

**HE-17. RETREAT, cooldown and SUBSIDING.** *Rule.* After a STRIKE the state is RETREAT: the horror moves at `V_CREEP_MPX` along the room graph to the nearest waypoint at least `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` from every living seat, and `cooldown_until_T = strike_T + STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` (invariant `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS > STAGGER_TICKS` 30, CITED). A **fizzle** (the predicate fails at the REACH's last tick, the phase has returned to CALM, or a restore finds the predicate false) goes to RETREAT with no harm, no hairlines, no GOUGE or RUPTURE and **no cooldown consumed**, so a fizzle cannot starve the next legitimate REACH; **the touch-down smear stays** (it is true). RETREAT → STALK when `T >= cooldown_until_T` and `phase` is BREACH; RETREAT → LURK when the phase is CALM or TELL and the record is not returning home. In SUBSIDING no new REACH starts and STALK goes to RETREAT; an in-flight REACH that reaches `reach_end_T` during SUBSIDING **resolves** (the predicate holds in SUBSIDING for sections in `locus_body`, so TellAudit passes) and then RETREATs, unless the knob `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH` is 1 (the merciful variant, default 0). *(orchestrator ruling: this reconciles the two clauses "SUBSIDING sends the horror to RETREAT" and "an in-flight REACH resolves iff the predicate holds at its last tick" as resolve-then-RETREAT; ambiguity C-HE-10.)* Pod-hop: the departed seat leaves `locus_body`, so `exogenous_permitted(actor_section(seat))` fails and an in-flight REACH on it fizzles; on a derelict locus, the departure of the last occupant recorded at BREACH entry also ends the breach (AT-12 (iii)). *Cites.* AT:200, AT:203, SE-24.

**HE-18. Never harm what is already down.** *Rule.* DOWNED and DEAD seats are not candidates (HE-13) and are skipped at resolution (HE-15). After the third strike the seat is DOWNED and the clock `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` is the Controller's and Suit's (SE-24); death and restore belong to Save/Load (SL-7). Horror owns only the act and its tell. A seat that becomes DOWNED or DEAD during a REACH is not hit; the REACH completes harmlessly (the telegraph was already told) and the record RETREATs. *Cites.* SE:223, SL:197.

**HE-19. Strike scope is geometry, not intent.** *Rule.* Resolution reads only Layout `room_at` and `room_adjacency` (open or closed leaf), the horror's and the seats' integer positions, and `solid_in` for the horror's own movement. No `probe()` and no physics query. The straight segment from the horror's foot point to P must not cross a closed door leaf (checked through the room-graph sill, not a ray). The same-room test (HE-15) is re-evaluated at resolution against the *current* layout, so a weld or cut during a REACH is honoured while the locked point stays the stored integer it was.

#### Group D — Presence: ingress, TRANSIT, return, dormancy, weld and cut

**HE-20. Instance, ingress, TRANSIT, egress and return.** *Rule.* The record has a **home body** (default: derelict B, the web-intrusion side, DA OQ-V5). At TELL entry, if `body_of(locus_section_id)` is not the record's body, the record leaves its body (E7 `unregister_subject`, an egress mark at the old position), enters `TRANSIT` with `transit_until_T = tell_enter_T + INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS`, and arrives at a hull segment of the locus body chosen by HE-F9: the compartment **one room hop from the room of the highest-`lux_pm` living seat in the locus body** (none lit or none present: the locus section's outermost compartment; ties lowest room id, then lowest hull-segment id), **never a room holding a living seat and never inside `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` of one**, no earlier than `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` after TELL entry. The ingress mark (smear and radial hairlines at the segment) is laid `INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` before the body appears, so a player looking at that wall sees the arrival coming; on arrival the record registers (E6) and enters STALK (or LURK if the phase is already CALM after an abort). If no entry is legal the record stays in TRANSIT and retries each run (diagnostic `horror_ingress_pending`; a BREACH entered with a pending ingress is a cry-wolf defect and an acceptance criterion). **Return:** when `RETURN_HOME` is 1 (default [PH]), `RETURN_DELAY_TICKS` after SUBSIDING ends or after a TELL abort the record returns to its home body by the same rule (no seat target: the home body's outermost compartment, lowest ids; the legality tests still apply); when `RETURN_HOME` is 0 it does not return and LURKs in the body of the last breach (the creative-director's lean; an Open Question bound to validation target (2), the 70 % CALM sighting: if the target fails, flip this knob first). All choices are deterministic functions of Attention's locus, Emitter reads and ids; nothing is authored (DA:101 "authoring places no entity"). TRANSIT is unregistered, has no position, publishes `state` TRANSIT and `transit_until_T`, harms nothing and lays no marks. The fiction is that the web runs through hulls; both ends leave a mark. Whether a CALM presence aboard the home ship after a home-ship breach is acceptable is an Open Question. *Why.* The first draft's "lowest-id hull segment of the outermost compartment" could place the entity rooms from the player and let it arrive late in BREACH (cry-wolf); one-hop ingress keeps teeth and keeps the approach told. *(orchestrator ruling: ingress refined to one room hop.)* *Cites.* DA:101, AT-12.

**HE-21. Dormancy.** *Rule.* A record whose body is unloaded is frozen: no step, no mark ageing, no disturbance, **no fast-forward** (LS-17, LSG:174). A mark's `life_left` falls only while the mark's section is loaded. A record in TRANSIT whose destination body unloads waits. Answer to Web Map ASK-H1: yes. A record may enter TRANSIT toward an unloaded body (for example a return home to derelict B while it is unloaded). It stays in TRANSIT, unregistered, with no position, no marks and no harm, and arrives at its first run after the body loads with a legal HE-20 entry. The transit machine's `TRANSIT_HOLD` and pod states are Web Map's and are never read as Horror's TRANSIT. *Cites.* LSG:174, LS-17, WM:458.

**HE-22. Weld and cut.** *Rule.* The path cache is keyed by the layout (HE-25) and recomputed on any `layout.state` change (a weld or cut applying, MSA-14 / MSA-20). If a weld changes `locus_body` membership, the target body is re-derived from `locus_section_id` on the next run (`locus_body` is recorded at TELL entry and is replaced mid-epoch only by an AT-14 fallback, which TellAudit records as LOCUS_FALLBACK; Horror re-reads `locus_section_id` each run). If a cut removes the section the record stands in from its body, the record's `body_id` is re-derived from `body_of(section_id)`; if its section is gone the record goes to TRANSIT with an egress mark and re-enters its home body. Horror has no cut verb and does not interrupt a cut; Modular Ship Assembly states the same ("Horror and Attention have no cut verb; the breach rhythm uses STRESS and M17, not detaches", MSA-22). *Cites.* MSA-14 / MSA-20 (weld and cut apply), MSA-22 (no cut verb).

#### Group E — Movement and pathing

**HE-23. Own position, Body's pure step.** *Rule.* Positions are Horror's, in integer base px with a two-axis sub-pixel residue and `last_octant`, inside `horror.state`. Movement calls Body's pure `LocomotionStep` (BDY-10) with `collide` bound to Layout `solid_in`, using Body's foot box (`FOOT_BOX_W` 16, `FOOT_BOX_H` 8, CITED; the 64 to 96 px sprite is visual only, because a larger box could never use an open doorway whose passage extent is exactly 16 across). Per run the horror picks one octant (the integer argmax of the eight unit vectors against the direction to the next waypoint, ties lowest octant) and steps once per tick for the five ticks of the run. There are no `AGENT` rows in Body at MVP (preserving `MAX_ACTORS` 8 headroom for Vertical Slice B seats), no `CharacterBody2D` and no physics query (CR-13 G3, H10). A horror's footprint is a Graph trail or a Horror mark, never a Body fact (BDY-4). `pos_prev` is the position at run start and `pos_curr` the position at run end. -> ADR candidate (1): horror position ownership versus Body `AGENT` rows. *Cites.* BDY-4, BDY-10, BDY-15.

**HE-24. Speeds and the escape invariant.** *Rule.* Registered constants, in milli-px per tick: `V_CREEP_MPX` 1000 (LURK, STALK in a TELL, RETREAT, the arrival walk), `V_HUNT_MPX` 1500 (STALK in BREACH). Each tick adds the speed to an accumulator, steps `floor(acc / 1000)` px and keeps the remainder (persisted per record). Registered invariant (HE-F6): `V_CREEP_MPX < V_HUNT_MPX < V_WALK × 1000` (2000), so **a walking seat, including a CRITICAL walk-only seat, always outpaces it**, and there is no lunge travel (the strike resolves at the locked point). A seat that is *carrying* moves at `V_CARRY` 1 (CITED, BDY-13): `V_HUNT_MPX` exceeds it, so a carrier cannot out-walk the approach, and a carrier needs 72 ticks to clear the disc against 40 available (`UNFOLD_LO` 95 less 54 for reaction less 1 for command application; conflict C-HE-8): the carrier's answer is to drop the cargo and walk, or accept a hit. *(orchestrator ruling: stated as intended; hauling is where the horror bites, Pillar 5 risk; the DA-29 wording note is an owed row.)* *Cites.* DA-29, BDY-13, REG `V_CARRY`.

**HE-25. Pathing over Layout reads.** *Rule.* The horror searches an integer room graph built from Layout `room_adjacency(section_id)` over every section of its body (`sections_of`), excluding any edge whose door leaf is closed. Breadth-first by hop count, ties lowest room id, at most `PATH_EXPANSIONS_MAX` node expansions per run (HE-F10); the result is cached as a next-hop table per goal room and layout key. Inside a room it steers by octant toward the next waypoint (a door sill or an `anchor_geometry` point). On a NONE or UNKNOWN Layout read it holds its last waypoint for at most `HOLD_MAX_RUNS`, then goes to LURK; **UNKNOWN is never read as "unlit" or as "open"**. If `step` reports no movement for `BLOCKED_RUNS_MAX` consecutive runs it discards the cached hop and re-paths once. Zero `probe()` calls at MVP. `NavigationServer2D` and `NavigationAgent2D` are rejected (node- and server-based, outside the replay hash; H10, G3). `route_length_px` stays diagnostic (LY:310). Whether door open or closed state is part of `layout_hash()` is not stated by Layout; until it is, the cache key includes a Horror-computed hash of the closed-leaf set read each run (C-HE-13). -> ADR candidate (2). *Cites.* LY:296-310, LY:446, EM:723.

**HE-26. Doors and the counterplay set.** *Rule.* A closed door leaf is solid to the horror (Layout `solid_in`). It issues no `set_opening`, opens no door, and may GOUGE or RUPTURE a hull segment, never a door. The **whole MVP counterplay set** is: retreat (walk away: `V_HUNT_MPX < V_WALK × 1000`), close a door (the strike room test and solid leaf), go dark (the seat leaves target preference on the next run; its price is every lit-only channelled verb for the breach and the ship under STRESS unattended), pod-hop (the departed seat leaves `locus_body`, so `exogenous_permitted(actor_section(seat))` fails and an in-flight REACH on it fizzles; on a derelict locus, the departure of the last occupant recorded at BREACH entry also ends the breach, AT-12 (iii)), drop the cargo (turns a carrier into a walker) and the **glowstick work-light** (*light the job, not yourself*: a thrown Chemlight lights a workspace so the player can step out of it dark; it does not pull the horror). The suppressant is *not* in the set (HE-39). **The anti-pillar's "tools may repel or distract (flares, torches, decoys)" is deliberately unused at MVP and deferred to Vertical Slice (`repel`, flares, decoys): MVP counterplay is avoidance only, and the routed thread's "tools repel, never kill" is a boundary (no tool ever kills, HE-38) and not an MVP promise that a tool repels.** *(orchestrator ruling: the earlier phrase "glowstick lure" is reworded because HE-13 prefers seats only and a glowstick is not a seat; creative-director concurs; decoys are Vertical Slice.)* No response is free and none is dominant: abort loses the job, hiding loses the task and the ship, going dark loses the lit verbs, committing risks a rung. *Cites.* AT-12, DA-29, EM:89.

#### Group F — Footprints

**HE-27. Footprint taxonomy: two halves, both truth channel.** *Rule.* Horror's footprints split exactly as Failure & Trail splits them: M17 kinds surface only as Graph descriptors that F&T draws, and the web-grammar marks are Horror's own.

| Kind | Half | Subject | Cap | Mark | Channel and path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CHILL | physical | compartment | one deposit of `CHILL_STEP_DJ` per rime mark, laid per `CHILL_SPACING_PX` of travel and at each LURK dwell start; `CHILL_STEP_DJ <= FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q` (registry, LSG:912); `CHILL_DEPOSITS_PER_EPOCH_MAX`; a deposit withheld by the epoch cap or the cold margin still leaves its rime mark (ring-only, HE-F4) | branching rime | M17, Class B, path (a) |
| GOUGE | physical | **hull segment only** | `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX`; magnitude `<= FOOTPRINT_MAX_GOUGE_BP` (registry, LSG:914) | radial hairlines | M17 |
| RUPTURE | physical | **hull segment only** | `RUPTURES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` | radial hairlines | M17 |
| STRESS | none | none | Horror issues none (Attention's alone, AT-11) | none | none |
| SMEAR | web grammar | floor point | one per `MARK_SPACING_PX` of travel, plus one touch-down smear at P per REACH start | wet iridescent smear | `horror.state` ring |
| RIME | web grammar | CHILL deposit point | one per CHILL | branching rime | ring |
| HAIRLINES | web grammar | hull contact point | one per STRIKE, GOUGE, RUPTURE, anchor REACH, ingress and egress | radial hairlines | ring |
| STRUCK | web grammar | strike point P | one per STRIKE entry run on which `apply_suit_damage` was called at least once; none on a miss | a full radial hairline burst centred on P (HAIRLINES class: same palette, same sheet family) | ring |

**STRUCK is the hit-only mark** (CD-GDD-ALIGN): the touch-down SMEAR is laid at REACH start on a hit and on a miss alike, so STRUCK is what makes the floor differ after the two. It is a HAIRLINES-class mark for priority, protection from smear eviction, lifetime and ageing, and it is laid whether or not P is near a hull face (a hull contact adds its own HAIRLINES at the contact point, so a hit at a wall leaves both). Dust direction and filament twitch are Vertical Slice A: the dust process is the Attention Presenter's, and `web_strand_*`, `vfx_tell_*` and the band-slide are exclusive to it (AV-18, AC-AT-113). The Graph has no mark record, so a persisted Horror ring is the only way a reload cannot lie by omission (Pillar 1). *CHILL is bookkeeping, and this document says so:* at `CHILL_STEP_DJ` 3000 a mark cools a 20-tile room by 0.05 degC (`C_TILE` 3000, registry, LSG:458), below the Graph's own "invisible" row (LSG:912) and far below the COOL band; the rime on the floor is **a surface mark the web leaves, not a claim about air temperature**, and the physical deposit is route-choice and Debug Overlay bookkeeping. Claiming otherwise would be the hidden-stat shape Pillar 1 forbids. *(orchestrator ruling: option B of the CHILL magnitude question; dropping CHILL at MVP would also be acceptable and is the degrade path.)* *Cites.* FT:199, FT:1412, LSG:196, LSG:912, CON:243.

**HE-28. Write path (a): published mutators, no commands.** *Rule.* `deposit_trace` (M17) and `apply_suit_damage` are **path (a)** of CR-9: published unconditional Class B mutators called from Horror's own P3 hook; the kernel names "a horror writing a footprint into the Graph's footprint channel" as the path-(a) example (KRN:104). Horror submits **no `SYSTEM`-origin command at MVP**, because no target of Horror's can reject. Attention's C22 and E-29 ("SYSTEM commands in P2") and LSG:807 ("the first 16 in arrival order within P2") carry a P2 premise that path (a) does not satisfy; this GDD **asserts no drain order** and lists both as owed refresh rows (C-HE-3). *Why.* The kernel is the authority on paths, and the budget rule of HE-29 holds under any drain order. *Cites.* KRN:104, AT:197, LSG:807.

**HE-29. Per-window footprint budget and per-run cap.** *Rule.* Horror counts its accepted M17 calls per **Graph window**, defined as *the deposits the next `graph.propagate` run drains*: writes made at tick `T` in `[6k, 6k+5]` drain at the Graph run at `6(k+1)`, because `horror.step` runs after `graph.propagate.failure` on coinciding ticks (via `attention.step` and `emitter.place`) and so can never be drained by the run it follows (window index `w = floor(T / 6)`, the Graph's divisor 6, offset 0, LSG:104). Horror never exceeds `HE_FOOTPRINT_BUDGET = MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP − STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` (16 − 8 = 8; DERIVED) per window, regardless of drain order. Because Horror's divisor (5) differs from the Graph's (6), one Graph window in five contains **two** Horror runs (`T mod 30` of 0 and 5, both inside `[6k, 6k+5]` for `k = 5j`); a per-run cap `MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` 4 keeps two runs at or under the window budget (`2 × MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN <= HE_FOOTPRINT_BUDGET`). Priority inside a run: RUPTURE, then GOUGE, then CHILL. Writes over either bound are **not attempted** (a trace is "a moment, not a queue", LSG:807) and are counted `horror_footprints_withheld`; in nominal play that counter is a defect signal and never a gameplay state. Horror's 8-call window budget is the remainder of the registered global per-run cap (`MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` 16, "deposit_trace writes honoured per run across all callers", LSG:807), so Horror leaves Attention's `STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` whole. Natural worst case per run: one hull deposit (RUPTURE or GOUGE, one per STRIKE, HE-F17) and one CHILL = 2; the three-kind run of E-HE-13 is a stub fixture for the priority rule (priority RUPTURE, GOUGE, CHILL). *Cites.* AT:197, AC-AT-92, LSG:807, LSG:911, REG `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP`.

**HE-30. The mark ring.** *Rule.* The ring holds at most `MAX_MARKS` records `{mark_id, kind, section_id, pos_px, seed, life_left_ticks}` (`kind` in SMEAR, RIME, HAIRLINES, STRUCK); `mark_id` comes from an in-block counter and is never reused. `seed` is a pure integer hash of `(mark_id, HE_HASH_SEED)`, **never `rng_derive`** (the precedent of Lighting LO-19 and Attention AT-20). On inserting into a full ring the **oldest SMEAR (lowest `mark_id` of kind SMEAR) is evicted; if the ring holds no SMEAR, the lowest `mark_id` of any kind is evicted** (this keeps the rare high-evidence hairlines, STRUCK marks and rime to their `MARK_LIFE_TICKS` without a per-kind quota; HE-F7). `life_left_ticks` starts at `MARK_LIFE_TICKS` and falls by 5 per run while the mark's section is loaded; the mark is removed at zero. A SMEAR is laid per `MARK_SPACING_PX` of moved distance while the record moves and one touch-down SMEAR at P at each REACH start (a stationary horror lays nothing else, so a long LURK does not flush the ring), a RIME at each CHILL deposit, HAIRLINES at hull contact (HE-16) and at ingress and egress, and one STRUCK at P on a STRIKE that called `apply_suit_damage` (HE-15). At most `MARKS_PER_RUN_MAX` inserts per run, in the priority STRUCK, HAIRLINES, RIME, SMEAR (a hit's record is never the insert that is dropped). Marks are laid whenever the entity moves or acts, **regardless of any viewer's hallucination state** (HE-32), and are drawn by a Horror PRESENTATION subscriber in the floor-marks band within `HORROR_MARK_DRAW_GROUPS` of the shared `D_marks` budget (Failure & Trail's `TRAIL_DMARKS_SHARE` 6 of `D_marks` 4-20 "crowds Horror's footprints out", FT:816). Every mutation marks its dirty keys **explicitly**: in Godot 4.7 packed-array element writes do not call the setter, a HIGH silent-failure class (breaking-changes, CR-8). *(orchestrator ruling: checkpoint CP-5; C-HE-12 closed. A long creep can no longer evict the hairlines of a strike while any smear remains.)* *Why.* Option B of the footprint taxonomy: the sim stays the authority and the view is reconstructible after a load. *Cites.* PIPE:137, PIPE:616, FT:816.

**HE-31. Rime and mark honesty.** *Rule.* Rime is **hue-less**, rendered L\* 70 to 78, with **non-repeating** branch angles taken from the mark's `seed`, and **never at a coolant fitting**: a CHILL deposit and its rime mark are withheld when the deposit point is within `FITTING_CLEARANCE_PX` of a coolant-loop fitting node (counter `horror_chill_withheld_fitting`), so a fitting never shows Horror rime. No flesh (hue 5-30, S 20-60 %, V 45-85 %), no violet (hue 270-330, S above 25 %), no Alarm Red, Chemlight or Tungsten appears in any mark (FT:886). A runtime guard withholds a CHILL deposit that would bring a compartment closer to `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` than `CHILL_MARGIN_MC`, and the data lint bounds the cumulative CHILL per compartment per epoch (HE-F4): **the cold of passage is a footprint, never a killer.** The AC-79 collision fixtures (frost fan against branching rime; RUPTURE trail against footprint) are blind-classified and signed off by the art-director. The art-director's proposed palette entry is **Rime White-Grey `#B0B8C0` `[PROVISIONAL]`**; the entry itself is an art-bible decision (creative-director, art-director), and until it lands the rime has no palette candidate (C-HE-21 open; it blocks asset-spec for rime and hairlines, not this GDD). *Cites.* FT:1427, FT:886, FT:1032, REG `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC`.

**HE-32. Footprints survive hallucination.** *Rule.* A real entity, registered under HE-34, keeps depositing M17 writes, marks, disturbance state and suit damage **whether or not any seat's Filter hides its body**; these outputs are bit-identical with and without a Filter stub (CON:243: "the hallucination hides the body, never the trail"). Horror holds no sanity reference and reads no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block (H5, CR-12). Phantom horrors are the Filter's: no Body row, no footprint, no M17 call, no record in `horror.state`, no registration with the Emitter, and Sanity's own rule matches (a phantom "exists only in `sanity.perception`", never registered, no footprint). A HIDE entry names a real HORROR ref; the entity stays registered and keeps obeying AT-15. *(provisional — Sanity & Hallucination Rules designed in this run and the Perception Filter undesigned: they must repeat this or raise a CONFLICT.)* *Cites.* CON:243, SN-22, H5.

**HE-33. Seat reads.** *Rule.* Horror enumerates the seats on a body with Body's `actors_on(body_id)`, reads each seat's `pos_curr(actor_id)` and `actor_section(actor_id)` (Body's SIM read view, BDY-29, BDY-30), and reads the seat's life state (`DOWNED`, `DEAD`, `IN_POD`, `STAGGERED`) from the Controller's `controller.state` `loco_state`, which Suit states is readable (SE:333; the published read name is unconfirmed: C-HE-18). The mapping from actor to `player_id` for `apply_suit_damage` is Body's and the Controller's binding *(provisional — read name owed)*. Horror reads **no** suit integrity value and no Suit rung (it never needs one: the rung ladder is Suit's); it reads no Sanity value and no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block. The predicate argument is always Body's `actor_section`, not the Graph's `section_of`. *Cites.* BDY-29, SE:333, PCI-14.

#### Group G — Light

**HE-34. Emitter subjects.** *Rule.* Each non-TRANSIT record is an Emitter HORROR subject: E6 `register_subject(ref, HORROR, anchor_px, section_id)` at arrival, E8 `set_subject_anchor(ref, anchor_px)` once per run (E8 is called once per run and lands at the next `emitter.place`: the one-tick lag EM:154 accepts. The anchor is otherwise current, because the sim position changes only at runs (HE-23)), E7 `unregister_subject(ref)` at egress or unload; registrations are canonical in `emitter.state` and survive a restore (EM-28, EM:712); in its own `post_restore` the record calls E6 only when it is non-TRANSIT and its ref is absent from the Emitter subject table; a duplicate E6 returns false by design (EM:152) and is not a defect. HORROR is always `moving` (EM-21). An unregistered ref answers like an unlit one (`lux` 0, `lit` false).
