## Formulas

Conventions. Every simulated quantity is an **integer** (ticks, base px, milli-px, basis points, permille, deci-joules, milli-degrees), because anything Horror hashes or replicates must be bit-identical on replay (kernel D.12 `state_hash`, H9): no float, no `sqrt`, no wall clock. Distances are compared as squared integers. Two formulas are analysis only and use real arithmetic (HE-F16's Poisson term and HE-F18's presentation alpha); neither is ever hashed or replicated. `ceil5(x)` rounds `x` up to the next multiple of `HORROR_RUN_TICKS` (5, DERIVED from the hook divisor, HE-4). Locked constants are cited by registry name with the registry value; the Graph's constants (`C_TILE`, `FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q`, `FOOTPRINT_MAX_GOUGE_BP`, `RUPTURE_HP`, `BREACH_HP`, `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS`, registered 2026-10-27) are cited with the registry value and their Life-Support Graph line and never restated as Horror's own. Each two-bound relation below is a **registry-formula candidate** (reflexion 2: register the formula, not only its constants); the registry entries are written from this final text by a later stage, never from the specialist drafts. `[PH]` is a placeholder; ★ marks a value a neighbour quotes.

### HE-F1 Per-window footprint budget

The per-window footprint budget formula is defined as:

`n_w ≤ HE_FOOTPRINT_BUDGET = MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP − STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN;  n_run ≤ MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN;  2 × MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN ≤ HE_FOOTPRINT_BUDGET`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `n_w` | int | 0..8 | block (`fp_count` for `fp_window`) | Horror's accepted M17 calls in one Graph window, where a window is *the deposits the next `graph.propagate` run drains*: writes at `T` in `[6k, 6k+5]` drain at the Graph run at `6(k+1)` (HE-29) |
| `n_run` | int | 0..4 | block | Accepted M17 calls in one `horror.step` run |
| `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` | int | 16 (CITED) | Life-Support Graph, registry (LSG:807) | The Graph's honoured M17 writes per run |
| `STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` | int | 1..16, [PH] 8 (CITED) | Attention, registry | Attention's claim on the same cap; Horror leaves it whole |
| `MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` ★ | int | 1..4, [PH] 4 | Horror | Per-run cap so that the two Horror runs that can share one window sum to the budget |
| `HE_FOOTPRINT_BUDGET` | int | DERIVED 8 | this formula | Never registered with a free value |

**Output Range:** `n_w` in 0..8 and `n_run` in 0..4. Over-budget writes are *not attempted* and are counted `horror_footprints_withheld`; in nominal play that counter is 0 (a defect signal, never a gameplay state). Natural worst case per run: one hull deposit (RUPTURE or GOUGE, one per STRIKE, HE-F17) and one CHILL = 2; the three-kind run of E-HE-13 is a stub fixture for the priority rule (priority RUPTURE, GOUGE, CHILL).

**Example:** window `k = 5` holds the Horror runs at `T = 30` and `T = 35` (one window in five holds two runs because LCM(5, 6) = 30). The run at 30 issues GOUGE + CHILL (2), the run at 35 issues CHILL (1): `n_5 = 3 ≤ 8`. *Saturating double* (AC-AT-92 pattern): a test stub requests 20 deposits in window 5; the first run is granted 4 (cap), the second 4, the other 12 are withheld and `horror_footprints_withheld += 12`; with Attention's 8 STRESS writes the Graph sees 16 and drops none. Windows 1 to 4 hold one Horror run each (`T = 10, 15, 20, 25`).

**Registry candidate:** `horror_footprint_budget`.

### HE-F2 Strike geometry and walk-out time

The strike geometry and walk-out formula is defined as:

`hit(seat) = living(seat) ∧ ¬DOWNED(seat) ∧ ¬DEAD(seat) ∧ actor_section(seat) = section(P) ∧ room_at(seat) = room_at(P) ∧ (dx² + dy²) < R_SNAP_PX² ∧ exogenous_permitted(actor_section(seat));   t_out(V) = ceil(R_SNAP_PX / V)`

with `dx = seat.x − P.x` and `dy = seat.y − P.y`, the seat's foot point read after `body.integrate` on the resolving tick (HE-3).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `seat.x`, `seat.y` | int px | world px | Body `pos_curr(actor_id)` | The seat's foot point |
| `P` | int px pair | world px | `horror.state` (`locked_pt`) | The locked strike point; the seat's foot point at REACH start; never updated |
| `R_SNAP_PX` ★ | int px | 48..96, [PH] 72 | Horror (kept equal to Interaction's `REACH_PX` 72 on purpose, one disc size teaches both reaches) | Strike radius; the disc is **strict** |
| `V` | int px/tick | `V_WALK` 2, `V_RUN` 3, `V_CARRY` 1 (CITED) | Player Controller; Body (BDY-13) | Locomotion class of the escaping seat; a CRITICAL walk-only seat moves at `V_WALK` |
| `room_at` | room id | Layout | Layout | A seat on a door sill belongs to the sill's room; the test stops a strike through a wall |
| `t_out(V)` | int ticks | 24..72 | this formula | Ticks of straight movement needed to stand on the disc boundary |

**Output Range:** `hit` is a boolean; `t_out(V_WALK) = 36`, `t_out(V_RUN) = 24`, `t_out(V_CARRY) = 72`. A seat that does not move is hit; a reactive walker is not (HE-F5 carries the reaction budget).

**Example:** `P = (400, 300)`, `R_SNAP_PX² = 5184`. A seat standing on `P` that starts walking east at 2 px/tick has `dx = 70` after 35 ticks (`4900 < 5184`, hit) and `dx = 72` after 36 ticks (`5184 < 5184` is false, clear). The strict disc is why 36 ticks suffice. A seat 60 px west of `P` but in the next room (different `room_at`) is not hit. Two seats at `dx = 10` and `dx = 50` in the same room are each hit once: two `apply_suit_damage` calls, one per seat. A DOWNED seat inside the disc is skipped.

**Registry candidate:** `strike_hit_test`.

### HE-F3 Integrity after k strikes (the `STRIKE_BP` two-bound)

The integrity-after-k-strikes formula is defined as:

`I_k = max(0, I_0 − k × STRIKE_BP);   k_fail(I) = floor((I − SUIT_HP_FAIL) / STRIKE_BP) + 1;   bounds: SUIT_HP_FULL − SUIT_HP_OK < STRIKE_BP  and  2 × STRIKE_BP < SUIT_HP_FULL − SUIT_HP_FAIL`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `I_0`, `I` | int bp | 0..`SUIT_HP_FULL` | Suit (Horror never reads it) | Suit integrity before the first strike |
| `STRIKE_BP` ★ | int bp | 3001..3749, [PH] 3200 | Horror (Suit-scale amount) | One strike's integrity cost; the open interval is derived from the two bounds |
| `SUIT_HP_FULL` / `SUIT_HP_OK` / `SUIT_LEAK_HP` / `SUIT_HP_FAIL` | int bp | 10000 / 7000 / 5000 / 2500 (CITED) | Suit, registry | Rung thresholds |
| `SUIT_PATCH_CAP_BP` | int bp | 6000 default, range 5100..6900 (CITED) | Suit, registry | Cap of `patch_integrity`; no passive regeneration exists |

**Output Range:** `I_k` in 0..10000; `k_fail(10000) = 3` for **every** `STRIKE_BP` in 3001..3749 (`7500/3001 = 2.499`, `7500/3749 = 2.0005`, so the floor plus one is 3 across the whole range). **`STRIKE_BP` is not a lethality dial inside its registered range**; the dials are attempts per breach, inattention (HE-F16's `p`), breaches per session and repair availability. The first bound takes strike one below `SUIT_HP_OK` (STRAINED, inert by Suit's design; the strike itself is the witness, HE-15); the second means two strikes never kill a full suit.

**Example:** `STRIKE_BP` 3200 from 10000: `I_1 = 6800` (below `SUIT_HP_OK`, STRAINED, inert), `I_2 = 3600` (below `SUIT_LEAK_HP`, CRITICAL, stagger, walk-only), `I_3 = 400` (below `SUIT_HP_FAIL`, FAILED → DOWNED → `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800 → DEAD, Suit's ladder and Save/Load's restore). *One-way ratchet:* a worn suit dies sooner. `k_fail(7000) = floor(4500/3200) + 1 = 2`; a patched suit starts from at most `SUIT_PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000, `k_fail(6000) = floor(3500/3200) + 1 = 2` (first strike → 2800, CRITICAL; second → 0, FAILED). The three strikes need not occur in one breach, but after the first landed strike and any patch, two strikes kill. Edge of the range: `STRIKE_BP` 3749 leaves 2502 after two strikes, one point above `SUIT_HP_FAIL` (survives); 3750 would not (outside the interval).

**Registry candidate:** `strike_integrity_bounds`.

### HE-F4 CHILL cumulative bound

The CHILL cumulative bound formula is defined as:

`ΔT_mC(Q) = floor(1000 × Q / (C_TILE × n_tiles));   CHILL_EPOCH_MAX_DJ = CHILL_DEPOSITS_PER_EPOCH_MAX × CHILL_STEP_DJ;   lint: ΔT_mC(CHILL_EPOCH_MAX_DJ) at n_tiles_min ≤ CHILL_EPOCH_MAX_MC;   runtime: deposit iff T_c − ΔT_mC(CHILL_STEP_DJ) ≥ COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC + CHILL_MARGIN_MC;   CHILL_STEP_DJ ≤ FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q` | int dJ | 0..`CHILL_EPOCH_MAX_DJ` | this formula | Heat removed by one deposit or one epoch of deposits |
| `C_TILE` | int dJ per °C per tile | 3000 | Life-Support Graph, registry (LSG:458, :937; CITED) | Heat capacity per tile |
| `n_tiles` | int | compartment tile count | Layout | Tiles in the deposited compartment |
| `n_tiles_min` | int | smallest authored compartment | Layout, Derelict Authoring *(provisional — not published; 8 assumed in the example)* | Worst case for the lint |
| `T_c` | int mC | −40000..70000+ | Graph `compartment(cid)` | The compartment's current temperature |
| `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` | int mC | −15000, range −25000..−5000 (CITED) | Life-Support Graph, registry | Where cold begins to harm a body |
| `CHILL_MARGIN_MC` | int mC | 2000..10000, [PH] 5000 | Horror | Runtime margin kept above the onset |
| `CHILL_STEP_DJ` ★ | int dJ | 1000..`FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q`, [PH] 3000 | Horror | One deposit; `FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q` 20000 is the Graph's cap (registry, LSG:912, CITED) |
| `CHILL_DEPOSITS_PER_EPOCH_MAX` | int | 1..48, [PH] 24 | Horror | Deposits per `tell_epoch` |
| `CHILL_EPOCH_MAX_MC` | int mC | 2000..8000, [PH] 5000 | Horror | Lint ceiling on the epoch total |

**Output Range:** one deposit in a 20-tile room is 50 mC; one epoch's maximum is 1200 mC (20 tiles), 2000 mC (12 tiles), 3000 mC (8 tiles). A deposit whose runtime check fails, or whose epoch cap is spent, is **not made** (counters `horror_chill_withheld_cold`, `horror_chill_withheld_cap`); the rime mark is **still laid** in those two cases (ring-only: the mark is a true surface record that the web passed, not a thermal claim), and withheld in the third case only, a coolant fitting within `FITTING_CLEARANCE_PX` (HE-31), where neither deposit nor mark is made.

**Example:** `CHILL_EPOCH_MAX_DJ = 24 × 3000 = 72000`. In a 20-tile room `ΔT = 1000 × 72000 / (3000 × 20) = 1200` mC (1.2 °C); the lint at `n_tiles_min` 8 gives 3000 mC ≤ 5000, pass. Runtime: the threshold is `−15000 + 5000 = −10000` mC. A room at −9000 mC deposits (`−9000 − 50 = −9050 ≥ −10000`); a room at −9990 mC withholds (`−10040 < −10000`). *Why this is bookkeeping:* taking a 20 °C, 20-tile room below the Graph's COOL band (under 10 °C) needs `10000 mC × 3000 × 20 / 1000 = 600000` dJ, which is 200 deposits at 3000, and Horror is capped at 24 per epoch; the rime on the floor is a surface mark, and no instrument or body cue disagrees with it (HE-27).

**Registry candidate:** `chill_cumulative_bound`.

### HE-F5 REACH reaction bound

The REACH reaction bound formula is defined as:

`UNFOLD_LO ≥ ceil5( ceil(R_SNAP_PX / V_WALK) + REACT_ALLOW_TICKS_HORROR + 1 );   UNFOLD_LO mod HORROR_RUN_TICKS = 0;   UNFOLD_HI ≥ UNFOLD_LO;   UNFOLD_TICKS = HORROR_RUN_TICKS × rng_int(UNFOLD_LO / 5, UNFOLD_HI / 5)`

The `+ 1` is the kernel's command application at `apply_tick ≥ T + 1` (KRN D.4): a walk command issued on the last reaction tick moves the seat one tick later. `rng_int` is drawn from `rng_derive(world_seed, "horror.tell", counter)` (kernel D.10); the stream is Horror's REACH length only and is **not** a tell length (HE-9).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `R_SNAP_PX` | int px | 72 | HE-F2 | Disc radius |
| `V_WALK` | int px/tick | 2 (CITED) | Player Controller, registry | Escaping speed |
| `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS_HORROR` | int ticks | 30..90, [PH] 54 | Horror (**unsourced**, C-HE-27; never Attention's `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS` 180) | Reaction allowance: the ticks from the limb entering the cone to the walk command; validated by the blind-legibility playtest |
| `UNFOLD_LO`, `UNFOLD_HI` ★ | int ticks | `UNFOLD_LO` 95, `UNFOLD_HI` 180, [PH]; both multiples of 5 | Horror | Telegraph length range |
| `counter` | int | ≥ 0 | block (`"horror.tell"` counter) | Incremented in the same block write as the draw; persisted; restore never rerolls |

**Output Range:** `UNFOLD_TICKS` takes 18 equally likely values, 95, 100, …, 180, mean 137.5 ticks (2.29 s). Bound at the placeholders: `36 + 54 + 1 = 91`, rounded up to 95; **4 ticks of unclaimed slack**. The carrier line is informative: `ceil(72/1) + 54 + 1 = 127` against 95 leaves 40 ticks available for 72 needed.

**Example:** counter 7 draws `rng_int(19, 36) = 22`, so `UNFOLD_TICKS = 110` and `reach_end_T = reach_start_T + 110`. A walker who reacts in 54 ticks is clear with at least 4 ticks to spare at every legal draw. A carrier who does not drop the cargo can walk out only when `UNFOLD_TICKS ≥ 130` (`127` rounded up to the next draw value), which is 11 of the 18 draws (61.1 %); a carrier who drops the cargo inside the reaction budget becomes a walker (the drop's cost is Interaction's, *provisional — Interaction* confirms a free instant drop). C-HE-8 states the carrier case as intended; the first playtest tweak is a smaller `R_SNAP_PX` (48: `24 + 54 + 1 = 79`, carrier `48 + 54 + 1 = 103`).

**Registry candidate:** `strike_walkout_bound`.

### HE-F6 Speed cadence and the escape invariant

The speed cadence and escape invariant formula is defined as:

`acc ← acc + V_MPX;  v_eff = floor(acc / 1000);  acc ← acc − 1000 × v_eff   (once per tick of a run);   V_CREEP_MPX < V_HUNT_MPX < V_WALK × 1000;   ceil(V_MPX / 1000) ≤ 3`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V_MPX` | int milli-px/tick | `V_CREEP_MPX` or `V_HUNT_MPX` | Horror | Speed of the current state: LURK, STALK in a TELL, RETREAT and the arrival walk use `V_CREEP_MPX`; STALK in BREACH uses `V_HUNT_MPX` |
| `V_CREEP_MPX` ★ | int | 1000..1400, [PH] 1000 | Horror | Below 1000 a tick can have `v_eff` 0, and Body resets its sub-pixel residue on a zero-speed tick (BDY-12), so the floor stays 1000 until that is verified |
| `V_HUNT_MPX` ★ | int | `V_CREEP_MPX`+1..1999, [PH] 1500 | Horror | Approach speed in BREACH |
| `V_WALK` | int px/tick | 2 (CITED) | Player Controller | A walking seat, including a CRITICAL walk-only seat |
| `acc` | int | 0..999 | block, per record | Persisted remainder |
| `v_eff` | int px/tick | 1..3 | this formula | The per-tick step passed to Body's `LocomotionStep` with the run's octant |

**Output Range:** `v_eff` in 1..2 at the placeholders; the sweep box of Body's step is sized for 3 (BDY-15). The invariant means a walker gains `V_WALK × 1000 − V_HUNT_MPX = 500` milli-px per tick on a hunter. A **carrier** (`V_CARRY` 1, 1000 milli-px) cannot out-walk `V_HUNT_MPX` (HE-24, C-HE-8).

**Example:** `V_HUNT_MPX` 1500, `acc = 0`: run one steps 1, 2, 1, 2, 1 = 7 px (`acc` 500); run two steps 2, 1, 2, 1, 2 = 8 px; mean 7.5 px per run, 1.5 px/tick, 90 px/s. `V_CREEP_MPX` 1000: always 1, 5 px per run, `acc` stays 0. A walker at 2 px/tick gains 0.5 px/tick on the hunter: 30 px/s.

**Registry candidate:** `horror_speed_invariant`.

### HE-F7 Mark ring eviction, lifetime and ageing

The mark ring eviction, lifetime and ageing formula is defined as:

`insert into a full ring evicts argmin(mark_id) over kind = SMEAR, else argmin(mark_id) over all kinds;   life_left ← life_left − HORROR_RUN_TICKS per run while the mark's section is loaded, removed at life_left ≤ 0;   stage = min(2, floor(3 × (MARK_LIFE_TICKS − life_left) / MARK_LIFE_TICKS));   smear_retention_ticks = (MAX_MARKS − n_other) × MARK_SPACING_PX × 1000 / V_CREEP_MPX;   MARK_LIFE_TICKS ≥ smear_retention_ticks ≥ TRAIL_TARGET_TICKS`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mark_id` | int | ≥ 1, never reused | block counter | Insertion order |
| `kind` | enum | SMEAR, RIME, HAIRLINES, STRUCK | block | Mark kind (HE-27) |
| `MAX_MARKS` | int | 8..48, [PH] 24 | Horror | Ring size |
| `MARK_LIFE_TICKS` | int ticks | 3600..54000, [PH] 18000 | Horror | Lifetime of a mark in a loaded section |
| `MARK_SPACING_PX` | int px | 48..144, [PH] 96 | Horror | One SMEAR per this much travel (two tiles); plus one touch-down SMEAR per REACH start |
| `CHILL_SPACING_PX` | int px | 192..768, [PH] 384 | Horror | One CHILL deposit and rime mark per this much travel, and at each LURK dwell start |
| `n_other` | int | 0..`MAX_MARKS` | derived | Non-smear marks resident: during a creep one rime per `CHILL_SPACING_PX / MARK_SPACING_PX + 1` ring slots, `ceil(24 / 5) = 5`, plus any hairlines and STRUCK marks (one per landed strike) |
| `TRAIL_TARGET_TICKS` | int ticks | 1800..3600, [PH] 1800 | game-designer target (the hidden-entity investigation window), not a runtime value | The trail an investigator arriving up to 30 s late should still find |
| `V_CREEP_MPX` | int | 1000 | HE-F6 | Creep speed |
| `MARKS_PER_RUN_MAX` | int | 1..4, [PH] 2 | Horror | Inserts per run; over the cap, priority STRUCK, then HAIRLINES, then RIME, then SMEAR; a dropped insert is counted `horror_marks_withheld` |

**Output Range:** `stage` in {0, 1, 2}: exactly three frame stages for smear and rime (and two for hairlines), so physical age is readable without a meter (Pillar 1) and no continuous value is drawn. `smear_retention_ticks` is 1824 at the placeholders. The ring, not the lifetime, is the binding bound on a creeping horror; the lifetime exists so a frozen loaded body does not keep stale marks forever.

**Example:** the ring is full at 24 with three hairline marks (ids 4, 5, 6), five rime marks and sixteen smears. A new smear (id 31) evicts the oldest smear (id 1) and never hairline 4; the strike's evidence outlives a long creep. A ring that holds no smear at all (24 hairline and rime marks) evicts the lowest `mark_id` of any kind on insert. Retention: `(24 − 5) × 96 × 1000 / 1000 = 1824` ticks (30.4 s) of smear trail at 1 px/tick, which meets `TRAIL_TARGET_TICKS` 1800 only while at most 5 non-smear marks are resident; every extra hairline mark costs 96 ticks of smear history, so a breach that lays 3 hairline marks leaves 1536 ticks (25.6 s), below the target (Open Question: raise `MAX_MARKS` after a cost spike). Ageing: a mark with `life_left` 12000 has age 6000 and `stage = floor(3 × 6000 / 18000) = 1`; at `life_left` 5999 the age is 12001 and the stage is 2.

**Registry candidate:** `mark_ring_retention`.

### HE-F8 Target selection from `lux_pm`

The target selection formula is defined as:

`candidates = { s : living(s) ∧ ¬DOWNED(s) ∧ ¬IN_POD(s) ∧ actor_section(s) ∈ body_scope ∧ lit(s) };   target = argmax over candidates of (lux_pm(s), −seat_id);   candidates = ∅ ⇒ target = ANCHOR (HE-16 (3))`

`body_scope` is `locus_body` in TELL, BREACH and SUBSIDING and the record's own body in CALM. Nothing is stored between runs.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `lux_pm(s)` | int permille | 0..1000 | Emitter `lux(ref)` | The seat's illumination; the owner stands in their own light (EM-21), so a seat with its lamp on reads 1000 |
| `lit(s)` | bool | — | Emitter (hysteresis on `LIT_ON_PM` 150, CITED) | The Emitter's flag, not recomputed by Horror |
| `seat_id` | int | ≥ 0 | Body | Tie-break: lowest wins |
| `ANCHOR` | position | Layout `anchor_geometry` | HE-16 (3) | The target when no candidate is lit |

**Output Range:** a seat id or ANCHOR; one `lux(ref)` read per candidate per run, zero probes (HE-43).

**Example:** seat A `lux_pm` 1000 (lamp on), seat B 0 (dark): target A. Seat A switches the lamp off: no lit candidate, target ANCHOR on the next run, and nothing remembers A (the target is not persisted). A thrown Chemlight pool reads 700 only for a seat that stands in it; the horror targets whichever *seat* is lit, never the pool (a pool is not a seat, HE-13). Two lit seats at 1000 and 1000: the lower `seat_id` wins; a stateless argmax can alternate as lamps change (C-HE-19, Vertical Slice).

**Registry candidate:** `horror_target_rule`.

### HE-F9 Ingress timing and entry choice

The ingress timing and entry choice formula is defined as:

`transit_until_T = tell_enter_T + INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS;   arrive_T = min{ T ≥ transit_until_T : T mod 5 = 0 ∧ legal_entry(T) };   stalk_visible_min = TELL_MIN_TICKS − INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS;   D_entry_max = LURK_STANDOFF_PX + floor(stalk_visible_min × V_CREEP_MPX / 1000);   INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS ≤ TELL_MIN_TICKS − STALK_VISIBLE_FLOOR_TICKS`

`legal_entry(T)` selects, among hull-bearing compartments of `locus_body` at hop distance 1 from the room of the highest-`lux_pm` living seat (none lit or present: the locus section's outermost compartment), a hull segment whose room holds no living seat and lies at least `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` from every living seat; ties lowest hop, then lowest room id, then lowest hull-segment id. The entry is chosen at the run at `transit_until_T − INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` and the ingress mark is laid then (re-chosen under E-HE-34 if it becomes illegal); the body arrives at `transit_until_T`, or at the first later run whose re-chosen entry has had its full mark lead.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` ★ | int ticks | 120..360, [PH] 300 | Horror | Earliest ingress after TELL entry |
| `TELL_MIN_TICKS` | int ticks | 600 (CITED, DERIVED from `W_MIN_WARNING_S`) | Attention, registry | Shortest tell |
| `STALK_VISIBLE_FLOOR_TICKS` | int ticks | 120..360, [PH] 240 | Horror | The least visible stalk the design accepts |
| `INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` | int ticks | 30..120, [PH] 60 | Horror | The mark precedes the body so a player watching that wall sees the arrival coming |
| `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` | int px | 144..288, [PH] 192 | Horror | HE-F14 |
| `V_CREEP_MPX` | int milli-px/tick | 1000..1400, [PH] 1000 | Horror | HE-F6 |
| `D_entry_max` | int px | derived 492 | this formula | The farthest an entry may lie from the seat for the stalk to close to the standoff before the shortest tell ends |

**Output Range:** `stalk_visible_min = 300` ticks (5 s): the entity's presence is published for at least 300 ticks before the shortest tell ends (up to 4 ticks later for run alignment); whether the player *sees* it depends on light. An entry beyond `D_entry_max` still arrives inside the tell but reaches the standoff after BREACH entry (the approach then runs at `V_HUNT_MPX`); it is a tuning and authoring signal, never a rule violation.

**Example:** TELL enters at `T = 1200` with a drawn length of 900. `transit_until_T = 1500`; the ingress mark is laid at 1440 and the body registers (E6) at the run at 1500; BREACH begins at 2100, so the stalk is published for 600 ticks. Relation check: `300 ≤ 600 − 240 = 360` (pass); `D_entry_max = 192 + 300 = 492` px, covering a one-hop entry in the 288 to 480 px room sizes of the slice. If a seat walks into the chosen room during the 60 ticks of mark lead, the entry is re-chosen by the same rule; the first hairline stays (it is a true mark of where the web touched).

**Registry candidate:** `horror_ingress_timing`.

### HE-F10 Path expansion bound

The path expansion bound formula is defined as:

`expansions_per_run ≤ PATH_EXPANSIONS_MAX;   PATH_EXPANSIONS_MAX ≥ |rooms(body)|_max;   cache hit ⇒ 0 expansions;   cache key = (goal_room, layout_hash, closed_leaf_hash)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PATH_EXPANSIONS_MAX` | int | 16..256, [PH] 64 | Horror | Node expansions per run on a cache miss |
| `|rooms(body)|_max` | int | 14 in the slice (5 + 4 + 5) | Layout | The largest welded body the slice can form |
| `layout_hash` | hash | — | Layout | Changes on any `layout.state` change (weld, cut) |
| `closed_leaf_hash` | hash | — | Horror (read each run) | Used until Layout states whether `layout_hash()` covers door state (C-HE-13) |

**Output Range:** 0 on a cache hit; at most 14 on a miss in the slice (breadth-first, ties lowest room id). `PATH_EXPANSIONS_MAX` below the room count truncates long routes; the horror then holds its waypoint up to `HOLD_MAX_RUNS` and goes to LURK.

**Example:** a goal on a 14-room body expands at most 14 nodes with at most 6 neighbours each, 84 edge relaxations, well under 64 expansions. A weld invalidates the cache for the welded body only; a door closing changes `closed_leaf_hash`, so the next run recomputes once.

**Registry candidate:** `path_expansion_bound`.

### HE-F11 L3 disturbance strength and duration bound

The L3 disturbance strength and duration formula is defined as:

`S_hi = 1000 − MOD_FLOOR_PM;   strength_pm = L3_STRENGTH_LO_PM + (h2 mod (S_hi − L3_STRENGTH_LO_PM + 1));   dur_ticks = L3_DUR_LO + (h3 mod (L3_DUR_HI − L3_DUR_LO + 1));   start iff h1 mod 1000 < L3_START_PM ∧ T ≥ last_end_T + L3_MIN_GAP_TICKS;   L3_STRENGTH_LO_PM ≤ S_hi`

`h1`, `h2`, `h3` are pure integer hashes of `(emitter_id, run_n, HE_HASH_SEED)` with distinct salts, never `rng_derive` (HE-42). The result enters Lighting's registered `modifier_stack` as the L3 factor, whose floor `MOD_FLOOR_PM` already clamps the chain; Horror never restates or applies that clamp.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MOD_FLOOR_PM` | int permille | 600, range 400..800 [PH] (CITED) | 2D Lighting & Occlusion, registry | The deepest any modifier chain may dim a lamp |
| `S_hi` | int permille | DERIVED (400 at the placeholder; the formula is cited, never the bare number) | this formula | Strength ceiling |
| `L3_STRENGTH_LO_PM` ★ | int permille | 50..`S_hi`, [PH] 150 | Horror | Shallowest dip |
| `L3_DUR_LO` / `L3_DUR_HI` ★ | int ticks | [PH] 18 / 30 | Horror (Lighting's gutter grammar, LO-19, 0.3 to 0.5 s) | Dip length |
| `L3_START_PM` | int permille | 10..200, [PH] 40 | Horror | Per eligible lamp per run start probability |
| `L3_MIN_GAP_TICKS` ★ | int ticks | ≥ 60, [PH] 120 | Horror (ruled as `L3_MIN_GAP_FRAMES`, renamed because a SIM block cannot count frames, H9) | Minimum quiet time per lamp |
| `L3_MAX_ACTIVE` / `L3_MAX_TRACKED` | int | [PH] 4 / 16 | Horror | Simultaneous dips; size of the `last_end_T` map |

**Output Range:** `strength_pm` in `[L3_STRENGTH_LO_PM, S_hi]`; `dur_ticks` in `[18, 30]`; duty per lamp at most `30 / (30 + 120)` = 20 %. Never below `MOD_FLOOR_PM` after the chain.

**Example:** at the 600 placeholder `S_hi = 400`, so `strength_pm = 150 + (h2 mod 251)`; a hash of 977 gives `977 mod 251 = 224` and `strength_pm = 374`; `dur_ticks = 18 + (h3 mod 13)`. Expected cycle per lamp near the horror: duration about 24, minimum gap 120, expected wait `1000 / 40 = 25` runs (125 ticks), about 269 ticks (4.5 s), duty about 9 %. The EMERGENCY subkind (`fx_cryo_lamp`), any BEAM, BEACON, FAULT_GLOW and any DEPLOYABLE never start a disturbance.

**Registry candidate:** `l3_strength_bound`.

### HE-F12 Subject count

The subject count formula is defined as:

`S_h = count(records not in TRANSIT);   S_h ≤ HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX;   HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX + A_max ≤ MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS;   E6 returns true for every first registration`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `S_h` | int | 0..2 | block | Registered Horror subjects |
| `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` ★ | int | 1..12, [PH] 2 | Horror (Interaction's `H` and Derelict Authoring's `HORRORS` quote this, C-HE-4 closed 2026-10-27) | One active plus one transient during ingress |
| `A_max` | int | 4 | Body (`MAX_ACTORS` 8 headroom; seats at MVP) | Actors counted as moving subjects |
| `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS` | int | 16 (CITED) | Emitter, registry | Moving-subject ceiling |

**Output Range:** `S_h` in 0..2; a false return from E6 on a first registration is a **budget defect** (a duplicate E6 on an already-registered ref returns false by design, EM:152, and is not) (the subject reads dark) and a failing criterion, never a gameplay state.

**Example:** at ingress the old record is unregistered (E7) before the new arrival registers (E6), so `S_h` stays 1; the transient 2 is a ceiling the order never reaches at MVP. `2 + 4 = 6 ≤ 16`.

**Registry candidate:** `horror_subject_count`.

### HE-F13 Tell composition timeline

The tell composition timeline formula is defined as:

`t_arrive = ceil5( (LURK_STANDOFF_PX − REACH_RANGE_PX) × 1000 / V_HUNT_MPX );   first_strike_T = tell_len + t_arrive + UNFOLD_TICKS;   layered_warning_min = TELL_MIN_TICKS + t_arrive + UNFOLD_LO ≥ W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE`

`T` is counted from TELL entry. `tell_len` is Attention's draw in `[TELL_MIN_TICKS, TELL_MAX_TICKS]`; the audit for any harm event is the registered `tell_window_contract` (HE-8), not this timeline.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tell_len` | int ticks | 600..1800 (CITED) | Attention, registry (`TELL_MIN_TICKS`, `TELL_MAX_TICKS`) | Length of the tell; Horror never shortens it |
| `t_arrive` | int ticks | 65 at the placeholders | this formula | BREACH entry to the first REACH start for a horror standing at the standoff |
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | int s | 10 (CITED) | Life-Support Graph, registry | The Graph's minimum warning |
| `TICK_RATE` | int Hz | 60 (CITED) | Kernel, registry | Tick rate |
| `UNFOLD_TICKS` | int ticks | 95..180 | HE-F5 | The first strike's telegraph |
| `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` | int px | 144..288, [PH] 192 | Horror (HE-F14) | The standoff the horror holds before the BREACH approach |
| `REACH_RANGE_PX` | int px | 73..159, [PH] 96 | Horror (HE-F14) | Distance at which a REACH may begin |
| `V_HUNT_MPX` | int milli-px/tick | `V_CREEP_MPX`+1..1999, [PH] 1500 | Horror (HE-F6) | Approach speed in BREACH |

**Output Range:** the first strike lands no earlier than `600 + 65 + 95 = 760` ticks (12.7 s) after TELL entry and no later than `1800 + 65 + 180 = 2045` ticks (34.1 s) for run-aligned edges; for an arbitrary TELL entry add up to `HORROR_RUN_TICKS − 1` = 4 ticks of run alignment (E-HE-17), so the latest first strike is 2049. The 760 lower bound is unaffected. `layered_warning_min = 760 ≥ 10 × 60 = 600`. Attention's L0 alone already meets the Graph's requirement (`TELL_MIN_TICKS` 600 = `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE`); L1 and L2 are additive.

**Example:** `tell_len = 900`, the horror resident in another body. `t = 0`: TELL begins; the record goes TRANSIT (egress mark at the old site). `t = 240`: ingress mark on the hull seam. `t = 300`: the body registers and STALKs at `V_CREEP_MPX`, held at 192 px. `t = 900`: BREACH, `V_HUNT_MPX`. `t = 965`: first REACH starts, the limb enters the cone. With `UNFOLD_TICKS` 110 (HE-F5 example), `t = 1075`: the snap, `I_1 = 6800`. The player has had 900 ticks of L0, 600 ticks of L1 and 110 ticks of L2 before the first strike. A suit already at `SUIT_HP_OK` (7000) dies at the second strike, no earlier than `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + UNFOLD_LO` = 395 ticks after the first (HE-F15).

**Registry candidate:** `horror_tell_composition`.

### HE-F14 REACH range ordering chain

The REACH range ordering formula is defined as:

`R_SNAP_PX < REACH_RANGE_PX < LURK_STANDOFF_PX;   REACH_RANGE_PX ≤ 3 × H_crew`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `R_SNAP_PX` | int px | 72 | HE-F2 | Strike disc radius |
| `REACH_RANGE_PX` ★ | int px | 73..159, [PH] 96 | Horror | Distance from the horror's foot point to P at which a REACH may begin; also the limb length used by the rig |
| `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` ★ | int px | 144..288, [PH] 192 | Horror | The nearest the horror comes to a living seat outside a BREACH approach |
| `H_crew` | int px | 53 | Art bible (ART:1028, 1042: crewmate figure 53 px; horror height band 1× to 3× = 53 to 159 px) | Crewmate height |

**Output Range:** `REACH_RANGE_PX` in 73..159 (the art cap binds below the standoff). The first draft's relation `REACH_RANGE_PX + core_radius ≤ field_h_min / 2` fails once the camera's look-ahead `L_MAX × s` and follow lag (PIPE:157) are counted, so only the tip at the seat's foot is guaranteed on screen at `k_auto + 2` (CP-3); body and limb visibility at `k = 1`, `k_auto` and `k_auto + 2` is a manual screenshot criterion, not a derived bound.

**Example:** `72 < 96 < 192` (pass) and `96 ≤ 3 × 53 = 159` (pass). `REACH_RANGE_PX` 144 would also satisfy both, but leaves only 48 px between the REACH start and the standoff and was withdrawn (C-HE-23).

**Registry candidate:** `reach_range_chain`.

### HE-F15 Cycle period of a seat that does not move

The cycle period formula is defined as:

`P = UNFOLD_TICKS + STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + t_back;   t_back = ceil5( (D_wp − REACH_RANGE_PX) × 1000 / V_HUNT_MPX );   t_out = ceil( (D_wp − REACH_RANGE_PX) × 1000 / V_CREEP_MPX ) ≤ STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS;   STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS > STAGGER_TICKS;   min_gap = STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + UNFOLD_LO`

After a strike the horror RETREATs to the nearest waypoint at least `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` from every living seat (HE-17); at `cooldown_until_T` it turns to STALK and walks back at `V_HUNT_MPX` until it is within `REACH_RANGE_PX`; the next REACH may begin only then (HE-14 (e) and (d)).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` ★ | int ticks | 180..900 and `> STAGGER_TICKS`, [PH] 300 | Horror; `STAGGER_TICKS` 30 (CITED, Player Controller) | The pause after a strike; also the floor under a RUPTURE's preceding scrape (L4) |
| `UNFOLD_TICKS` | int ticks | 95..180 | HE-F5 | The telegraph that opens each cycle |
| `REACH_RANGE_PX` | int px | 73..159, [PH] 96 | Horror (HE-F14) | Distance at which the next REACH may begin |
| `V_HUNT_MPX` | int milli-px/tick | `V_CREEP_MPX`+1..1999, [PH] 1500 | Horror (HE-F6) | The return speed in `t_back` |
| `V_CREEP_MPX` | int milli-px/tick | 1000..1400, [PH] 1000 | Horror (HE-F6) | The retreat speed in `t_out` |
| `D_wp` | int px | ≥ `LURK_STANDOFF_PX`, nominal 192 | Layout `anchor_geometry` | Distance from the seat to the retreat waypoint |
| `t_back` | int ticks | 65 at the nominal waypoint | this formula | Return trip, rounded up to the run grid |
| `t_out` | int ticks | 96 at the nominal waypoint | this formula | Retreat trip; the lint asserts it finishes inside the cooldown so the retreat reads as a retreat, not a hover |
| `min_gap` | int ticks | 395 | this formula | The cooldown-gated lower bound between two strike resolutions when no return trip is needed (a REACH that fizzled leaves the cooldown unconsumed) |

**Output Range:** `P` in 460..545 ticks at the nominal waypoint (7.7 to 9.1 s), mean 502.5 (8.4 s). A retreat waypoint farther than nominal lengthens `P` by `(D_wp − 192) × 1000 / V_HUNT_MPX`; the cooldown below 180 turns the peak into a flat screech and above 600 into a lull (game-designer bounds).

**Example:** strike at `T = 4000`: `cooldown_until_T = 4300`; the horror reached its waypoint at about `T = 4096` (96 ticks out at 1 px/tick), waited, turned at 4300, was back within 96 px at 4365 (64 ticks, run grid), and the next REACH starts at 4365; with `UNFOLD_TICKS` 110 the next strike resolves at 4475, a period of 475 ticks (7.9 s). A single-seat player therefore gets a breathing gap of at least 365 ticks (6.1 s) between a snap and the next limb.

**Registry candidate:** `horror_cycle_period`.

### HE-F16 Stationary-seat lethality and the session ratchet

The stationary-seat lethality formula is defined as:

`s_1 = t_arrive;   s_{k+1} = s_k + U_k + STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + t_back;   T_3 = t_arrive + U_1 + U_2 + U_3 + 2 × (STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + t_back);   third REACH starts inside a breach of length B iff s_3 < B;   P_death(λ) = 1 − e^{−λ} × (1 + λ + λ²/2),  λ = E_s × n × p`

The first four terms are integer simulation arithmetic; `P_death` is design analysis (real arithmetic, never hashed): the probability of at least three landed strikes in a session when strikes arrive as a Poisson process (no passive integrity regeneration exists, so hits accumulate until `patch_integrity`, HE-F3).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `U_k` | int ticks | 95..180 (18 values) | HE-F5 | The k-th telegraph length |
| `t_arrive`, `t_back` | int ticks | 65 each | HE-F13, HE-F15 | First approach and per-cycle return |
| `B` | int ticks | `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` 900 .. `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` 3600 (CITED) | Attention, registry | BREACH length; a REACH may begin only while the phase is BREACH and then resolves into SUBSIDING if the predicate holds (HE-17) |
| `E_s` | real | 3..8 *(provisional — Attention's breaches per session is undesigned as a rate)* | Attention | Told breaches per session with the seat in the locus body |
| `n` | real | 0..9 | HE-F15 | REACH attempts that reach the seat per breach |
| `p` | real | 0..1 | playtest | Per-attempt probability that the seat is struck (inattention, late reaction, cornered, carrying); **this is the lethality dial** |
| `λ` | real | ≥ 0 | derived | Expected landed strikes per session |

**Output Range:** `T_3` in 1080..1335 ticks after BREACH entry (18.0 to 22.3 s), mean 1207.5 (20.1 s). The earliest possible third REACH start is `s_3 = 65 + 95 + 95 + 730 = 985`, so a stationary seat **cannot be killed in a minimum-length breach** (`B = 900`: two starts, rung CRITICAL, stagger, a seat that then moves lives); it can in any breach of at least 986 ticks and certainly (probability 1) in any breach of at least 1156 ticks. REACH starts for a stationary seat: 2 at `B = 900`, 4 at `B = 1800`, 7 or 8 at `B = 3600`.

**Example:** `P(s_3 < B)` over the 18 × 18 equiprobable draw pairs: 0 at 900, 0.3 % at 990, 1.9 % at 1000, 28.1 % at 1050, 75.9 % at 1100, 99.7 % at 1155, 100 % at 1156. Session ratchet: `λ = 0.5` gives 1.4 %, `λ = 1.0` gives 8.0 % (the median target), `λ = 1.5` gives 19.1 %, `λ = 2.4` gives 43.0 %, `λ = 3.0` gives 57.7 %, `λ = 6.0` gives 93.8 %. With `E_s = 4` and `n = 6`, `p = 5 %` gives `λ = 1.2` and about 12 %; `p = 10 %` gives `λ = 2.4` and 43 %. The whole "death is a bitch" dial is the per-attempt miss rate `p`, set by telegraph legibility (HE-14), not by any number in the knob table.

**Registry candidate:** `stationary_seat_lethality` (the integer part only).

### HE-F17 Hull act ladder gate

The hull act ladder formula is defined as:

`rupture_ok = gouged_this_epoch(seg) ∧ T ≥ gouge_T(seg) + STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS ∧ ruptures_epoch < RUPTURES_PER_EPOCH_MAX ∧ exogenous_permitted(section(seg));   gouge_ok = gouges_epoch < GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX ∧ exogenous_permitted(section(seg));   deposit(STRIKE at P within HULL_CONTACT_PX of seg) = RUPTURE if rupture_ok, else GOUGE if gouge_ok, else none`

One hull deposit kind per STRIKE: the RUPTURE act is a later act than the GOUGE act it requires (HE-16 (2); *orchestrator ruling: HE-16 is silent on a strike where both are eligible, and one deposit per act keeps each act preceded by its own REACH*). The anchor REACH (no lit seat) may deposit one GOUGE only (`ANCHOR_REACH_GOUGES` 1), never RUPTURE. The kind is hull only (lint `GOUGE_NON_HULL`).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gouges_epoch`, `ruptures_epoch` | int | 0..`GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX`, 0..`RUPTURES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` | block (reset at TELL entry) | Hull acts this `tell_epoch` |
| `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` | int | 0..6, [PH] 3 | Horror | Hull GOUGE ceiling per epoch |
| `RUPTURES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` | int | 0..1, [PH] 1 | Horror | A RUPTURE needs a Graph-told clock afterwards (decompression, `RUPTURE_HP` 3600, registry, LSG:102) |
| `HULL_CONTACT_PX` | int px | 12..48, [PH] 24 | Horror | P within this of a hull-segment face |
| `gouge_T(seg)` | int tick | — | block | When this epoch's GOUGE landed on the segment |
| `magnitude` | int bp | ≤ `FOOTPRINT_MAX_GOUGE_BP` 2500 (registry, LSG:914, CITED) | Graph | M17 magnitude |

**Output Range:** per epoch at most 3 GOUGE and 1 RUPTURE, hence at most 4 hull-deposit M17 calls from strikes (plus the anchor GOUGE, which counts against `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX`). The L4 gate `T ≥ gouge_T + STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` always holds for the *next* strike because consecutive strikes are at least `min_gap` = 395 apart (HE-F15); it exists to forbid a same-act GOUGE-plus-RUPTURE and to keep the scrape ahead of the tear.

**Example:** a lit seat stands beside a hull face. Strike one at `T = 1075`: P within 24 px of segment S, no GOUGE yet: `deposit = GOUGE` (`gouges_epoch = 1`, `gouge_T = 1075`), radial hairlines laid. Strike two at `T = 1550` on S: `rupture_ok` (gouged, `1550 ≥ 1075 + 300`, `ruptures_epoch 0 < 1`): `deposit = RUPTURE`. Strike three on S: `ruptures_epoch = 1`, so `GOUGE` (if under 3). A GOUGE sets the segment `fatigued`, an ENDOGENOUS creep that outlives the breach and sits under the Graph's own `W_MIN_WARNING_S` gate (HE-16 (4)); Horror neither alters nor shortens it.

**Registry candidate:** `hull_act_ladder`.

### HE-F18 Presentation interpolation alpha (render side only)

The presentation interpolation alpha formula is defined as:

`alpha5 = ((T_now − T_run) + tick_alpha) / HORROR_RUN_TICKS;   draw_pos = pos_prev + (pos_curr − pos_prev) × alpha5`

This is presentation arithmetic (real numbers, never hashed, never in a SIM block); the rig *pose* is stepped once per run and never tweened (PIPE row 14). It is owed to the Pipeline because the kernel's `tick_alpha` spans one tick, not five (C-HE-14).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T_now`, `T_run` | int tick | — | Kernel | The presented tick and the tick of the most recent Horror run |
| `tick_alpha` | real | [0, 1] | Kernel | Fraction within the current tick |
| `pos_prev`, `pos_curr` | int px | — | `horror.view` | Positions at the previous and the latest run |

**Output Range:** `alpha5` in [0, 1]. **Example:** two ticks after the run with `tick_alpha` 0.5: `alpha5 = (2 + 0.5) / 5 = 0.5`, the draw position is the midpoint. A rig pose change on any tick that is not a multiple of 5 fails the cadence criterion.

**Registry candidate:** none (render-side; owed to the Pipeline).

## Edge Cases

Each case states the exact outcome and the rule it follows. Ids are `E-HE-n`. "Fizzle" always means the HE-17 outcome: RETREAT with no harm, no hairlines, no GOUGE or RUPTURE and no cooldown consumed, the touch-down smear stays, the `"horror.tell"` draw stays consumed. Where a case settles something the Detailed Design left silent, it is marked *(orchestrator ruling)* and is repeated in the review questions. Cases that depend on an undesigned neighbour are marked *(provisional — <dep>)*.

#### Zero, maximum and out-of-range values

- **If a seat stands at exactly `R_SNAP_PX` (72 px) from P at resolution (E-HE-1)**: it is not hit. The disc is strict, `dx² + dy² < R_SNAP_PX²` with integers (`5184 < 5184` is false). The walk-out arithmetic of HE-F5 depends on the strictness, and an integer comparison keeps the outcome identical on replay.
- **If a seat that is already CRITICAL (integrity 3600) is struck (E-HE-2)**: `I = max(0, 3600 − 3200) = 400`, below `SUIT_HP_FAIL`, so FAILED → DOWNED → `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` → DEAD on Suit's ladder. The horror enters RETREAT after the hold. With no living, non-DOWNED candidate left, the target is the anchor (HE-13, HE-F8). At `cooldown_until_T` in BREACH it STALKs toward the anchor and runs anchor REACHes (hairlines and at most one GOUGE under `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX`, never RUPTURE or `apply_suit_damage`, HE-16 (3)) until SUBSIDING sends it to RETREAT. It never LURKs in BREACH (phase view). The ladder and the death are Suit's, the restore is Save/Load's (HE-18).
- **If `apply_suit_damage` would take integrity below 0 (a patched suit at 2800 struck again) (E-HE-3)**: integrity clamps at 0 (`I_k = max(0, …)`, HE-F3); the mutator is Class B, clamp-never-reject, so Horror never sees a rejection and has no error path to write.
- **If the type row has `UNFOLD_LO` or `UNFOLD_HI` that is not a multiple of 5, `UNFOLD_HI < UNFOLD_LO`, or `UNFOLD_LO` below the HE-F5 bound (E-HE-4)**: the data load fails with a lint error and the type row is refused; nothing rounds silently. A silent round-up would bias every telegraph by 1 to 4 ticks at the exact bound that sizes the walk-out.
- **If `STRIKE_BP` is outside 3001..3749 or `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` is at or below `STAGGER_TICKS` (E-HE-5)**: the data load fails the registered two-bound (HE-F3) or the cooldown invariant (HE-F15). A value outside the interval changes the hits-to-FAILED count, which is Suit's rung design, not Horror's to retune.
- **If a seat walks into the horror's mouth during a BREACH and the lit seat is already within `REACH_RANGE_PX` at the run the cooldown is clear (E-HE-6)**: the REACH begins on that run (conditions (a) to (e) of HE-14 hold); the telegraph is still at least `UNFOLD_LO` ticks and P is the seat's foot point at lock. A close approach shortens the horror's walk, never its warning.
- **If no waypoint is at least `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` from every living seat (a small body, seats on all sides) (E-HE-7)**: the horror holds its position and does nothing (the cornered rule of HE-11); it deposits no harm and lays no marks while still. A cornered horror is a stationary hazard, never a pursuer.
- **If `MAX_MARKS` is reached with no SMEAR resident (E-HE-8)**: an insert evicts the lowest `mark_id` of any kind (HE-F7); a ring of 24 hairline and rime marks can therefore lose a hairline to a new smear, but only when the ring holds no smear at all, which a creeping horror never produces.
- **If more than `MARKS_PER_RUN_MAX` marks would be inserted in one run (E-HE-9)**: the first `MARKS_PER_RUN_MAX` are inserted in the order STRUCK, HAIRLINES, RIME, SMEAR and the rest are dropped and counted `horror_marks_withheld`. A run can legitimately want three (an ingress with smear, rime and hairlines); the cap bounds the dirty keys per tick (`MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256).

#### Simultaneous rules

- **If the predicate flips false between REACH begin and REACH end (the seat pod-hops, the breach is cleared early, the phase returns to CALM) (E-HE-10)**: the REACH fizzles at its last tick. The tip rested on P for the whole telegraph, the touch-down smear is a true mark and stays, and the cooldown is not consumed, so a fizzle cannot starve the next legitimate REACH (HE-17).
- **If `reach_end_T` falls in SUBSIDING and the seat's section is still in `locus_body` (E-HE-11)**: the REACH resolves; the predicate holds in SUBSIDING for sections in `locus_body`, so `tell_window_contract` passes; the state then goes RETREAT. With `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH` 1 it fizzles instead (the merciful variant, off by default; C-HE-10).
- **If a STRIKE is eligible for both GOUGE and RUPTURE on the same segment (E-HE-12)**: one hull deposit per STRIKE; RUPTURE when `rupture_ok`, else GOUGE (HE-F17). The scrape (GOUGE) is always a separate, earlier, told act than the tear *(orchestrator ruling: HE-16 is silent on the tie)*.
- **If one run wants a RUPTURE, a GOUGE and a CHILL while the window already holds 6 accepted calls (E-HE-13)**: the priority RUPTURE, GOUGE, CHILL applies within `MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN`, then the window budget of 8 binds; RUPTURE and GOUGE are issued (window total 8), the CHILL is withheld (its rime mark is still laid) and `horror_footprints_withheld` increments. Harm outranks bookkeeping.
- **If a weld or cut changes the layout on the same tick a REACH resolves (E-HE-14)**: the locked P stays the stored integer; the same-room test (HE-15) and the closed-leaf test (HE-19) are re-evaluated against the layout as it is at resolution. A seat whose room now differs from P's room is not hit, so a layout change can only spare a seat, never reach one through a new wall.
- **If TELL ends in an abort on the same run a TRANSIT arrival completes (E-HE-15)**: the record registers (E6) and arrives as LURK, not STALK (HE-20); no strike, no mark burst, and the return rule applies `RETURN_DELAY_TICKS` later if `RETURN_HOME` is 1.
- **If a second TELL begins while the record is in RETREAT from the previous breach (E-HE-16)**: RETREAT → LURK → STALK on the new `tell_epoch`; it never skips LURK, and the per-epoch counters (`strikes_epoch`, `gouges_epoch`, `ruptures_epoch`, `chill_epoch_dj`) reset at that TELL entry.
- **If Attention's phase changes on a tick on which `horror.step` does not run (E-HE-17)**: Horror reads the view as committed by Attention's most recent run and acts at its next run, at most 4 ticks later (HE-4). A REACH can therefore begin up to 4 ticks after BREACH entry plus the approach; the delay only ever lengthens the warning.
- **If a seat is STAGGERED during a REACH (E-HE-18)**: the Controller freezes it and ignores input for `STAGGER_TICKS` 30 (PCI:170; STAGGERED is not the CRITICAL walk-only cap). A seat staggered at REACH start whose walk command lands at tick 54 still clears at 91. A seat staggered mid-walk loses the frozen ticks plus a re-command, and clears only on draws long enough to absorb them. Horror does not adapt (no stored state). The stagger is itself Suit's or the Controller's told consequence (another axis reaching CRITICAL, or friendly harm). A strike-two stagger cannot overlap the next REACH because `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` 300 > `STAGGER_TICKS`.

#### Degenerate strategies

- **If a seat closes a door leaf between itself and the horror (E-HE-19)**: the leaf is solid to the horror (`solid_in`), it opens no door, and the straight segment from its foot point to P must not cross a closed leaf (HE-19), so no strike lands through it. Counterplay by design (HE-26); the price is the task and the ship under STRESS.
- **If the highest-`lux_pm` seat is in a room with no open route from the horror (E-HE-20)**: HE-13 still names that seat the target. The goal room is chosen over the full adjacency (closed leaves counted for distance), the movement uses the open-leaf graph, and the horror stops in the last reachable room at the closed leaf and begins no REACH (the REACH distance and segment tests fail). It does not switch to a reachable but dimmer seat, because that would be a stored preference *(orchestrator ruling: HE-25 is silent on an unreachable target)*. A sealed seat is immune to the body; the Graph's own clocks and Attention's STRESS keep running. The share of told breaches with zero harm to a seat that stayed in the locus body is a playtest metric (30 to 60 % is the band; above 60 %, hull and ship consequences rise before any harm number does).
- **If the seat goes dark and stands still (E-HE-21)**: the target is the anchor on the next run (HE-13). The anchor REACH lays hairlines and, by default, one hull GOUGE under `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` (HE-16 (3)); it never calls `apply_suit_damage` and never issues RUPTURE. Going dark spares the body and costs the ship, and every lit-only channelled verb is unavailable for the breach and the subside (about `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` + `SUBSIDE_TICKS` = 2100 ticks, 35 s). The anchor must lie within `HULL_CONTACT_PX` of a hull face for the GOUGE; if it does not, hairlines only.
- **If a dark seat stands within `R_SNAP_PX` of the anchor point at an anchor REACH's resolution (E-HE-22)**: no `apply_suit_damage` (an anchor REACH never harms a body, HE-16 (3)); the limb rests beside the seat and the hull takes the GOUGE. The seat has not been hunted, but a limb on its boots is not a safe place; whether a dark seat parked on the anchor should become targetable is an Open Question (the game-designer's `TOUCH_PX` guard was not adopted).
- **If the seat switches its lamp off after a REACH has begun (E-HE-23)**: P is already locked and stored; the strike resolves at P and hits every living seat in the disc, whether or not it was the target (HE-15). The lamp-off only changes the *next* run's target; nothing is remembered. Flapping the lamp to steer the horror costs the player the re-approach and cannot move a locked point.
- **If a seat stands in a thrown Chemlight pool (E-HE-24)**: the pool lights the seat, the Emitter's `lit` flag sets, and the seat is a candidate like any lit seat (HE-F8). The pool is never itself a target (a pool is not a seat). The glowstick works as a *work-light*: light the job, then step out of the pool dark; it does not pull the horror (HE-26).
- **If a seat walks through the horror (no collision) (E-HE-25)**: nothing happens in CALM, TELL or between REACHes; a rooted horror in REACH can be walked through, which may be an escape route out of a dead end. Accepted as a playtest item (Body's BDY-17: actors do not collide; the draw order decides how the overlap reads).
- **If a seat is in a dead-end room whose floor span is below `2 × R_SNAP_PX` (144 px) with a single exit (E-HE-26)**: it can be struck without a legal walk-out. This is an authoring defect, not a Horror rule: Derelict Authoring owes a lint (no dead-end room with floor span below 144 px and a single exit) and DA-29's wording note; Horror does not special-case it.
- **If a seat carries a module and a REACH begins (E-HE-27)**: the carrier needs 72 ticks against 40 available and is caught unless it drops the cargo within the reaction budget or `UNFOLD_TICKS ≥ 130` (11 of 18 draws). Intended: hauling is where the horror bites (HE-24, C-HE-8); the first playtest tweak is a smaller `R_SNAP_PX`.
- **If a seat uses a tool on the horror, including the data-dormant suppressant (E-HE-28)**: the verb fails `INVALID_TARGET` (IA-39); `tool_suppressant` has no effect at MVP (AT-19) and `repel(origin, T)` is never called; the horror's state does not change; nothing is rewarded and nothing drops. Its acceptance criteria read NOT ASSESSED, never PASS (HE-39).

#### Layout, weld and cut

- **If a weld joins two bodies during a TELL or BREACH (E-HE-29)**: `locus_body` is the body in force at the call tick: recorded at TELL entry and replaced only by an AT-14 fallback (LOCUS_FALLBACK); a weld alone does not change it (AT-14); the horror's walkable graph does (recomputed on the `layout.state` change), so it may path across the new join. Strikes still require `exogenous_permitted(actor_section(seat))`, so a seat in a section outside `locus_body` is never hit.
- **If a cut removes the section the record stands in from its body (E-HE-30)**: `body_id` is re-derived from `body_of(section_id)`; if the section is gone the record goes TRANSIT with an egress mark and re-enters its home body by the HE-20 rule. Horror has no cut verb and never interrupts a cut (MSA-22) *(provisional — Modular Ship Assembly designed in this run)*.
- **If a Layout read returns NONE or UNKNOWN mid-path (E-HE-31)**: the record holds its last waypoint for at most `HOLD_MAX_RUNS` 12, then goes LURK. UNKNOWN is never read as "unlit" or as "open" (HE-25). A REACH already in flight is not cancelled by a Layout change; the locked point is a stored integer.
- **If a door closes on the cached path (E-HE-32)**: the edge is excluded, the cache is recomputed once (`closed_leaf_hash` changed, C-HE-13), and if the step reports no movement for `BLOCKED_RUNS_MAX` consecutive runs the cached hop is discarded and the path recomputed once more.
- **If no legal ingress entry exists (E-HE-33)**: the record stays in TRANSIT and retries every run; the diagnostic `horror_ingress_pending` is raised. A BREACH entered with ingress pending is a **cry-wolf defect** (the breach has no teeth), an acceptance criterion and an authoring signal, not a rule; the harm gates still require a registered record in the locus body, so nothing is struck.
- **If the Layout chosen entry becomes illegal during the `INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` window (a seat enters that room) (E-HE-34)**: the entry is re-chosen by the same rule; the first hairline mark stays (it is a true mark of where the web touched). The arrival is delayed to the new mark time plus the lead.
- **If a seat stands in an adjacent room within 72 px of P across a one-tile wall (E-HE-35)**: not hit. The disc is 1.5 tiles wide and wider than a wall, so the same-room test (HE-15) is the rule that stops a strike through a wall (Pillar 1: no lie about the world).

#### Save, restore and dormancy

- **If a restore lands INTO REACH and `exogenous_permitted` still holds (E-HE-36)**: the record keeps its stored `locked_pt`, its `"horror.tell"` counter and its rooted state, and the remaining telegraph is exactly `reach_end_T − T_restore`: restore neither shortens nor lengthens it and never rerolls the draw. A save taken inside a REACH is Save/Load's SL-5 deferral case *(provisional — Save/Load: autosave is a soft gate that defers during a told breach)*, so the unfair case (a reload into the last ticks of a telegraph) is not Horror's to repair. Lengthening the telegraph on restore was considered (the game-designer's GF10 floor) and **rejected**: it mutates the simulation at restore time, breaks save-equivalence (G4) and SL-15's idempotent, no-clamp restore, and would make `snapshot(restore(snapshot()))` differ.
- **If a restore lands INTO REACH and the predicate no longer holds (the autosave predates a pod-hop or the breach ended) (E-HE-37)**: RETREAT with no strike, no hairlines and no GOUGE or RUPTURE (HE-41); the touch-down smear already in the ring stays.
- **If a restore lands INTO STRIKE (E-HE-38)**: the strike's `apply_suit_damage` and M17 calls were made on the entry run and are part of Suit's and the Graph's restored state; STRIKE is only the held snap pose and restoring it issues no second hit. `strike_T` and the seats hit are stored, so the hit cue is not replayed.
- **If a restore lands INTO TRANSIT with `transit_until_T` in the past (E-HE-39)**: legal; the record arrives at the next run with a legal entry. A TRANSIT record that also has a registered Emitter subject, or a non-TRANSIT record without a legal section, fails restore as `STATE_INCOHERENT`.
- **If any restored value is out of range (`strikes_epoch` above its cap, a mark ring longer than `MAX_MARKS`, a position outside its section, `acc_mpx` ≥ 1000) (E-HE-40)**: restore fails hard with the matching `HORROR_RESTORE_*` code (`RANGE`, `MARK_RING`, `SECTION_UNKNOWN`) and the Save/Load layer refuses the file; nothing is clamped (SL-15). A clamp would hide a corrupt save behind a plausible world.
- **If the player dies and the game restarts from the newest autosave (E-HE-41)**: `horror.state` restores with the WORLD blocks, including the `"horror.tell"` counter, so the replay draws the same `UNFOLD_TICKS` sequence: **a reload is not a fresh set of draws**, and there is no reroll oracle. The marks are bit-identical to uninterrupted play (G4); what the player learned stays in their head (Pillar 5).
- **If an autosave fires during CALM while a LURK stands at the standoff (E-HE-42)**: no Horror gate is needed; CALM is non-lethal by construction (HE-10), no lethal horror is ever adjacent to a seat in a CALM save, and SL-5 is unchanged. If the standoff rule is ever relaxed, SL-5 must be revisited.
- **If a record's body is unloaded (E-HE-43)**: the record is frozen, its marks do not age, its disturbances do not run and nothing fast-forwards on reload (HE-21, LS-17). A record in TRANSIT whose destination unloads waits.

#### Budgets, registration and determinism

- **If `register_subject` (E6) returns false on a first registration (not a duplicate) (E-HE-44)**: the subject reads dark (`lux` 0, `lit` false); that is a **budget defect**, a failing criterion and a lint, never a gameplay state. The horror still moves and strikes (its harm does not depend on being a subject); the defect is that nothing can light it.
- **If a stub requests more footprints than the window allows (E-HE-45)**: at most `HE_FOOTPRINT_BUDGET` 8 per window are made, at most `MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` 4 per run; the rest are not attempted (a trace is a moment, not a queue) and counted `horror_footprints_withheld`. In nominal play the counter is 0; a nonzero count in a build is a defect.
- **If a mark insert mutates a packed array element without declaring its dirty key (E-HE-46)**: Godot 4.7 packed-array element writes do not call the setter, so the kernel would miss the change and `state_hash` would differ after a restore. Every mutation of the ring, the disturbance list and the record declares explicit dirty keys; a lint and a save-equivalence criterion enforce it.
- **If the same `world_seed` and the same inputs are replayed (E-HE-47)**: the strike lengths, positions, marks, disturbance timings and `state_hash` are identical (HE-42). Randomness is `rng_derive` for the REACH length only; mark seeds, LURK waypoints and L3 timing are pure integer hashes of ids, the run index and `HE_HASH_SEED`.

#### Hallucination and the Perception Filter

- **If the Filter hides a real horror's body from one seat (Vertical Slice class (a), "hidden real entity") (E-HE-48)**: the marks, L3 state, M17 writes, `apply_suit_damage` calls and `horror.view` are bit-identical with and without the hide (HE-32). The limb tip and body are undrawn for that seat, but the touch-down smear at P is a true mark the Filter may not edit, so "a smear appeared under my feet" falsifies the hide at a cost of looking at the floor. The hide collapses when `horror.view` reports a state outside {LURK, RETREAT} *(provisional — Sanity & Hallucination Rules designed in this run)*.
- **If a phantom horror co-occurs with a real one (E-HE-49)**: the phantom has no record, no mark, no footprint, no registration and no strike (HE-32); the real one proceeds on its own clock. A seat that flees the phantom can walk into the real disc; "is the floor marked?" is the check, and TellAudit records nothing for the phantom (AT E-53).
- **If the Filter shows a crewmate as a horror and a seat harms that crewmate (Vertical Slice class (c)) (E-HE-50)**: that is the friendly-harm act under Interaction and Sanity's gate, not Horror's; no Horror state changes. The rig presenter is only the substitute body; a real horror emits nothing and carries no beam, so the true beam remains a perfect discriminator (HE-2).
- **If a seat's own Sanity state makes it see violet fringes or false gauges (E-HE-51)**: Horror's marks, disturbances and positions are TRUTH channel and are not corruptible; the body can be omitted or wholly substituted only by the Filter, and marks never use flesh or violet (HE-31). Horror reads no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block (CR-12).

#### Co-op hooks (Vertical Slice and later)

- **If two lit seats are in the locus body (E-HE-52)**: the higher `lux_pm` wins; a tie goes to the lower seat id; a stateless argmax can alternate as lamps change, and a hysteresis would need a stored value (C-HE-19, an Open Question). A strike hits every living seat in the disc once, in ascending player id, each as its own `apply_suit_damage` call.
- **If a seat is DOWNED by a teammate (friendly harm) or by another cause during a REACH (E-HE-53)**: it is not "living, non-DOWNED" at resolution and is not hit; the REACH completes harmlessly and the record RETREATs. DOWNED and DEAD seats are never candidates (HE-18).
- **If a second record is requested while `HORROR_MAX_MVP` is 1 (E-HE-54)**: the request is refused by the data lint and the boot check; at runtime `S_h ≤ HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` 2 is a ceiling reserved for the transient during ingress. Rosters per ring and more types are Vertical Slice.
- **If a seat is in a different body from `locus_body` (E-HE-55)**: it is not a candidate in TELL, BREACH or SUBSIDING, is never hit, and is not the subject of the predicate (the predicate's argument is Body's `actor_section`, not the Graph's `section_of`). In CALM the candidates are the seats in the record's own body.

## Dependencies

Direction reads from Horror's side. "Depends on" means Horror cannot run without it; "named by" means the neighbour's own GDD already names Horror and its text is quoted in Interactions with Other Systems, so the edge is bidirectional. Hard means a missing neighbour stops the MVP loop; soft means a degraded but correct run. Status is the neighbour's at authoring time (2026-10-26/27): **designed**, **in flight** (a draft on disk), **undesigned-provisional** (no GDD; every assumption is `[PROVISIONAL]` and registered with the owner named).

| System | Direction | Hard / soft | Interface (names fixed elsewhere) | Counterpart text (where the neighbour names Horror) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Command & Simulation Layer | Horror depends on it | Hard | hook `horror.step` (CR-3); block `horror.state` (CR-8); path-(a) mutators (CR-9); `rng_derive` (D.10); `read_view`; CR-12, H5, H9, H10 | KRN:201 row 9; KRN:687 | designed |
| Life-Support Graph | Horror depends on it | Hard | M17 `deposit_trace` (CHILL, hull GOUGE, RUPTURE); `compartment()`; `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` | LSG:268; LSG:863; LSG:144; LSG:1063; LSG:1151 | designed |
| Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query | Horror depends on it | Hard | E6, E7, E8; `lux(ref)`; `lit`; EM:89 (adopted) | EM:330; EM:752; EM:229 | designed in this run |
| Attention System | Horror depends on it | Hard | `attention.view` (`phase`, `tell_epoch`, `tell_outcome`, `locus_section_id`, `exogenous_permitted`); the TellAudit scope; hook `attention.step` | AT:373; AT:975; AT-15 R15; AT:1647 | designed in this run |
| Suit & Equipment | Horror depends on it | Hard (bodily harm) | `apply_suit_damage(player_id, amount_bp, source_ref)` Class B; the EXOGENOUS class on `source_ref`; `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` | SE:475; SE:322; AC-SE-41 | designed in this run |
| Ship Body & Interior Physics | Horror depends on it | Hard | `LocomotionStep`; `actors_on`, `pos_curr`, `actor_section`; hook `body.integrate` (a transitive predecessor through `emitter.place`) | BDY:340; BDY:775; BDY-4 | designed in this run |
| Ship Section & Layout Data Model | Horror depends on it | Hard | `room_adjacency`, `room_at`, `anchor_geometry`, `solid_in`, `body_of`, `sections_of`; hull-segment lookup (owed) | LY:446 | designed in this run (hull lookup provisional) |

| Derelict Authoring & Handmade Kits | DA names Horror | Soft | home body (derelict B); budget quote; DA-29 | DA:685; DA:1235; DA:101 | designed in this run |

| Perception Filter | the Filter depends on Horror (routed) | Soft | `horror.view`; the rig presenter and `leak` input; truth-channel tags | PCI row 16; ART:594 | undesigned-provisional |

**Provisional registrations (reflexion rule: a placeholder from an undesigned neighbour is marked and registered so the later value propagates).** The values Horror takes from or hands to undesigned neighbours, each owed to the registry with the owner named: the `loco_state` read name (Controller), `hull_segments_near` and the `layout_hash` door-state question (Layout, the Graph), `emitters_near` (Emitter, Lighting), the five-tick interpolation alpha (Pipeline), the audio fact names (Audio), the `leak` input (Filter), and the horror-subject budget number (Interaction and Derelict Authoring quote Horror's, not their own). Registry candidates for Horror's own constants are written from the final GDD text, after the final revision, and are not appended here.

**Bidirectionality audit.** Every neighbour in the table above that names Horror (kernel, Graph, Emitter, Attention, Lighting, Failure & Trail, Suit, Interaction, Body, Layout, Save/Load, Derelict Authoring, Pipeline, Controller, Item DB, Assembly, Sanity, Web Map) appears with its counterpart text; the neighbours that do not yet name Horror (Boarding, HUD, Instruments, Debug Overlay, Depth Rings, Codex, Co-op, Chart Table, and the Controller beyond PCI row 16) get rows from this side only, and the missing counterpart is listed as an owed row in Cross-References.

## Tuning Knobs

Every Horror-owned value lives in the per-type data file keyed by `type_id` (HE-5, R26); no behavioural constant lives in code. Every default is a **`[PLACEHOLDER]`** pending playtest, and every row is a candidate for the registry, written from this final text by a later stage. **★ marks a value that a neighbour quotes or that a registered relation binds** (a cross-system fact: another GDD, the registry or an acceptance criterion outside this document reads it); an unstarred knob is Horror-internal. Durations are authored as integer counts of runs (`HORROR_RUN_TICKS` 5) and published in ticks; a duration that is not a multiple of 5 is a data-load failure (HE-4, E-HE-4). "Interacts with" names the formula or neighbour that binds the row; the formula, not the row, is the source of truth for any two-bound.

**The dials that move lethality are not in this table.** `STRIKE_BP` cannot change the hits-to-FAILED count inside its registered interval (HE-F3), so "make it more lethal" is a request to change the per-attempt miss rate `p`, the attempts per breach, the breaches per session (Attention's) or repair availability (Suit's). The first of these is telegraph legibility (HE-14 and the blind-legibility criteria), not a number.

#### Speeds and ranges

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V_CREEP_MPX` ★ | 1000 | milli-px/tick | 1000..1400 | STALK loses its dread and nears `V_HUNT_MPX`; the standoff is hard to hold | below 1000 a tick can have `v_eff` 0 and Body resets sub-pixel residue on a zero tick (BDY-12), so the floor stays 1000 until verified | HE-F6, HE-F7 (trail length), HE-F9, HE-F15 |
| `V_HUNT_MPX` ★ | 1500 | milli-px/tick | `V_CREEP_MPX`+1..1999 | the walker's margin shrinks toward 1 milli-px/tick at 1999 and cry-wolf flips to a chase; at 2000 the invariant `V_HUNT_MPX < V_WALK × 1000` and DA-29 break | the horror rarely reaches a lit idle seat inside a minimum breach; fewer attempts | HE-F6 invariant (`< V_WALK × 1000`), HE-F13, HE-F15, `V_CARRY` |
| `R_SNAP_PX` ★ | 72 | px | 48..96 | corridors fully covered; dodging needs room; carrier arithmetic worsens | one-tile disc, very dodgeable, tile-aligned | HE-F2, HE-F5, Interaction `REACH_PX` 72 (kept equal on purpose), E-HE-26; first playtest tweak is 48 |
| `REACH_RANGE_PX` ★ | 96 | px | 73..159 | limb off-screen at high zoom and the standoff gap shrinks | the horror must be nearly adjacent; feels timid | HE-F14 (`R_SNAP_PX < · < LURK_STANDOFF_PX`, `≤ 3 × H_crew`), HE-F13 |
| `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` ★ | 192 | px | 144..288 | never visible at `k_auto + 2` | the CALM presence reads as a stalker | HE-F14, HE-F9 (`D_entry_max`), L3 radius; Sanity `HIDE_SAFE_R` 288 (SN-22 boot assertion `HIDE_SAFE_R > LURK_STANDOFF_PX`, SANITY_BOOT_HIDE_STANDOFF): at 288 Sanity refuses to boot; retune together |
| `HULL_CONTACT_PX` | 24 | px | 12..48 | every wall-side strike gouges | hull acts almost never accompany strikes | HE-F17, `ANCHOR_REACH_GOUGES` |

#### The REACH telegraph and the strike

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `UNFOLD_LO` ★ | 95 | ticks | per HE-F5 and `>= REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` 90 (Adaptive Audio), multiple of 5 | the telegraph reads as a stall | below the bound: strikes the reaction cannot escape | HE-F5, HE-F15 (`min_gap`), blind-legibility criteria; Adaptive Audio `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` 90 <= `UNFOLD_LO` (registry `reach_resolve_start`) |
| `UNFOLD_HI` ★ | 180 | ticks | `UNFOLD_LO`..270, multiple of 5 | rhythm lost; dread dilutes | draw range collapses; telegraph length predictable | HE-F5, HE-F16 |
| `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS_HORROR` | 54 | ticks | 30..90 | `UNFOLD_LO` ≥ 127 and a slow breach | asserts reactions faster than most players | HE-F5; **never** Attention's `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS` 180 (C-HE-27, unsourced; validated by playtest) |
| `STRIKE_BP` ★ | 3200 | bp | 3001..3749 (derived from the Suit's rungs) | out of range: two strikes kill | out of range: strike one changes no rung | HE-F3; not a lethality dial in range |
| `STRIKE_HOLD_RUNS` | 2 | runs | 1..4 | the snap pose freezes visibly | the snap pose is one frame, unreadable | rig `snap` pose; `strike_T` |
| `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` ★ | 300 | ticks | 180..900, and `> STAGGER_TICKS` 30 | a lull; three hits need minutes | a flat screech; attempts double | HE-F15, HE-F16, `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` |
| `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH` | 0 | flag | 0 or 1 | 1: the merciful variant, an in-flight REACH fizzles in SUBSIDING | 0: commit (an in-flight REACH resolves) | HE-17, C-HE-10, `SUBSIDE_TICKS` |
| `ANCHOR_REACH_GOUGES` ★ | 1 | flag | 0 or 1 | n/a | 0: "go dark and stand still" is a complete answer to a breach | HE-16 (3), HE-F17, `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` (C-HE-24) |
| `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` | 3 | count | 0..6 | the hull is shredded under a slow breach | hull evidence is thin | HE-F17, the Graph's `fatigued` creep (LSG:519) |
| `RUPTURES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` | 1 | count | 0..1 | two lethal chains; the breach exceeds fairness | no tear; the breach has no hull teeth | HE-F17, `RUPTURE_HP` (registry, LSG:102) |

#### Presence, ingress and return

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` ★ | 300 | ticks | 120..360 | only `TELL_MIN_TICKS − it` of visible stalk; cry-wolf risk | the horror appears almost instantly in the tell; no mystery | HE-F9 (`≤ TELL_MIN_TICKS − STALK_VISIBLE_FLOOR_TICKS`), `TELL_MIN_TICKS` 600 |
| `STALK_VISIBLE_FLOOR_TICKS` | 240 | ticks | 120..360 | caps the ingress delay below 300 | accepts a very short visible stalk | HE-F9 |
| `INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` | 60 | ticks | 30..120 | the arrival is announced too early to mean anything | the first sight is a teleport (Pillar 1) | HE-20, HE-F9 |
| `RETURN_DELAY_TICKS` | 600 | ticks | 300..1200 | the horror lingers aboard in CALM | the CALM presence resumes right after a breach | HE-20 |
| `RETURN_HOME` | 1 | flag | 0 or 1 | n/a | 0: it lurks in the body of the last breach (the creative-director lean); flip this knob **first** if validation target (2), the 70 % CALM sighting, or target (7), the 60 % CALM behaviour change, fails | HE-20, Open Questions |
| `LURK_DWELL_TICKS` | 900 | ticks | 300..3600 | static wallpaper | restless; a second tell cadence | HE-11 |
| `HORROR_MAX_MVP` | 1 | count | 1 (2 is Vertical Slice) | n/a | n/a | HE-1, HE-F12 |
| `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` ★ | 2 | count | 1..12 | Emitter budget pressure; Interaction's `H` and Derelict Authoring's `HORRORS` quote this (C-HE-4 closed 2026-10-27) | an E6 false return risk during ingress | HE-F12, `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS` 16, C-HE-4 |
| `HOLD_MAX_RUNS` | 12 | runs | 4..36 | a stalled horror stands visible too long | goes LURK on a brief Layout glitch | HE-25, E-HE-31 |
| `BLOCKED_RUNS_MAX` | 6 | runs | 3..24 | long stalls behind a changed door | re-paths needlessly | HE-25, E-HE-32 |
| `PATH_EXPANSIONS_MAX` | 64 | expansions | 16..256 | per-run cost (spike; `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 is a logging threshold) | long routes truncate | HE-F10 |
