#### Relations to carry into the registry (reflexion 2: register the formula, not only the constants)

`UNFOLD_LO ≥ ceil5(ceil(R_SNAP_PX / V_WALK) + REACT_ALLOW_TICKS_HORROR + 1)` (HE-F5); `V_CREEP_MPX < V_HUNT_MPX < V_WALK × 1000` (HE-F6); `SUIT_HP_FULL − SUIT_HP_OK < STRIKE_BP` and `2 × STRIKE_BP < SUIT_HP_FULL − SUIT_HP_FAIL` (HE-F3); `2 × MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN ≤ MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP − STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` (HE-F1); `R_SNAP_PX < REACH_RANGE_PX < LURK_STANDOFF_PX` and `REACH_RANGE_PX ≤ 3 × H_crew` (HE-F14); `MARK_LIFE_TICKS ≥ smear_retention_ticks ≥ TRAIL_TARGET_TICKS` (HE-F7); `strength_pm ≤ 1000 − MOD_FLOOR_PM` (HE-F11); `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS ≤ TELL_MIN_TICKS − STALK_VISIBLE_FLOOR_TICKS` (HE-F9); `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS > STAGGER_TICKS` and `t_out ≤ STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` (HE-F15); `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX + A_max ≤ MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS` (HE-F12); `LURK_STANDOFF_PX < HIDE_SAFE_R` (Sanity-owned, SN-22); `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` 90 <= `UNFOLD_LO` (Adaptive Audio, registry `reach_resolve_start`).

## Visual/Audio Requirements

*Consulted: art-director (the visual half below follows its specification, reconciled with the rulings and the checkpoint rulings). Audio: no audio proposal exists and the Adaptive Audio & Tells stage directory is empty, so this section states only the **facts Horror publishes** and the **mood the sound must sit inside**; every sound, layer, mix and cue name is Adaptive Audio's (HE-37; Designed, `adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`). Requirements, never pixels, shaders or code: implementation patterns are marked "-> ADR candidate". Constants named here that are not in the Tuning Knobs table are **presentation-side** (render only, never in `horror.state`, never hashed) and are listed in their own table below.*

**Verdict.** Horror's visual job is narrow and the art bible already frames it (ART 5.4): a real horror is **a lit Void Steel mass with a thin iridescence band, seen only in someone's light, hidden by walls and by darkness, casting nothing, emitting nothing, and leaving honest web-grammar marks.** Everything else the player sees "from the horror" belongs to a neighbour (Attention's strands and tell, Failure & Trail's seam descriptors, Suit's rung and seam jet, Lighting's executed dips). Five decisions carry the section: (1) the rig body has **no contour, no shadow, no glow, binary alpha, and is never a flat fill**, so it cannot be mistaken for the stage-2 "shadow with no occluder" lie; (2) the REACH telegraph is the **limb-enters-the-cone-before-the-body** image with a **stationary tip on the locked strike point**, so the picture is itself the honest warning; (3) three marks ship (smear, branching rime, radial hairlines), each in a different value and topology class from every Failure & Trail trail, with the rime needing a **new palette entry** (C-HE-21); (4) every Horror visual is **truth channel**, the only corruptible things being the per-viewer omission of the body (Filter, Vertical Slice) and any phantom or class (c) rig the Filter drives, which **must leak violet or flesh and are never Horror's**; (5) the HUD shows **nothing** about a horror.

### Events: sprite and VFX state, audio fact, channel

Legend. **H** = Horror's presenter draws it. **N:x** = a neighbour draws or executes it and Horror only publishes state. Channel: **T** = truth (a function of SIM state; a hallucination cannot add, remove or alter it); **C** = corruptible (the Filter's). The audio column names the **fact** `horror.view` publishes; the sound is Adaptive Audio's. All "poses" are stepped every 5th tick (12 fps); position is interpolated (HE-40).

| # | Event (source) | What the player must see (sprite state / VFX) | Owner | Audio fact published (class owned by Adaptive Audio) | Channel | Cites |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V1 | **LURK** (CALM) | `rest` pose at a true position, visible only if lit; slow idle: at most one 15-degree quantum of drift on one limb per pose step, hash-irregular, never periodic. A seen LURK reads as an animal at rest: patient, not posed. Same silhouette and mass as the lethal presence | H | `state`, `pos_px`, `section_id`: the positional source of its diegetic sounds; no stinger, no proximity cue | T | ART 5.4; HE-11 |
| V2 | **STALK** (TELL, and the BREACH approach) | `move` pose cycle (creep): core displacement by sim position, limbs trail and pick; irregular gait (hash), never a metronome, so it cannot be mistaken for the tell's beat-locked cadence. No strand, no band-slide, no dust impulse on the beat | H | as V1 plus `state_enter_T` | T | ART 2.7; AV-18; HE-12 |
| V3 | **REACH start** (BREACH only) | A limb enters the beam cone **before the core does**; it begins to unfold toward the locked point; a **touch-down SMEAR** is laid at P (a true ring entry, not a marker). No icon, ring, arc, disc or ground decal | H | `locked_pt`, `reach_end_T` published on the run of the state change: a positional REACH cue scheduled to resolve at `reach_end_T` (owed) | T | ART 5.4; HE-14; CP-1 |
| V4 | **REACH hold** (last ~30 % of `UNFOLD_TICKS`) | Limb fully extended, segments straight within one quantum, **tip stationary on the locked point**; a 1-quantum irregular sway on the mid segments only. Pose tags `reach_a`, `reach_b`, `reach_c`, never `held` or `taut` (those are `web_strand_*` tags, AV-18) | H | as V3 | T | HE-14; VA-1 |
| V5 | **REACH fizzle** (predicate fails at the last tick) | The reverse of the unfold poses (the same limb retract as RETREAT). No snap, no hairlines. The touch-down smear stays | H | `state` returns to RETREAT; no strike cue | T | HE-17 |
| V6 | **STRIKE** (resolution) | One pose step to `snap` (limb curled back to the core side), **no lunge travel**. Within `HULL_CONTACT_PX`: radial hairlines laid at the contact point (V12). If a seat was hit: one **STRUCK** burst laid at P (a true ring entry; never a decal on the seat). **No hit flash, hit-stop, screen shake, camera jolt, impact decal on the seat, blood or number.** The consequence is shown by neighbours: Suit's rung change, stagger and seam jet; Failure & Trail's hull descriptor | H (+ N:Suit, N:F&T) | `strike_T`, the seats hit, the hull segments gouged or ruptured: a positional strike cue from `strike_T` (the witness of strike one, HE-15) | T | HE-15; FT:98 |
| V7 | **RETREAT** | `move` pose, limb retracting to `rest`, core displacement away. As legible as the approach; the same object leaving, not a dissolve | H | as V1 | T | HE-17 |
| V8 | **Hull ingress** (TELL, at least `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` after entry) | The rig first appears at a hull segment, preferably starting **behind a wall front face** so the wall's Y-sort hides it and it **emerges across the wall footprint's south edge**; on a south-facing wall the rig starts in `rest` with the ingress mark laid on the same step. **No fade, dissolve or pop-in animation** (alpha is 0 or 255); visibility changes only because light reaches it. The ingress mark (smear and hairlines) precedes the body by `INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` 60 | H (+ V10, V12) | the ingress event with its tick and the segment: a hull creak at the segment (owed) | T | ART 3.3; PIPE:303; HE-20 |
| V9 | **Hull egress** (return, or a TELL abort) | The rig exits across a hull segment the same way (behind the wall face or in the dark) and leaves the egress mark (smear and hairlines at the segment). Same grammar as any mark; no special exit art | H | the egress event with its tick | T | HE-20 |
| V10 | **Smear** (path mark) | A wet, branching Void Steel smear on the floor with one band-streak of iridescence **while lit**. No filament attached. Floor-marks band, never Y-sorted | H | none (marks are silent) | T | ART 5.5; PIPE:137 |
| V11 | **Branching rime** (at CHILL deposit points) | White-grey rime that forks like a filament: hue-less, non-repeating angles, never at a coolant fitting. A surface mark the web leaves, not a claim about air temperature (HE-27) | H | none | T | FT:1427; HE-31 |

MVP: drawn **on the floor at the base of the hull face**; a wall-face mark needs a draw band the Pipeline has not defined (Vertical Slice) | H | none | T | FT-31; D-AD5 |

A frame swap on the alpha-binary sprite, never a fade; not a meter | H | none | T | HE-F7; D-AD8 |

The copy is cosmetic and cannot be hash-visible; on restore no decay plays | H | none | T | HE-30 |

**Visual feedback Horror must not produce (lint).** No hit flash, hit-stop, damage number, health pip, threat bar, proximity ring, direction arrow, off-screen indicator, lock-on, outline, glow, aura, tint, vignette, shake, chromatic aberration or any HUD glyph. No ground decal marking the strike point. No horror shadow or occluder (HE-2). No fade in or out of any Horror sprite.

### Animation and motion constraints

**The rig (ART 5.4, PIPE row 14, ART 8.10).**

| Rule | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Pose clock | the pose advances **every 5th tick** (12 fps) from sim state; the stated exception to `present_time` | PIPE:303; HE-4 |
| Position | integer base px from the sim, interpolated `pos_prev` to `pos_curr` with the five-tick alpha (HE-F18). **Pose is stepped, position is smoothed**; poses are never tweened | HE-40 |
| Segment rotation | quantised to **15 degrees** (24 angles); only web pieces and horror rig segments may rotate | PIPE:116; ART 8.10 |
| Sort origin | the **core's foot row**; every segment shares it; joint overlap by `z_index` inside the horror's own parent | PIPE:129 |
| Facings | **none** (ART 5.6): the pose is chosen from state and the heading to the target | ART 5.6 |
| Canonical poses | `rest`, `reach`, `move`, each passing the ART 3.1 silhouette test at 25 % with no colour and no contour **before** colouring; `snap` is a derived fourth pose of the same segments and must also pass | ART 5.4; 8.9 |
| Camera | the camera never reacts to a horror; the presenter never writes it | PIPE C1, C9 |
| Alpha | binary, 0 or 255 | ART 8.9 line 5 |
| Segment cost | one rig = core plus 3 limbs of 3 to 4 segments = 10 to 13 sprites in the single Y-sorted layer | ART 5.7, 8.6 |

Quantising pixel segments in the 640 × 360 pass can blur at sub-pixel; the art bible's accepted fallbacks are **pre-rotated 24-angle segment sets** (a tool step) or a base-resolution sub-viewport for horrors (ART 8.10). -> ADR candidate (3): rig stepping and quantisation. Per-segment iridescence through per-instance shader parameters is `[BIN]` (needs the lighting spike or a technical-artist check); `Skeleton2D` is reserved for a later filament-rider type.

**The REACH telegraph as a picture (the only per-strike warning).** `UNFOLD_TICKS` is drawn from [`UNFOLD_LO` 95, `UNFOLD_HI` 180] (19 to 36 poses). Shares of the drawn length, rounded to whole poses, with the hold never below 6 poses:

| Stage | Share | What changes | What stays still |
|---|---|---|---|
| a. Enter | first ~15 % (3 to 5 poses) | the reach limb crosses into the beam or lit pool before the core | the core's position (if it also moves, at `V_HUNT_MPX` or slower) |
| b. Unfold | next ~55 % (10 to 20 poses) | segments straighten **root to tip, one quantum per pose step per segment**; the tip travels toward the locked point | the non-reach limbs rest |
| c. Hold | last ~30 % (6 poses at the lower bound: 28.5 ticks rounded up to 30; 11 at the upper) | segments straight, a 1-quantum irregular sway on the mid segments only | **the tip, on the locked point** |
| d. Snap | the resolution tick | one pose step to `snap` | -- |

Visual-honesty invariants (presentation criteria; ids `VA-n` so they do not collide with the rule ids):
- **VA-1 (tip = locked point).** At the hold stage the rig's reach-limb tip pixel lies within `TIP_LOCK_TOL_PX` (4 [PH]) of the sim's `locked_pt`. A telegraph that points at a different place than it strikes is a lie (Pillar 1). Tested by comparing the rig's published tip anchor to `horror.view` in a headless presenter stub.
- **VA-2 (reach cap).** The limb's maximum visual reach from the core is `3 × H_crew` = 3 × 53 = 159 base px (ART 5.4 and 8.2), so `REACH_RANGE_PX <= 159` (HE-F14).
- **VA-3 (the strike zone is legible without a decal).** With the tip on the locked point, the player sees "this tile and one around it"; nothing draws the 72 px radius.
- **VA-4 (stillness is the cue).** The telegraph depends on motion (unfold), then stillness (hold), then a one-step snap, and survives achromatopsia (shape and motion only) and reduced motion.
- **VA-5 (minimum tip).** The limb tip is at least 4 base px wide (above the 2 × 2 floor), so an extended limb resolves at 1:1 at k = 1. At 384 × 216 the 80 px rig is about 37 % of field height and a 159 px reach is 74 % of field width; at k = 1 on 1080p it is 7.4 % of field height and only the form class (limb extended or not) is guaranteed.

**Creep, unfold, snap (ART 5.4, 3.3).** *Creep* (LURK, STALK, RETREAT): continuous core displacement, stepped limb poses, no two consecutive steps repeat a limb configuration (hash on `(entity_id, pose_index)`), never a visible period. *Unfold* (REACH): the root-to-tip ripple above, no bounce and no overshoot (overshoot is the HUD *settle* word, ART 7.3). *Snap* (STRIKE): one pose step, the only fast motion Horror owns; the contrast with creep makes it readable; no afterimage, no trail. **Never:** squash and stretch, overshoot, a wind-up frame separate from the unfold, blur, motion lines.

**Three "something at the edge of my light" sources, separated.**

| Source | Owner | Where | Sync | The test the player already uses (AT:1184) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tell strands, dust impulse, lamp gutter | Attention + Lighting | every lit region | **same beat, global** | "everywhere on the beat?" |
| Horror L3 dip | Horror state, Lighting executes | lamps within `L3_RADIUS_PX` of **one horror** | **unsynchronised, local**, irregular | "one place, moving along a path?" |
| Horror marks | Horror | one location per mark | none; persisted | "was it here before I arrived?" |
| Sanity violet fringe | Sanity | the viewer's own rim | per viewer, breathing | "does my crewmate see it?" |

L3 outranks L2 on a given lamp while active (LO-18), so the lamp nearest the horror shows the horror's rhythm and the rest of the room shows the beat. That is intended and is the test above (AC-HE-111); do not equalise them.

**Lighting interaction (physical honesty).** A real horror contributes **no occluder, casts no shadow, emits nothing and changes no `lux`** (HE-2; EM-20 (c); LO rail 10). The beam passes through its volume and lights what is behind it; the rig's sprite hides what stands behind it by Y-sort only, exactly as crewmates do, so a horror is not singled out as a "ghost". The mitigation for the "solid thing that casts nothing" feel is that the rig is **normal-mapped and specular** (relief reacts to beam direction), never flat. Fiction gloss for the player-facing codex (creative-director, owed to the art bible): *"The web does not stop light; it only takes it. Nothing that belongs to the web casts a shadow, so a shadow with no occluder is never the web: it is you."*

### Art-bible principles that apply

| Ref | Applies as |
|---|---|
| ART 1 Principle 1 (darkness is honest; light is a decision) | A horror is not drawn unlit, not drawn by fog, not drawn by a mask. "Looking is spending" (ART 5.4) |
| ART 1 Principle 2 (state lives in the sprite or trail, not the icon) | Every Horror state is a pose; every footprint is a mark; no icons |
| ART 1 Principle 3 (emission is exposure) | Horrors emit nothing; the player's light is the only cause of seeing them |
| ART 1 Corollary (the lie leaks) | A real Horror rig carries **no violet and no flesh**; a rig with them is by definition not Horror's |
| ART 2.7 The Tell | No strands, band-slide, stub reveals or dust-on-beat (AV-18) |
| ART 2.8 Breach rhythm | "More things moving, not the screen fighting the player": Horror adds one moving thing, no red, no sweep, no flash |
| ART 3.1, 3.2, 5.4 | Elongated, asymmetric, open, branching silhouette; limb count not four; no closed circle, no bilateral symmetry, no four limbs in pairs, no upright compact stance, nothing held, no Tungsten or emission, no worn human part, no Graphite plating, no albedo hue |
| ART 3.3 | Web shapes branch and never repeat; the web passes behind human geometry and emerges (V8) |
| ART 3.5 | The web earns no hero shape; legibility comes from silhouette, relief and the band, not extra contrast, outline or glow |
| ART 4.1, 4.4, 4.7 | The iridescence arc excludes 270 to 330 degrees; violet and flesh reserved; a new semantic value (rime white-grey) needs a palette entry and a named shape backup |
| ART 4.5 | The luminance ladder: see the collision checks below |
| ART 5.5 | Real horror against class (c) against hidden entity; footprints (iii) to (v) |
| ART 5.6 | LOD: silhouette first, marks second, texture never; minimum feature 2 × 2 px; no rig facings |
| ART 5.7 | Budget: a horror type is 10 to 15 segment sprites plus 3 test poses; footprints about 25 images for 5 loops (MVP ships 3: see the asset budget) |
| ART 6.6 rule 5 | A horror and a filament use the same grammar by design; separate by **motion and mass**: a filament has no core, a horror's branches converge |
| ART 8.2, 8.5, 8.6, 8.9, 8.10, 8.11 | Pixel sizes, naming, atlases, acceptance, rig rotation, hull-floor tint (does **not** apply to horrors) and the at-most-5 material count |

### Palette and semantic colour

**Rig body.** Albedo **Void Steel `#0B1018` only**: no Graphite plating, Hull Grey or other albedo hue (ART 5.4). Silhouette and relief come from the `_nrm` and `_spc` height passes (2 to 4 levels, at least 3 distinct levels per segment slice), not extra albedo values; the palette lock (ART 8.9 line 5) therefore holds with no change. The **iridescence band** is a thin-film specular band cycling teal, green, bronze and gold at low saturation, a band not a fill, the 270 to 330 degree arc excluded, peak at or under `BAND_PEAK_LSTAR_MAX` (about L\* 61, Attention's spike-confirmed ceiling at AT:1264: cited, never restated as Horror's, and not a registry name), no bloom at `glow_hdr_threshold`, intensity scaling with ring (faint at the outer ring, ART 4.3). **At MVP the horror lives in a ring-1 derelict, so the band is faint and the rig must read by silhouette and relief first.** **No contour** (D-AD2): a 1 px Void Steel contour on a Void Steel mass is invisible and ART 3.5 denies the web a hero shape, so the edge is defined by relief and the band. **Never flat:** a real mass shows relief highlights on its beam-facing sides (flip test, ART 6.2 rule 6); that is what separates it in a single still frame from the stage-2 lie, which is a flat violet-black polygon with no relief and no band (ART 4.4, 8.11 flag 2). **Unlit: not drawn**; the ambient-floor tint of ART 8.11 flag 1 applies to hull geometry and never to horrors.

**Marks (D-AD1: not drawn unlit).** ART 8.11 flag 1 (i) extends the hull-floor tint to trails because a trail is a mark on a hull surface, but excludes "web, filaments or horrors"; Horror marks are web-grammar marks on a hull surface, so the text is ambiguous. Horror marks follow the **web rule**: ART 5.5 says "iridescent where lit", Principle 1 says a footprint should cost the player light to read, and a hull-tinted dark mark would sit between Failure & Trail's trails and Horror's marks and invite the collision AC-79 exists to separate. In the dark the player walks over unseen marks; that is the intended price.

| Mark | Albedo | Hue | Band | Value under a beam, on lit Graphite floor (L\* about 43) | Not allowed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Smear** | Void Steel body | band hues only, low saturation | **yes**, one specular streak, peak at or under `BAND_PEAK_LSTAR_MAX` | darker than the floor with a brighter streak | any loop hue, Chemlight, Tungsten, violet, flesh, Alarm Red |
| **Branching rime** | **Rime White-Grey** (proposed `#B0B8C0`, `[PROVISIONAL]`, C-HE-21) | hue-less (S under 10 %) | no | **L\* 70 to 78** (FT:1427) | any fan, any regular angle, any location at a coolant fitting |
| **Radial hairlines** | Rime White-Grey | hue-less | no | L\* 70 to 78 | a hole, a dark centre, Hull Grey, a line along a rib or seam |

**Luminance and collision checks (ART 4.5).** Rime or hairlines (about L\* 74) against **Hull Grey** (L\* 61) F&T cracks: a gap of 13 and a different topology (radial from a point against along a rib or seam). Against **Coolant Ice** (L\* 91) frost fans: a gap of 17 plus topology (a static radial fan at a fitting against an irregular fork away from fittings); this is the AC-79 collision fixture and value is a second separating axis. Against the **Chemlight pool** (L\* about 70): a **new greyscale collision**, separated by shape (a floor mark of fixed extent with branching edges against an omnidirectional pool around a thrown source sprite, steady then waning); a new ART 4.7 row is owed. Against Power Amber and O2 Cyan (L\* about 80): a gap of 6, but those are emissive loop media on pipes and the shape and location gap is large; recorded and acceptable. Warm-lit rime lands at hue 40 to 43 degrees, S about 28 %: it passes the flesh-hue test (at least 36 degrees) but is the second-closest sane colour to flesh after warm-lit Hull Grey, so the asset test runs on **lit** rime (R-5).

**The new palette entry (C-HE-21).** A hue-less rime at L\* 70 to 78 has no palette hex (Hull Grey is L\* 61, Paper White `#DDE4EA` is UI only, Coolant Ice is a loop hue), so the palette-lock check would fail the rime sheet. Proposed: **Rime White-Grey `#B0B8C0`** (about L\* 74, S about 8 %, hue about 210 degrees, the cool family of Hull Grey), role "Horror footprint rime and hairlines only; never a pipe, fill, fixture, UI or loop colour", on the Chemlight precedent. This is an art-bible decision (creative-director, art-director); it blocks asset-spec for rime and hairlines, not this GDD. The fallback is to read FT:1427 as a rendered-appearance range and use Hull Grey, which then needs a "thin-element only" exemption for a floor mark (worse).

**Forbidden in every Horror asset (automatic scan, FT:886):** Alarm Red; Chemlight; Tungsten (both); violet (hue 270-330, S over 25 %); flesh (hue 5-30, S 20-60 %, V 45-85 %); loop hues; Graphite plating; any filament shape from `web_strand_*`; any pixel not in the palette (plus Rime White-Grey once approved) or transparent.

**Semantic colour rule.** Horror has no colour language of its own: its meaning is carried by silhouette, motion, value class and location. The only hues on a real horror are the band's, which decorate exposure (Principle 3) and are not state. The player must never learn "the colour of this horror tells me X".

### Truth channel against corruptible

| Visual | Class | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Rig body of a **real** horror (all poses) | **T**; per-viewer omission of the whole body (Filter, Vertical Slice) is the only permitted corruption; never edited, tinted, re-posed, moved or delayed | ART 5.0 "never edited, only wholly substituted or omitted" |
| REACH telegraph poses (V3 to V5) | **T** | the pose is a function of SIM state; a hallucination cannot add, remove or alter the telegraph of a real strike |
| Smear, rime, hairline marks, ageing, eviction decay | **T** | persisted SIM ring; "the hallucination hides the body, never the trail" (CON:243) |
| L3 disturbance state | **T** (Lighting executes) | HE-35; the Filter cannot add or remove dips |
| Hull descriptors from RUPTURE and GOUGE | **T** (consequence channel; F&T draws) | LSG:168 closed list |
| Suit rung, stagger, seam jet after a strike | **T** (Suit's) | SE-22, SE-23 |
| Ingress and egress marks | **T** | the same ring |
| **Phantom entity rig** (class (a) invented) | **C** (the Filter's) | must leak violet or flesh; may not use Horror's marks, L3 or Attention assets (AV-18); leaves **no** footprint |
| **Class (c) crewmate-as-horror rig** | **C** | driven by the crewmate's TRUE velocity and TRUE beam; fringe and leak per ART 5.5 |
| Hidden-real-entity "nothing drawn" | **C** (omission only) | body only; marks, L3 and harm unchanged |

- **V-T1.** Every Horror presenter output derives from SIM-published `horror.view`, `attention.view` and the mark ring. No presenter reads `PERCEPTION_OUT` or any sanity value (H5, CR-12).
- **V-T2 (bit-identity).** With any seat's Filter hiding a body, the marks, L3 state, M17 writes and suit damage are bit-identical to the run without a Filter (AC-HE-63).
- **V-T3 (no lie by omission on reload).** Marks persist and re-draw on restore; an unpersisted mark would let a reload lie.
- **The `leak` input (D-AD4).** The rig presenter takes `(pose_stream, type_id)` and an optional `leak` that **only the Filter may set**. A real horror renders with `leak` 0; any phantom or class (c) render must have `leak` above 0 (violet `#B07BE6` at the contour, per ART 5.5; flesh in the stage-3 drift). **Lint: a rig instance without a Horror ref in `horror.view` and without `leak` above 0 is an error** (a lie that does not leak is forbidden by the Corollary).

### Audio: facts and mood

Horror publishes **facts** (the table above) and owns no sound, mix or cue name. The mood any sound must sit inside is **patient, wet and structural**: closer to weather with intent than to a monster with a personality; it never roars at the camera. Constraints that bind Adaptive Audio and that Horror's lints assert (Audio AA-6 (the identity table), Sanity R28): the Horror sub-tell family is **disjoint** from Attention's tell family, the Graph loop sounds and Sanity's hallucination family; phantom horrors use the Filter and Sanity classes, **never the Graph witness acoustic classes**; Horror never mutes, ducks or filters anything; there is **no non-diegetic proximity cue** (a distance-scaled stinger is a meter by ear, Pillar 3); the source of every Horror sound is positional (the entity's `pos_px`, the segment, or `locked_pt`). The cues Horror's facts allow Audio to schedule, Designed (`adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`, AA-28): a floor-borne creep (V1, V2, V7), which Audio plays only while the entity is moving in LURK, STALK or RETREAT and never in TRANSIT; a REACH cue from `locked_pt`, a non-percussive lead-in started `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` 90 before `reach_end_T` and ending there with no impact transient (V3); a strike cue at `strike_T` (V6); a hull creak at an ingress or egress segment (V8, V9), which covers the transit (Audio AA-28 a, d). Marks and L3 dips are silent (Lighting's electrical grammar covers a dipping lamp).

### Presentation-side constants (render only; not SIM, not in `horror.state`, not hashed)

| Name | Default | Unit | Meaning | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TIP_LOCK_TOL_PX` | 4 [PLACEHOLDER] | base px | tolerance between the rig's tip anchor and `locked_pt` at the hold stage (VA-1) | art-director, rig presenter |
| `MARK_DECAY_TICKS` | 90 [PLACEHOLDER] | ticks | the cosmetic 3-frame decay of an evicted mark's presentation copy (V14) | art-director, mark presenter |
| `LIMB_TIP_MIN_PX` | 4 | base px | minimum limb-tip width (VA-5) | art-director |

### Assets implied (specifications only, no pixels; for `/asset-spec`)

Names follow ART 8.5 (`<cat>[_<dialect>]_<subject>[_<variant tokens>]_<map>.png`; the sheet is the file unit; poses and states are Aseprite tags and slices). All cells keep a 4 px transparent margin (ART 8.6), lossless, binary alpha.

| # | Asset | Stem and sheets | Size and frames | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | **Horror type 01 rig sheet** (the only MVP type: floor-attached, three-branch, about 1.5 × crewmate height = about 80 px, one reach limb) | `horror_t01` with `_alb`, `_nrm`, `_spc` (atlas `horror_t01`, 1024 × 1024) | core 64 × 64 or 96 × 96; segments 32 × 32, 48 × 32, 64 × 32; 10 to 15 segment sprites; slices `core`, `seg_01`..`seg_12` | `t01` is a placeholder, renamed never aliased. `_msk` B = the iridescence band region. The height and material pass of 2 to 4 levels carries the relief that replaces a contour |
| H2 | **Pose test fixtures** | `horror_t01_pose_rest`, `_reach`, `_move`, `_snap` (in `tests/`, not shipped) | 4 composites | the silhouette test and the lit-frame test |
| H3 | **Smear mark** | `vfx_footprint_smear` `_alb`, `_msk` (page `vfx_shared`) | 48 × 48 cells; 2 variants × (3 lay-down + 2 aged) = 10 | `_msk` B = the band streak. Flip x, flip y and both give 8 looks with no runtime rotation (D-AD3) |
| H4 | **Branching rime mark** | `vfx_footprint_rime` `_alb`, `_msk` | 48 × 48; 2 variants × (3 + 2) = 10 | Rime White-Grey albedo; irregular fork angles; no fan |
| H5 | **Radial hairline mark** | `vfx_footprint_hairline` `_alb`, `_msk` | 48 × 48 (96 × 96 if a longer reach is needed); 2 variants × (2 lay-down + 1 aged) = 6 | radial from a centre pixel with **no hole**; 2 px minimum stroke. Also serves the STRUCK mark as a full 360-degree burst centred on P (the hull-contact use is clipped to the floor side of the face); if the hull fan cannot be rotated or mirrored into a full burst, up to 3 extra cells (one per stage) are needed |
| H6 | **Rig presenter hooks** | no art | -- | takes `(pose_stream, type_id, leak)` |
| H7 | **Palette entry** | `palette_synaptic_sea.gpl` and the swatch gain Rime White-Grey `#B0B8C0` | -- | contingent on C-HE-21 |
| H8 | **Atlas page** | `vfx_shared` gains H3 to H5 | 1024 × 1024 page | shared with F&T and the Tell; `HORROR_MARK_DRAW_GROUPS` 4 within `D_marks` |

**Not needed (by decision):** no new light texture, light or material (the smear and rig reuse the one iridescence material already in the at-most-5 count; rime and hairlines use the plain unlit-albedo path), no new hex besides H7, no UI or HUD asset, no sound asset (Audio's), no `web_strand_*` or `vfx_tell_*` reuse, no dust or filament-twitch asset (Vertical Slice A), no shadow blob, no outline.

**Budget (C-A5).** ART 5.7 budgets about 25 images for **5** footprint loops; MVP ships 3 marks. *(orchestrator ruling: the art-director's "lean 24" cut gives smear and rime only two post-lay-down frames, but HE-F7 defines three ageing stages for them, so the lean cut would lose stage 2. The MVP budget is therefore 2 × (5 + 5 + 3) = **26 images**; the ceiling is the art-director's full 32; the STRUCK burst reuses the hairline sheet and adds at most 3 cells, 29 in the worst case.)* 26 images is about the figure ART 5.7 sets for five loops, spent on three: an Open Question for the art-director, not an MVP blocker.

**Naming-lint exclusivity (AV-18 extended).** `vfx_footprint_*` and `horror_*` stems are reserved for the Horror presenter and the Filter's rig re-use; Attention's presenter references neither. The pose tags `rest`, `reach_a/b/c`, `move`, `snap` must not appear in `web_strand_*` (which uses `held` and `taut`).

### Accessibility (visual half)

- **Reduced motion.** The REACH **unfold stays** (it is the telegraph; removing it removes the warning). Reduced motion cuts the idle sway, the creep irregularity amplitude and the L3 dip depth to the `MOD_FLOOR_PM`-bound minimum, but **never** the unfold-hold-snap sequence. Mark ageing stays (three frame stages).
- **Reduced flashing.** L3 dips follow Lighting's recipe (spacing kept, depth capped); **no Horror visual changes luminance faster than 3 Hz**; the snap is one pose step, not a luminance event.
- **Colourblind and achromatopsia.** Horror has no hue-coded channel. Rime against frost: shape, topology and value. Smear: the band hue is decoration; the branching floor smear is the channel. The telegraph survives (silhouette, motion, stillness).
- **Low vision.** The ambient-floor option reveals hull only, **never** horrors or marks (web rule): the horror stays beam-dependent.
- **Deaf and hard of hearing.** The visual channels are the picture-telegraph, L3 dips and marks. The telegraph is beam-dependent only for the limb **body**; its tip rests on the victim's own foot point inside the victim's own light (HE-14), so the tip is lit for the victim (R-2, resolved by CP-1). Audio's facts are Audio's.
- **Photosensitivity.** No flash anywhere in Horror's visuals.
- **No caption or indicator for a horror under any setting** (AT-24).
- **Zoom (ladder k in [1, k_auto + 2]).** All Horror lengths are base px and scale with `s = field_h / 360`. Marks follow FT-34: full legibility at `k_auto` and the first zoom-out; at k = 1 only polarity, form class and value class are guaranteed, so a hairline may not resolve (accepted and logged, not a licence to cull marks).

### Risks (carried to the creative-director and the lighting spike)

- **R-1 Real horror against the stage-2 lie.** A Void Steel mass on a lit Graphite floor is a dark polygon, as is the lie. Separated by relief (at least 3 normal/specular levels, flip test), the band, a branching silhouette, Y-sort occlusion and motion. If AC-HE-108's blind still-frame test fails, the horror asset is redrawn; the stage-2 lie is never changed.
- **R-2 The telegraph needs light on the limb: RESOLVED by CP-1** (the tip is on the victim's own foot point, lit by the victim's own light). The residual risk (the body is unseen from behind the cone) is carried by AC-HE-106.
- **R-3 Outer ring, faint band.** Tune in the lighting spike with the rig in a lit frame at each ring ambient.
- **R-4 Ingress emerging.** V8 depends on Layout exposing a hull-entry anchor on a wall footprint; otherwise ingress is a visible appear. Binary alpha forbids a fade; mitigated by "never into a room holding a seat" and the ingress mark. Owed: a "behind a wall footprint" flag on hull-entry anchors.
- **R-5 Rime near-flesh when warm-lit.** Run the ART 4.4 sampling on lit rime and on lit Hull Grey first.
- **R-6 Rig puppetry (ART 5.4).** Mitigated by hash-irregular creep, no repeated limb configurations in consecutive steps, per-instance variance seeded by `entity_id`, and joint overlap.
- **R-7 L3 as a free radar.** Bounded by `L3_RADIUS_PX`, `L3_MIN_GAP_TICKS` and the EMERGENCY exclusion so it never becomes a second tell cadence.
- **R-8 Mark density.** 24 marks on a 5-compartment derelict may compete with F&T trails (`TRAIL_DMARKS_SHARE` 6); capped by `HORROR_MARK_DRAW_GROUPS` and tested in the AC-79 fixtures; the `D_marks` 4 to 6 starvation is C-HE-31.
- **R-9 Engine gaps.** `docs/engine-reference/godot/` has no `scripting.md`, no `core.md`, and no `Skeleton2D` or Y-sort coverage (`modules/rendering.md` covers post-processing, anti-aliasing and the backend; `modules/audio.md` covers playback and buses, which are Audio's). Per-instance shader parameters, the GDScript cost of a 12 fps limb animator and the Y-sorted draw count at 50 to 300 sprites are **spike parameters** (ART 8.12 steps 5 and 8); nothing in this section names an API beyond ART 8.10's `[BIN]` entries.

### Decisions made in this section

| Id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-AD1 | Horror marks are **not drawn unlit** (web rule, not trail rule) | ART 5.5 "iridescent where lit"; Principle 1; avoids the hull-tint collision with F&T |
| D-AD2 | Horror rigs carry **no contour** | Void Steel on Void Steel is invisible; ART 3.5 |
| D-AD3 | Marks use **2 authored variants × flips**, no runtime rotation; selection by pure integer hash | PIPE:116 lists only web pieces and rig segments as rotatable |
| D-AD4 | The rig presenter exposes a Filter-only `leak` input; an unbacked rig without leak is a lint error | the Corollary: a lie must leak |
| D-AD5 | Hairline minimum stroke **2 px**, taper by fork not width | ART 5.6 minimum feature |
| D-AD6 | Adopt FT:1427 and propose Rime White-Grey `#B0B8C0` | the palette lock needs an entry; C-HE-21 |
| D-AD7 | Pose tags `rest`, `reach_a/b/c`, `move`, `snap` (not `held`, `taut`) | AV-18 lint disjointness |
| D-AD8 | Three-stage mark ageing as frame swaps | physical time readable without a meter; binary alpha forbids fades |

📌 **Asset Spec** — Visual/Audio requirements are defined. **Preconditions (BLOCKING, AC-HE-123):** the owed art-bible and Pipeline refresh rows (ART:542, ART 5.5, PIPE:140, IDX:35) must have landed for the rig, and Rime White-Grey (C-HE-21) must be decided for rime and hairlines. Then run `/asset-spec system:horror-entities` to produce the per-asset visual specs and AI generation prompts from this section.

## Game Feel

Horror has **no input of its own**: the player never presses anything at it (PC-12, HE-38). Its "feel" is the feel of being *read* by something patient and then *choosing* inside a window the design guarantees is long enough. The targets below therefore specify the **input-to-feedback chain** (what the player does in answer to what the horror shows), the **timing of the picture** (ticks, pose counts, px) and the **absence of every action-game punctuation** (hit-stop, shake, flash), each as a number. Conversions: 1 tick = 16.7 ms at `TICK_RATE` 60; one Horror run = 5 ticks = 83.3 ms = one 12 fps rig pose; 95 ticks = 1.58 s; 180 ticks = 3.00 s; 300 ticks = 5.00 s. Every number is `[PLACEHOLDER]` pending the blind-legibility playtest (AC-HE-106) and cites its Tuning Knob or Formula.

### Feel Reference

- **Adopt: the fixed-point telegraph of boss-style area attacks (Souls-like ground strikes, platformer stomps).** A long, readable wind-up whose *area is locked at its start*, so that moving out always works and a hit is the player's own stillness. Borrowed quality: **committed, heavy, fair.** The horror's snap is the only fast thing in its vocabulary, and it lands on a place the player was shown for one to three seconds (HE-14, HE-F5).
- **Adopt: *Alien: Isolation*'s one persistent, physically present body** (a true position, never despawned) **and *Darkwood*'s light that attracts and marks that carry the past** (HE-13, HE-30). Borrowed quality: weather with intent, not a monster with a personality.
- **Adopt in spirit: *Project Zomboid*'s "death is final and common"** (the user's decision), bounded to three told strikes; and *Amnesia*'s "you cannot fight it, only hide" (no attack input).
- **Anti-reference (reject precisely):** *Alien: Isolation*'s director-driven menace gauge and grab-instakill (a hidden number and an unreadable death); any action-game hit-stop, screen shake, damage number or red flash (HE-38, the camera refuses horror callers); a stinger that scales with distance (a meter by ear, Pillar 3). **It must not feel** like a jump-scare machine, a boss with a health bar, or a creature that "locks on" and follows you (validation target 4).

### Input Responsiveness

The only inputs in the chain belong to the player (walk, lamp, door, pod) and to the sim (the horror's run). The budget is the **walk-out window** of HE-F5: from the limb entering the cone to the seat standing on the disc boundary, the player gets `UNFOLD_LO` 95 ticks, of which 54 are the assumed reaction allowance, 1 the kernel's command application and 36 the walk (`ceil(R_SNAP_PX / V_WALK)`), leaving **4 ticks (67 ms) of slack** at the shortest draw.

| Action or cause | Max latency to visible or audible response | Frame budget (60 fps) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Attention's phase edge on a non-run tick to Horror acting on it | 4 ticks (67 ms) | 4 | Horror reads `attention.view` as committed by Attention's last run and acts at its next run (HE-4, E-HE-17); it only ever *lengthens* the warning |
| Horror state change (REACH begin, STRIKE, RETREAT) to the rig pose on screen | 1 tick (17 ms) after the run that made it | 1 | The pose is a function of `(state, state_enter_T, now)`; the run that commits `locked_pt` and `reach_end_T` is the run whose pose the presenter draws |
| Player's walk command to the seat moving | 1 tick (17 ms) | 1 | Kernel command application at `apply_tick >= T + 1` (KRN D.4); counted inside the 95-tick budget |
| Player switches the lamp off to the horror's *next-run* target changing | 5 ticks (83 ms) | 5 | Target recomputed each run (HE-13); an **in-flight REACH is unaffected** (P is locked, E-HE-23) |
| `strike_T` to the positional strike cue starting | 1 tick (17 ms) | 1 | A requirement on Adaptive Audio (Designed, `adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`, AA-28 c); the cue is the witness of strike one (HE-15) |
| Strike to Suit's rung change and the Controller's stagger | at the next `suit.vitals` run, at most 6 ticks (100 ms) after the hit, with no hold (SE-23, `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` 1); the Controller's stagger follows on the cap falling edge it reads after `suit.vitals` (PC-9) | <= 6 | Suit's and the Controller's; stagger lasts `STAGGER_TICKS` 30 (500 ms) |
| Closing a door leaf to the strike no longer reaching through it | the same tick | 0 | The room test and the closed-leaf test are evaluated at resolution (HE-19) |

### Animation Feel Targets

"Startup" is the telegraph before the act has any effect; "Active" is the committed window in which the act is fixed and nothing the player does changes where it lands; "Recovery" is the horror's committed, vulnerable-in-the-world time (it is never vulnerable to the player; recovery is the player's **breathing room**). One pose = 5 ticks. All counts follow `UNFOLD_TICKS` in [95, 180] (19 to 36 poses).

| Animation | Startup (ticks, poses) | Active (ticks, poses) | Recovery (ticks, poses) | Feel goal | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **REACH** (enter + unfold + hold) | enter ~15 %: 15 to 27 (3 to 5); unfold ~55 %: 50 to 100 (10 to 20) | hold ~30 %, never under 30 ticks: 30 to 54 (6 to 11); **the tip does not move** | resolves into the snap | Committed, deliberate, readable: a heavy thing placing a limb | The limb enters the cone before the body; no icon, ring or decal; `reach_a/b/c` poses (V3, V4) |
| **STRIKE** (snap) | 0 (no wind-up beyond the unfold) | 1 pose = 5 ticks (83 ms), then held for `STRIKE_HOLD_RUNS` 2 = 10 ticks (167 ms) | RETREAT begins at the end of the hold | Crisp and binary: the only fast motion Horror owns | No lunge travel, afterimage or trail (HE-24); the touch-down smear and the positional cue are the punctuation |
| **REACH fizzle** | n/a | the reverse of the unfold poses over the same stage lengths | none; the cooldown is not consumed | Identical to a deliberate withdrawal, so a harmless hold cannot be told from a harmful one | The hold itself is always honest (V5) |
| **RETREAT** | 0 | creep at `V_CREEP_MPX` 1000: 5 px per run; 96 ticks out to a nominal waypoint at 192 px | `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` 300 (5.0 s) before STALK may resume; 65 ticks back at `V_HUNT_MPX` | A retreat reads as a retreat, not a hover (`t_out` 96 is inside the cooldown, HE-F15) | The realised period of a seat that does not move is 460 to 545 ticks (7.7 to 9.1 s); the earliest next strike is 395 ticks (6.6 s) after the last |
| **Creep** (LURK, STALK in a TELL) | n/a | 1 px per tick, a pose per run, hash-irregular limb choice, no repeated configuration in consecutive steps | n/a | Patient, wet, structural; never a metronome (not the tell's beat), never a jitter | Dwell 900 ticks (15 s) at a LURK waypoint |
| **Hunt** (STALK in BREACH) | n/a | 1.5 px per tick on average (7 then 8 px per run); a walker gains 0.5 px per tick on it | n/a | Inevitable but outwalkable: pressure, not a chase | `V_HUNT_MPX` below `V_WALK × 1000` is the escape invariant (HE-F6) |
| **Ingress** | the ingress mark is laid 60 ticks (1.0 s) before the body | the rig emerges across the wall footprint's south edge; alpha is 0 or 255 | n/a | A body crossing a light boundary, never a fade | At least 300 ticks (5.0 s) of published stalk remain before the shortest tell ends (HE-F9) |
| **Mark lay-down** | 3 frames (smear, rime) or 2 (hairlines), 5 ticks each | hold on the last lay-down frame (stage 0) | aged frames at stage 1 from age 6000 ticks and stage 2 from age 12000 ticks (thirds of `MARK_LIFE_TICKS` 18000) | A floor that remembers, readable as wet, tacky, dry | Frame swaps on a binary-alpha sprite; no fade |

### Impact Moments

Every row is **0 by design** (HE-38, the anti-pillar "not a combat game"; the camera refuses a Horror-tagged caller). The punctuation the strike *does* carry is listed after the table. "Configurable?" is **No** for every row: these are lint failures, not tuning options.

| Impact type | Duration | Effect | Configurable? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hit-stop (freeze frames) | 0 ms | none | No (HE-38; AC-HE-72, AC-HE-116) |
| Screen shake | 0 ms | none | No (PIPE C1, C9; the jolt API refuses a Horror-tagged caller) |
| Camera impact or zoom punch | 0 ms | none | No (the camera never reacts to a horror) |
| Hit flash, vignette, chromatic aberration | 0 ms | none | No (Visual/Audio: forbidden feedback) |
| Damage number, health pip, threat bar | n/a | none | No (HE-38) |
| Controller rumble | none from Horror | Neither the Player Controller & Input GDD nor Suit & Equipment defines a rumble rule; if one is added for the stagger or the CRITICAL rung it is theirs (HUD & Suit Layer: designed (`hud-and-suit-layer.md`)) | No (not Horror's) |
| Time-scale slowdown | 0 ms | none | No |

**The punctuation the strike does carry:** the single **snap pose** (5 ticks, then a 10-tick hold), the **touch-down smear** laid at the point 95 to 180 ticks earlier and still on the floor, **Audio's positional cue** starting within 1 tick of `strike_T`, the **STRUCK burst** laid at P only when a seat was hit (a miss leaves the smear alone), and **Suit's own consequence** (the rung, the stagger, from strike two the seam jet). Strike one has no persistent witness on the body (Suit's STRAINED rung is inert by design, SE:83); the strike and the STRUCK mark on the floor are the witness, which is why those things exist (HE-15, C-HE-25).

### Weight and Responsiveness Profile

- **Weight.** Heavy and deliberate. The horror is slower than the walker (1.0 to 1.5 px per tick against 2), roots its body for the whole telegraph and resolves in one step. It never hurries, never poses and never roars.
- **Player control.** High at every moment until the commit. The strike point is locked, so *moving out of the disc always works*; the player can course-correct for the whole telegraph and, because there is no tracking, cannot be "chased" into a hit. Control ends only for a carrier (`V_CARRY` 1 needs 72 ticks against 40 available; the answer is to drop the cargo) and for a seat that stands still.
- **Snap quality.** The creep and unfold are stepped at 12 fps with smoothed position (analogue in motion, discrete in pose); the strike is a **binary one-step snap**. The contrast between the long stillness of the hold and the single step of the snap is the whole read.
- **Acceleration model.** Simulation feel, no ramp: speed is a constant cadence (`v_eff` per tick from the accumulator, HE-F6), and a speed change at a phase edge is instantaneous in the sim and invisible as acceleration on screen (position is interpolated, pose is stepped). There is no lunge momentum and no overshoot.
- **Failure texture.** Fair, never random: the player was shown the limb, the stillness and the smear, and a hit reads as "I stood there" or "I was carrying". The target is that **at least 80 % of horror-caused deaths are described with a cause the player saw** (validation target 1). There is nothing hidden: no stored aggro, no lock-on, no number. Dying is lethal (user decision), restores from the newest autosave, and shows no death screen or beat (SL-7).

### Feel Acceptance Criteria

Subjective targets stated precisely enough to get consistent verdicts; the manual rows that measure them are the numbered criteria in Acceptance Criteria Group M, and the sample rule (N >= 10) applies.

- [ ] Playtesters describe the telegraph as "it was aiming at that spot" or equivalent for at least 87.5 % of first-time players (7 of 8 equivalent, N >= 10; AC-HE-106); none uses "cheap", "unfair" or "came out of nowhere" unprompted for a *told* strike.
- [ ] Of the players who were hit, at least 75 % call it avoidable (AC-HE-106); at least 80 % of horror-caused deaths are described with a cause the player saw (AC-HE-116).
- [ ] The unfold-hold-snap sequence reads at k = 1, `k_auto` and `k_auto + 2` as "limb extended, tip on a tile" (at k = 1 only "limb extended") (AC-HE-107).
- [ ] The creep reads as patient and irregular: no tester says "glitchy", "teleporting", "jittery" or "puppet" (AC-HE-108, AC-HE-117); pose changes land only on multiples of 5 ticks.
- [ ] No tester reports a hit-stop, shake, flash or number from the horror (AC-HE-116), and none reports an unfair pop-in on ingress (AC-HE-112).
- [ ] A player who reacts within the 54-tick allowance escapes at every legal draw of `UNFOLD_TICKS` (AC-HE-88); the 4-tick slack is felt as "just made it" at the shortest draw, never as "it hit me where I was not".
- [ ] Zero testers describe the horror "remembering" or "locking on" across a lamp-off (validation target 4, AC-HE-115).
- [ ] The breathing gap between a snap and the next limb (at least 365 ticks, 6.1 s, for a seat that stays in range) is described as a lull to act in, not as a long pause or a screech (AC-HE-115).
- [ ] Reduced motion keeps the telegraph readable (AC-HE-113).

## UI Requirements

**Horror Entities has no UI of its own.** It owns no HUD element, no instrument, no chart-table row, no menu and no overlay, and it must not acquire one: Pillar 3 says the web's attention and its consequences are "shown in the world, never a meter", and the anti-pillar against a combat game rules out every standard enemy-awareness widget. Everything the player learns about a horror comes through the world: the rig in the beam, the marks on the floor, the dips on nearby lamps, Audio's positional facts, and Suit's and Failure & Trail's consequences. The surfaces that exist in the game and touch this system are owned elsewhere; this section states what each must **not** do and what Horror supplies. A UX spec is therefore not requested for Horror; the UX note at the end is a *negative* requirement routed to the HUD owner.

| Information | Display location | Update frequency | Condition | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Where a horror is, what state it is in, which seat it prefers, where its limb will land | **Nowhere in the HUD.** The player reads the rig, the limb tip and the touch-down smear in the world | n/a | never | Horror (world only) |
| That a hit landed | The snap pose, Audio's positional cue and, afterwards, the STRUCK burst on the floor at P (a miss leaves only the touch-down smear) (HE-15); then Suit's own rung display and stagger | on the strike | a seat in the disc | Suit & Equipment, HUD & Suit Layer |
| Suit integrity after strikes | Suit's existing instrument or HUD row (STRAINED is inert and carries no witness by design, SE:83; CRITICAL shows the leak, seam jet and stagger) | Suit's | Suit's | Suit & Equipment, HUD & Suit Layer |
| The ship's hull damage from a GOUGE or RUPTURE | Failure & Trail's hull descriptor and the Graph's own instruments | the Graph's | a hull act | Failure & Trail, In-World Instruments |
| That the lamp is on or off (the horror's target rule) | Controller and Suit's lamp state; the lit-only verb gating | immediate | always | Player Controller, Interaction |
| Where it has been | the marks themselves (smear, rime, hairlines), never a map pin, trail overlay or "last seen" marker on the Chart Table or Web Map | n/a | never | Horror (world only); Chart Table, Web Map must not add one |
| True horror positions, target, state, locked point, budgets and counters | **Debug Overlay (True Values), development builds only** | per run | developer flag | Debug Overlay (`horror.view` is read-only); never exposed as an accessibility feature |
| Save status | Save/Load's pause-panel and failed-write notice, unrelated to Horror | Save/Load's | Save/Load's | Save/Load |

**Rules for the surfaces that exist (negative requirements, each an acceptance criterion).**
- No HUD, instrument, chart-table or web-map element shows a horror, a threat bar, a proximity indicator, a direction, an off-screen arrow, a countdown to a strike, a "last seen" pin or a target ring, **under any accessibility setting** (Pillar 3, AT-24; AC-HE-119).
- No audio-to-visual accessibility feature may name, point to or time a horror (a captioned cue such as "something nearby" is a proximity indicator by another name). The accessibility mapping for Horror's audio facts is an Open Question for the accessibility specialist and the HUD owner, with the constraint that it never reveals position, state or direction.
- No cursor, reticle or interaction prompt appears on a horror: HORROR subjects take no verb and any forced command is `INVALID_TARGET` (IA-39, AC-IA-113).
- The Debug Overlay's true-value drawing is a development tool; a shipping build cannot enable it.
- Accessibility options that change Horror's presentation (reduced motion, reduced flashing, ambient-floor reveal) act on the world drawing described in Visual/Audio Requirements and never add a surface (the ambient-floor option reveals hull only, never a horror or a mark).

**UX note (deliberately not a UX flag).** This system has **no UI of its own**, so no `/ux-design` pass is requested for it. The HUD & Suit Layer (designed (`hud-and-suit-layer.md`)), In-World Instruments, Chart Table and Debug Overlay (designed (`debug-overlay-true-values.md`)) UX specs must repeat the negative requirements above and list "no horror element" in their own acceptance criteria; the HUD state list in AC-HE-119 is finalised from the HUD UX spec. `(provisional — In-World Instruments and Chart Table UX specs)`.

"Owed rows" are wording or value refreshes that other documents must make; this GDD **never edits** another system's GDD, the index, the art bible or the registry's existing entries.

#### Stale-token scan list (run before closing; each must be zero outside a quotation or a negation)

`real occlusion`, `cast shadow(s)`, `occlude the beam`; `horror.tell` as Attention's stream; "Horrors push noise"; `H <= 16` as Horror's budget; "per run per subject" as Horror's unit; `flicker` near a lamp, fixture or lantern; `meter`, `bar`, `gauge`, `indicator` near a horror or proximity; `threat`, `aggro`, `alert`, `awareness`, `suspicion`; `kill`, `damage number`, `hit flash`, `XP`; `GOUGE` on a non-hull subject; "hand point" as a beam origin.

## Acceptance Criteria

Every criterion is GIVEN / WHEN / THEN and carries a tag: **(auto)** an automated test (gdUnit4, `tests/unit/horror_entities/horror_entities_<feature>_test.gd`, or `tests/integration/horror_entities/` for the integration rows), **(lint)** a static or data scan, **(review)** a document or design review, **(manual)** a playtest or hands-on session with retained evidence, **(spike)** a prototype measurement whose result sets a placeholder. "Fixture" means a deterministic scripted scenario with a fixed `world_seed`. A criterion that depends on an undesigned or in-flight neighbour is marked *(provisional — <dep>)* and reads **NOT ASSESSED** until that GDD exists, never PASS.

**Gate levels (coding standards).** Logic and Integration rows are BLOCKING. Every Visual/Feel and UI row (Group M) is BLOCKING and requires a **retained screenshot** under `production/qa/evidence/horror-entities/<ac-id>/` (a parse check is not a run), captured at **k = 1, `k_auto` and `k_auto + 2`** unless the row says otherwise, with a lead sign-off line (art-director for visuals, game-designer for feel). Data lints are BLOCKING in CI. A manual row with fewer testers than its stated N reads NOT ASSESSED, never PASS.

**Negative-fixture convention.** A criterion marked *(negative fixture, expected FAIL)* runs under an expected-failure harness: it passes the suite **only when the data load or boot check REJECTS the value**. Positive and negative cases inside one row are asserted separately.

**Smoke subset (under 60 s in CI; every Horror change keeps it green; the rest run nightly):** AC-HE-1, 2, 3, 10, 12 (the clean-ship breach), 27, 55, 76 (block conformance), 80 (determinism) and 81 (save-equivalence). Every bug filed against a Horror criterion needs a regression test that was watched failing first (test standards).

**Story types for the QA plan.** Groups T, B, P, F, L, I, S, Q and Z are Logic (BLOCKING). Rows tagged "integration" (AC-HE-28, 93, 94, 95) are Integration (BLOCKING). Group M is Visual/Feel and UI (BLOCKING, retained screenshot and lead sign-off). Review and lint rows are documentation gates.
